# Agent instructions — SurvivalGame2026

Read this at the start of work on **player pawn** gameplay or when touching movement, pools, or combat.

## Commandment #1 — Player feature umbrellas

All player-facing behavior in these domains is owned and **tuned through** these components. Prefer adding properties, hooks, and call paths **here** so designers and agents know where to look.

| Domain | Owner component | Scope |
|--------|-----------------|--------|
| **Movement** | `Code/Player/PlayerMovement.cs` | Anything that changes how the player **moves** (locomotion, modes, motion rules for the pawn). |
| **Vitals** | `Code/Player/PlayerVitals.cs` | **Loss/gain** of **health, stamina, oxygen** (and similar pools). Host bookkeeping may use `VitalsAuthority`; per-pawn tuning and the main “what the pawn has” contract stay on `PlayerVitals`. |
| **Combat** | `Code/Player/PlayerCombat.cs` | **Attacks and blocks** — input, phases, weapon tuning, client vs server flow. Host validation may use `CombatAuthority`; **serialized / designer-facing combat parameters for this pawn** stay on `PlayerCombat` unless there is a strong reason otherwise. |
| **Animation** | `Code/Player/PlayerAnimation.cs` | Citizen **hold poses**, the one-handed sword swing (`PlayerAnimation.MeleeSwing.cs`: rest pose on the shoulder of the side being aimed from, press draws the matching wind-up through the windup, the **strike clip** fires when the host active window opens — all graph layers on the project's sword build of the human model, see "Sword animation assets" below), demo held props. Combat/equipment request intents; this component applies animgraph + presentation. A melee profile with `heldModel` in `equipment_profiles.json` shows that model at its own .vmdl scale instead of the box sword: `Code/Player/HeldModelFit.cs` reads the mesh once (longest axis = handle, wider end = head, edge = side of the head furthest from the handle line) and `TickMeleeDemoStickTransform` puts the head at the tip, the butt in the rear hand along the handle axis and the edge toward the body's forward — no per-item angles (`EnsureMeleeDemoStick` rebuilds on a main-hand change; `axe_stone` → `models/weapons/axe_reforged.vmdl`, `club_wood` → `models/weapons/sword_reforged.vmdl`). Models imported by `import_artist_pack.py` carry their origin at the butt, which `HeldModelFit` honours before the cross-section guess (see "Artist model import"). Runtime presentation props (the `melee_demo_stick` box-sword) must be `NetworkMode.Never` — a plain child of a networked pawn is `Snapshot` by default and replicates to joining clients, which showed **two swords** on the host player. |
| **Augments** | `Code/Player/PlayerAugments.cs` | Crafted body augments: 6 body parts × 3 sockets + bank. **Enhance** (augment cores, `resource_augmentCore`, free personal craft for now) opens sockets one at a time per part — head/torso 3 cores for the first, arms/legs 2, hands/feet 1, then 1 per extra socket (`AugmentBodyParts`). Placing an augment on the paper doll is free and only *pending*; **Augment** (gold coins, `resource_goldCoins`) commits every pending socket: part base 50 / 75 / 100 gold × the socket's position in its part (first socket 1×, second 2×, third 3×) — augments carry no tier of their own. A socket is *active* only while its placed augment matches the committed id; pulling one out stops the effect at once and costs nothing. Definitions list `slots` (array of allowed sockets) in `Assets/data/augment_definitions.json`. **Activation** (`PlayerAugments.Triggers.cs`): `activation` passive / trigger / wheel and `mode` oneshot / toggle per definition, with `cooldownSeconds`, `batterySeconds` (toggle drain) and `effectSeconds/Scale/RadiusMeters/Chance` tunables; `implemented: false` = data-only stub (info block says so). Trigger augments are dragged from the doll into the six key-bind slots on the Augments page (binding-only drags, `PlayerAugmentBindGridHost`) and fire on **Z X C V G T** (`Augment1`-`Augment6` actions, `PlayerAugments.BindKeyLabels`; Mark 2026-11-02 - the number row 1-0 selects hotbar slots again, see "Hotbar banks"). Wheel augments: **hold Tab** past `PlayerAugments.WheelHoldSeconds` (0.22 s; `AugmentWheel` action on tab - a TAP of Tab still toggles the inventory menu, `PlayerGameMenuController.WasInventoryTapReleased`) opens the radial (`AugmentWheelHud`, look frozen), mouse direction picks, release fires. F is the shove and nothing else. Live checks go through `IsAbilityOn` (passives while paid, toggles while on). Host effects (Sonic Burst pin via `EntityLocomotion.HostSetTrapped`, War Cry via `EntityBrain.HostProvoke`) re-validate socket + cooldown on the host; once-per-window passives (Death Grip, Armor Plating) use `HostTryConsumePassiveCooldown`. Implemented now: Spring Legs, Double Jump, Sneaky Feet, Recovery Slide, Grapple Drive, Death Grip, Heat Breaker, Armor Plating, Parry Recharge (`PlayerVitals.HostBeginStaminaRegenBoost`), Sonic Burst, War Cry, Medusa Eye (`PlayerAugments.MedusaEye.cs`: owner polls at 10 Hz for the nearest `EntityVitals.IsMachineOrAscended` entity in the middle third of the screen with LOS — the fox's stare test — and after `effectSeconds` asks the host, which re-checks kind + range, spends the 60 s cooldown and pins it via `EntityLocomotion.HostSetTrapped` for `effectScale` s), Laser Eyes (`PlayerAugments.LaserEyes.cs`: owner aims and syncs `LaserActive` / `LaserEndWorld`, host re-traces eye → end every 0.2 s and applies `effectScale` per tick for `effectSeconds`, every peer draws two DebugOverlay beams from the `eyes` attachment), Deadshot (`PlayerAugments.Deadshot.cs` owner tags — red sphere child of the hit object, one per victim, `effectScale` max, `effectRadiusMeters` reach, needs PrimaryRanged out; `PlayerCombat.Deadshot.cs` host volley — direct `TakeDamage` at each tag + stuck arrow, one ammo + one wear per shot, range re-checked), Thermal Eye (`Code/Augments/ThermalVision.cs`: `ThermalVisionPostProcess` after-opaque blue remap via `shaders/thermal_view.shader` + translucent `shaders/thermal_body.shader` material override on every PlayerVitals / EntityVitals / AnimalBrain renderer, bind-pose height + radius heat map; `thermalView true` hack forces it); the other 30 table entries are stubs. Entities carry an `EntityKind` (Feral / Robot / Ascended) from `EntityArchetype`; `EnemyType.PatrolBot` is a scav-stat robot (console `spawnPatrolBot`, perception row `patrolbotT1`). HUD: `AugmentBindsHud` strip above the hotbar (cooldown sweep, battery bar, toggle edge). Station UI: `Code/UI/Menu/AugmentStationMenuSection.cs`; the paper doll itself is `AugmentPaperdollView` (+ `AugmentPlayerPreviewPanel`, a ScenePanel citizen) and is reused read-only by the **Augments** tab page (`AugmentsMenuSection`, core-icon tab, `MenuPanelFlags.Augments`) — same doll, no Enhance / Augment / bank, sockets on a read-only grid host; shared info block `Code/Augments/AugmentInfo.cs` feeds both the station box and the item tooltip. (The dodge roll — grounded Block/Crouch (+A/D) + Space, camera-relative, `PlayerMovement.DodgeRoll.cs` — is core movement, not an augment.) |

Other scripts and whole subtrees exist (environment, AI, networking managers, etc.). For **this player prefab’s** movement, vitals, combat, and animation, **go through these umbrellas** for edits and for understanding what calls what.

## Primary melee — attack action (not collision damage)

Sword **colliders must not** be the source of HP damage (use them for VFX / clash / debug only). Real damage comes only from a **server-authoritative** phased attack on **`PlayerCombat`**:

**Directional exchange (Mark 2026-11-14: "trash the combat we have now and replace it with" the Last Oasis-inspired spec v0.1).** Every swing is **windup → release → recovery**, all from the weapon class row (`melee_weapon_classes.json` + `combat_timings.csv`; testscene1's `MeleeWeaponTimingLibrary` rows win there). **Square timing set, 2026-11-17** (Mark: no "weird values ... everything should be nice and square so we can add different equipment later on"; Last Oasis publishes no numbers; after a ×1.25 pass looked like "slow motion" he took the proposed table). One-handed (`melee_weapon_classes.json`, `combat_timings.csv`, the `MeleeWeaponClassCatalog` fallbacks and testscene1's library rows): windup 0.6, chained 0.5, release 0.2, recovery 0.5, combo end 0.75, blocked recovery **0.2** (not the table's 0.3: Mark wants ".3-.4s to react" to a counter - free at 0.2, the counter releases at 0.1 + 0.5 and lands ~0.65, so ~0.3 s from free and ~0.4 s from the red arrow to press, before the 0.15 s guard rise), heavy windup 0.9 / release 0.25 / recovery 0.75 / blocked 0.4, staggers hit 0.4 / heavy 0.75 / blocked heavy 0.6, counter windup 0.5. Every other class keeps its original ratio to one-handed per field, rounded to 0.05 (dagger's counter leaves only ~0.15 s - a fast weapon). Exchange windows: counter window 0.5, light block reaction 0.1, chamber ±0.075, chain / input buffer / feint recovery 0.15, heavy drain after 1.0, hard stagger protection 0.5, morph settle 0.15 / re-arm 0.5, block rise / switch 0.15, block min hold 0.1; punch windup 0.4, lock 0.2, victim stagger 0.9, blocked 0.5, cooldown 2; `VitalsAuthority` regen delay after attack / block 0.75 / 0.4. basicplayer, scavT1 and bossT1 carry the exchange windows explicitly in their prefabs (a changed code default the prefab does not store stays stale on clones until an editor restart - the live pawn read the old light block reaction). The scav styles keep their ×1.25 seconds from the pass before (slower AI reactions than the 2026-11-15 tuning). The wind-up draw eases out (`PlayerAnimation.WindDrawCurve`, players and scavs): quick out of the carry, slowing into the cocked frame. One-handed: `windupSeconds` 0.6 (0.55 before; **slowed 2026-11-15** from 0.45 - Mark: "if i barely hesitate after the red arrow pops up then i take a hit 100% of the time"), `activeSeconds` (the release, the only damaging phase) 0.18, `recoverySeconds` 0.45 - **retimed 2026-11-14 from Mark's Last Oasis footage** (2026-11-05 clip at 33 ms a frame: wind-up ~0.5 s, strike ~0.1 s, follow-through ~0.45 s, chained strike to strike ~1.07 s; the spec v0.1 numbers 0.2 / 0.18 / 0.25 with a recovery-skipping chain ran 0.33 s, Mark: "TOO fast"). The damage release stays 0.18 (`create_sword_anims.py` `PATH_ACTIVE_S` solves the strike path from it); since 2026-11-16 the clips play that path over 0.30 s (`STRIKE_SWOOP_S`). Other classes are the spec guesses scaled the same way (windup x2.25, combo windup 0.89 x windup, recovery x1.8, combo end x1.5, heavy windup x2, staggers / counter windup x2); the old `scavT1` class row is gone (scavs swing `oneHanded`, the club they hold). Measured live: 0.455 / 0.177 / 0.456 s, spammed clicks 1.087 s strike to strike. **Three directions** (left / right / overhead; the spear's up is its thrust; the old down-jab, the parry, the static 3-hit combo and the Q special are gone). The non-weapon rules live on `PlayerCombat` (`PlayerCombat.Exchange.cs`, inspector group *Combat — Exchange*): **chaining** — a click from the last `ChainInputWindowSeconds` (0.15) of a release through its recovery chains: the next swing winds up the moment the recovery (the follow-through) ends (Last Oasis: every chained swing plays its follow-through), in `comboWindupSeconds` (0.41, 25 % shorter than the 0.55 windup, any direction - Mark 2026-11-14: "Attack 2 and 3 in a combo have faster windup by 25%"; only the windup is shorter, the recovery is not), at most `ComboMaxHits` (3), the last one recovering `comboEndRecoverySeconds` (0.6); the host takes the chain in `HostTryTakeChain` at the recovery end (a chained press up to `HostLatencySlackSeconds` late still chains); any other click waits `AttackInputBufferSeconds` (0.15, one stored press, newest wins). **Heavy** — still held when the light windup ends (and `staminaHeavyCost` 20 payable): strikes on release, never before `heavyWindupSeconds` 0.7 and **never by itself** (2026-11-16, Mark: "it should never just get released at some arbitrary point" - `HeavyChargeMaxSeconds` 1.4 is gone; remote peers keep the cocked pose up through the synced `HeavyHoldShown`), draining `HeavyHoldDrainPerSecond` 5/s after `HeavyHoldDrainAfterSeconds` 0.8 (an empty bar lets it go); release 0.22, recovery 0.6, ×`heavyDamageMultiplier` 1.5. **Feint** — the guard button in the first `FeintCutoffFraction` (75 %) of a windup cancels it for `FeintStaminaCost` 5 and `FeintRecoverySeconds` 0.15 of attack lock, and so does the guard at **any point before the swing turns heavy while the attack button is still held** (2026-11-16, Mark: holding a light that has yet to go heavy, block ditches the attack and goes into block - `ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.CanFeint(held)`); a held guard button then raises the guard; later in a tapped windup, in the release and the recovery, the guard waits for the swing to end. A feinting owner also sends its button release, so a feint the host refused strikes instead of hanging as a heavy. **Heavy morph** (2026-11-16, `PlayerCombat.HeavyMorph.cs`, Mark: "right attack being held, right click, mouse move to overhead, release, player does overhead strike") — the guard button on a held heavy is never a feint or a guard: it starts the morph; **one pick per press** (2026-11-17, Mark: "you have 1 chance to select the new direction and once its selected thats where it stays. If they want to morph again they have to repeat the process"): the first side the attack cursor moves to is the new side and the morph ends there (`TickOwnerHeavyMorph` clears `_ownerMorphing`); another guard press on the still-held heavy morphs again. Owner-side only - the host accepts each side change it is sent (`HostTryHeavyMorph`). The cocked weapon moves to that side (`PlayerAnimation.MorphCockedSwing`, through the swap pose when it crosses the face), the host moves the swing's side (`HostTryHeavyMorph` → `ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.MorphTo`: damage arc, the side a guard must take, the strike clip; other peers via `RpcBroadcastHeavyMorph`) and the release strikes from there, never sooner than `HeavyMorphSettleSeconds` 0.15 after the last side change. No extra stamina (the hold drain keeps running). **Re-arm** (2026-11-17, Mark: "it has like a half second after you pick the new direction before itll be a heavy hit. It should do the sparkle animation thing just sooner than if you hadnt morphed"): every side change disarms the heavy for `HeavyMorphRearmSeconds` (0.5); then the heavy-ready glint plays again (owner `OwnerHeavyArmedAt`, peers `ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.HeavyArmedElapsed`) and the release is a heavy. Let go sooner and it strikes from the new side **as a light** (Mark chose this over waiting: light damage, stagger, release and recovery; the heavy stamina already paid stays spent). A scav guarding a morphed heavy re-reads the side after a fresh reaction (`EntityCombat` `_threatType`) and may misread it again. Mouse moves on a held heavy without the guard press do nothing. **Hits** stagger the victim (`hitStaggerSeconds` 0.3, heavy `heavyHitStaggerSeconds` 0.6) and **interrupt its swing** (`HostTryStagger`; a punch never does); hard staggers do not repeat inside `HardStaggerProtectionSeconds` 0.5. **Turning** — the camera is free; the swing basis turns with the body (`GetSwingTurnDegrees` = the body's unwrapped turn since the swing committed), so it follows the look at the body turn cap in every phase (the per-phase 540 / 360 / 540°/s rates are deleted). **Turn damage** (2026-11-17, Mark: "if you rotate while hitting the sword is going faster ... Maybe 1.25 is the max buff? and .75 is the max debuff? ... if we rotate a little we get 1.10"): `PlayerCombat.TurnDamageScale` scales a lateral swing's damage by the body's average turn speed over the release so far as a share of the turn cap: turning the way the blade travels (a right swing travels right → left, i.e. turning left; a left swing turning right) up to `TurnDamageMaxScale` 1.25, against it down to `TurnDamageMinScale` 0.75, linear (40 % of the cap with the blade = 1.10); an overhead travels down, not sideways — no change; scavs (no body tracker) never scale. Host only, measured in the hit tick (`TurnScaleSoFar`), so the cap is also the anti-cheat bound. **Owner prediction / host authority**: the owner runs the same timeline (`OwnerSwingPhaseNow`, chain / buffer / feint decisions, footwork, guard gate) for instant feedback and sends each swing as it fires (`ComboStep` 2+ = fired as a chain); the host (`HostSubmitPlayerSwing`) picks the windup itself (class / combo / counter — client press times never shorten it), pays the stamina when the swing really starts, keeps a click it cannot honour yet as the one pending press (`HostLatencySlackSeconds` 0.15 for the round trip) and rejects / drops what it cannot run — the owner then lets go of the predicted swing. Remote peers replay with the host's windup (`AttackReleaseIntent.HostWindupSeconds`); a cut swing is broadcast (`RpcBroadcastSwingCancelled`). No lag compensation yet (the host sweeps against current positions).

1. **Attack selection** — directional cursor locks on press (`SouthpawSwing` inverts L/R attack **type** only). Three attacks: left, right, up = overhead. **No stab / jab / thrust** (removed 2026-11-14, Mark: Last Oasis "doesnt have a stab either"): `SwingDirs.Down`, `MeleeAttackTypes.Stab`, the class `forwardAttackIsThrust` flag, the stab path / overlay, `Sword_Cocked_Stab` / `Sword_Attack_Stab` and the `*_STAB` poses are gone; a downward mouse flick falls to the nearer side (`ClassifyLiveSwingFrame`). The spear's up is an overhead like every other weapon. **Attack teardrop (HUD):** screen-left teardrop = **right** swing / right-side attack path; screen-right = **left** attack. **Block teardrop:** screen-left = left hold (same side as guard). Mouse evidence for attacks uses mirrored L/R cardinals vs block.
2. **Outcomes** — a landed hit keeps sweeping (no more hit checks) to the end of the release, then recovers or chains. A **blocked or chambered** swing ends on contact and locks the attacker out (no attack, no guard) for the class `recoveryBlockedSeconds` (0.2 one-handed since 2026-11-17, was 0.45); a blocked heavy only `recoveryBlockedHeavySeconds` (0.3) so it keeps the initiative over the blocker's `heavyBlockStaggerSeconds` (0.5) — `PlayerCombat.ApplyAttackerRecoveryFromFinish`. **Priority**: blocking a light gives the blocker `LightBlockReactionSeconds` (0.1) of attack lock, then `CounterWindowSeconds` (0.5) in which its first swing winds up `counterWindupSeconds` (0.5 one-handed since 2026-11-17, was 0.3). **Initiative can be traded back** (2026-11-17, Mark: a scav's counter after its block always landed - "from the moment the player knows the attack is coming to when it can damage me is just simply too fast"; with 0.45 / 0.3 the attacker came free exactly as the counter landed): every class's `recoveryBlockedSeconds` ×0.55 and `counterWindupSeconds` ×1.5, so the blocked attacker is free ~0.35 s before the counter lands - enough for a reaction and the 0.1 s guard rise, and the counter is still faster than a fresh swing. combat_test "a blocked attacker can guard the blocker's counter" (guard raised a 0.15 s reaction after the lock ends) guards it. **A held guard comes up on time too** (2026-11-17, Mark: "Scav uses its initiative, i hold block and it goes right through"): the owner predicts its own swing (release + class recovery) and `CanStartBlock` keeps the guard down while that runs; a swing the host stopped on a block / chamber now ends that prediction (`PlayerCombat.OwnerOnSwingStoppedOnContact`, `RpcOwnerSwingStoppedOnContact`) - before, the held guard rose ~0.7 s after the block, after the counter had landed. combat_test "a blocked swing ends the owner's predicted swing". **Damage** = `round(MeleeWeaponBaseDamage × MeleeBaseCombatDamageMultiplier)` (× heavy).
3. **Geometry** — `MeleeAttackPath` in combat-local space (+X forward, +Y up, +Z right). L/R: horizontal arc; overhead: vertical XY arc. Reach comes from class **meters** (`reachForwardMeters` / `reachLateralMeters`) converted once per pawn via `PlayerCombat.GetMeleeAttackRangeUnits` (BodyHeight/1.8). Player classes were cut 10 % twice on 2026-11-05 (Mark: "too far out"; one-handed 1.9 → 1.54 m, two-handed 1.78, spear 2.11 / 1.94, dagger 1.05 / 0.97). The scav (`scavT1`, root scale 0.85) reaches 85 % of the player's total: class 1.28 m, so its arc tip lands 1.66 m from the body against the player's 1.96 m (tip distance = reach × 1.14 + the 0.2 m forward push; the class reach does not scale with the pawn's root scale). The training dummy class stays 1.71. **Per-weapon reach = the held blade** (Mark 2026-11-23: "the club's arc is far too large, make it the same length as the weapon"; then "the arc doesnt show where the sword blade is" - the first fit put it at ~80 % of the blade): a profile's `meleeOverrides` (`reachForwardMeters` / `reachLateralMeters` in `equipment_profiles.json`) replaces the class reach. The fit is measured, not guessed: `create_sword_anims.py -- path` prints where the baked strike clips carry the 1.0 m sword's tip (1.08-1.50 m from the swing pivot, widest at the start of the cut, ~1.2 m in front), i.e. the fist rides ~0.31 m out from the pivot over the first half of the cut; the hand sits 12 % up the handle (`MeleeDemoStickGripAlongLength` 0.12 on `basicplayer.prefab` - held by the grip, not the pommel), so reach = (0.53 + 0.88 x model length) / 1.14: `club_wood` 0.68 m → 0.99, `sword_bone` 0.83 m → 1.1, `sword_scrap` 0.94 m → 1.19, `sword_reforged` 1.0 m → 1.24, `axe_stone` 0.72 m → 1.02, `axe_reforged` 1.01 m → 1.25 (the two axes also carry the `Block` action - sword behaviour for now, still harvest tools). The arc is a circle and the real tip path is not, so the tip still runs up to ~0.2 m outside the arc at the very start of a cut and ~0.1 m inside it in front. The slash ribbon covers the blade from tip to hilt (`SwingSlashWidthFraction` 0.65 on the prefab). Scavs hold theirs the same 12 % up the handle (`EntityCombat.SwordGripAlongLength`, a constant kept equal to the pawn's knob). The overrides apply on top of the scene library rows too, and scavs holding `club_wood` close to that shorter reach. Weapons without the override (unarmed, future one-handers) still use the class 1.54. `GetMeleeWeaponTimings` reads the weapon from the pawn's own slots on the host and the owner and from `PlayerEquipment.NetworkedMainHandResourceId` on every other peer (a client has no slots for other pawns: it used to resolve them to the fallback class, so their slash ribbon kept the 1.54 m reach). `sword_bone` / `sword_scrap` have equipment profiles since the same change (one-handed sword rig, their generated butt-at-origin models as `heldModel`, club stats until the tiers are tuned). Edit reach in `melee_weapon_classes.json`, `combat_timings.csv`, the code fallbacks **and testscene1's `MeleeWeaponTimingLibrary` rows, which override the JSON in that scene** (the editor keeps the old rows in memory until the scene is closed and reopened). `create_sword_anims.py` `PATH_RANGE_U` follows the one-handed reach (61.6 u) so the strike clips stay on the arc; the last path frame keeps `PATH_END_ARM_CLEAR` (5 cm) off the torso so a guard cut from it does not sweep the arm through the chest.
4. **Victim hit reaction** — a landed swing flails the victim for the attacker's class stagger (above); `PlayerAnimation.HitReaction` owns the window. Shove reactions keep their own durations.
5. **Hits** — `MeleeAttackSweep` sphere substeps along tip+heel motion (`MeleeHitVolumeThickness`). Per-target dedup; optional multi-hit via `MeleeAllowMultipleHitsPerAttack`.
6. **Attack path** — `MeleeAttackArcDegreeStep` (default 1°) samples the swing along `MeleeAttackPath` (~150 samples on a 150° arc, 360 on a full turn). Colored overlay lines are optional (`MeleeDebugDrawEnabled`); the path sampling is core combat readability. **Arcs are bound to the swing clip:** a peer only draws active-sweep lines while `PlayerAnimation.HasActiveMeleeSwingPresentation` is true on that machine — never an arc without an animation. Damage sweeps are unaffected (authority never depends on presentation).
7. **`CombatAuthority`** validates intent, rate limits, stamina, then calls `PlayerCombat.ServerStartMeleeAttackAction`.
8. **Footwork** (Mark 2026-11-14 spec) — swings, the guard and staggers never cap or root the pawn: `PlayerCombat.CurrentFootworkState` → `PlayerMovement.GetCombatFootworkScale` scales ground speed (light windup 0.95, release 0.9, recovery 0.95, blocking 0.85, heavy windup 0.8, heavy release 0.75, staggered 0.4; inspector group *Combat Footwork*); sprint stays allowed. Only an aimed bow walks (`IsAttackWalkCapActive`) and the bola charge is walk-only (`MeleeAttackMoveSpeedScale`). The look is never slowed or cone-limited. **Click anim (`PlayerAnimation.MeleeSwing.cs`; Last Oasis reference, Mark 2026-11-05):** the click starts an **animated wind-up draw that runs through the whole windup** (2026-11-16, Mark: "the windup should be moving through the whole thing, right now its just snapping to poses mostly") — `Sword_Cocked_R/L/Over` go `REST_*` → `WIND_*` → `COCKED_*` (`create_sword_anims.py` `wind_up`) under a `CCycleControlAnimNode` each (`make_human_sword_assets.py` `WIND_UP_CLIPS`), so their playhead is the float parameter `sword_wind` = windup progress, set every frame by `PlayerAnimation.WindUpProgress` (press → strike time; never goes back, so a light held into a heavy hangs on the cocked frame). Remote peers draw over the windup the host sends inside the strike-on-cue value (`StrikeOnCueAfter` = -(1 + windup); any value below 0 still means "strike on the host's cue"); the cocked poses are **wound up with a bent elbow** (forehand: fist in front of the right shoulder, club back over it; backhand, its mirror: fist in front of the left shoulder below the chin, club back over the left shoulder; overhead: fist above the head, blade hanging down behind it), never a straight arm on the hit path. The strike (0.64 s, `MeleeStrikeClipSeconds`) goes cocked → a few entry frames (`STRIKE_ENTRY`) → the path over **0.30 s** (`STRIKE_SWOOP_S`; 2026-11-16, Mark: "slower strike phase" with the combat timings kept - the damage window is still the 0.18 s release, so the visible blade trails the damage sweep by up to 0.12 s) → a deep follow-through **held** to 0.47 s (forehand out past the left side, backhand out past the right, both drooping; chop at the ground) → settle → rest; a follow-up click cuts the hold and draws the next wind-up straight out of the follow-through, so chains flow R → L → R. The slash ribbon is the damage arc, not the visible blade (below). The forehand and backhand are mirrors (Mark 2026-11-05): both hit paths tilt 25° (`MeleeAttackTiltDegreesRight` / `Left`, a slight downward swoop), both cocks hold the fist in front of the attacking-side shoulder with the club back over it (the backhand fist never crosses the face - the old behind-the-neck cock clipped the arm through the head), both follow-throughs end out past the far side with the blade drooping. `swing_dir L|R|U` + `swing` film any direction from the console. everything is an upper-body layer of the animgraph, so the legs keep walking. Idle: the graph's **`sword_pose`** selector shows the weapon by the waist whatever side the player aims (2026-11-17, see *Idle carry* under the sword animation section). The click calls `BeginMeleeSwingSnap` (cocked pose at once) + `ReleaseMeleeSwingSnap` (strike predicted one windup later); the host's `PlayMeleeSwingAttack` (remotes via Sync + `HostBroadcastMeleeSwingAnim`) schedules the authoritative strike: `sword_attack` picks the clip and `b_attack` fires it when the damage window opens — every strike clip starts on its cocked frame, sweeps the path over 0.30 s from the moment the 0.18 s release opens (`create_sword_anims.py` `STRIKE_SWOOP_S` / `PATH_ACTIVE_S`) and settles back to rest by **`MeleeStrikeClipSeconds`** (0.40 s). **`b_sword_interrupt`** (graph bool, true while a new swing is cocked) and **`b_sword_guard`** (true while the guard is up) exit the attack state early, so a follow-up's cocked pose or the block pose replaces the settle frames (`Tools/make_human_sword_assets.py`: re-cock cut 0.12 s, guard cut 0.05 s via `b_sword_guard`). Hit reaction / block / shove cancel through `AbortMeleeAttackAnimClip` / `CancelMeleeSwingSnap`. **Graph edits never hot-swap cleanly on a live pawn** — after regenerating the sword graph, stop and restart play.
9. **Combat recovery** — the swing runtime itself carries the windup, release and recovery (`ServerHasActiveMeleeAttackAction` stays true through the recovery); only blocked / chambered swings and the shove use the recovery lock; **victim/blocker fall uses stagger `IsGrounded=false` only** (never `UseAnimGraph=false` flail — that froze clients). Chain-busy (`IsMeleeAttackChainBusy`) = recovery / hit-reaction lock + host swing + the owner's predicted timeline.
   **Stored presses:** one, newest wins (chained at the release end, else fired when free inside the input buffer); the guard button, a stagger and a feint clear it. No auto-repeat from a held button. The block channel still allows a HELD button to re-guard the first frame it may (`CombatChannel.Step allowHeldStart`, no re-guard cooldown; a consumed/broken block still requires an actual release first). Clearing a recovery sequence must preserve a running swing clip (`ExitCombatSequenceToLocomotion` skips the `b_attack` reset while a swing is presenting).
10. **Hit reaction (victim)** — `PlayerAnimation.HitReaction.cs` is the **only** "I was just hit" state, and it lives on **animation, not combat**, so the window and the pose have one owner that always ticks. **Resolve victims by `PlayerAnimation`** (shove trace, `ApplyMeleeStaggerToVictim`) — being hit is not equipment-dependent; `PlayerCombat` only exposes `IsHitReactionActive` for `IsCombatActionLocked` and `OnHitReactionBegan()` to drop its own in-flight state. Host picks a duration (`HitReactionSeconds` default **0.9 s**, or the caller's value — shove victim 1.2 s, shove-vs-block 0.5 s, heavy block 0.4 s) and `[Rpc.Broadcast]` gives every machine the same local deadline. Pose is the **looping flail sequence** `airborne_flail_movement` with `UseAnimGraph=false` (re-asserted from `PlayerAnimation.OnUpdate`, restarted only on the first frame) and the **held sword is destroyed for the whole window** (`TickHoldPose` / `TickMeleeDemoStickTransform` refuse to re-create it); the window end **hard-restores** locomotion and the hold pose once. `PlayerAnimation.TickHitReactionPose` owns **both** the pose and its exit and runs from `OnUpdate` **and `OnPreRender`** — a pawn's `OnUpdate` is skipped on peers where it is a proxy, so an exit that lived only in `PlayerCombat.TickHitReactionState` let that peer loop the flail forever. `HitReactionMaxSeconds` (2.5 s) caps any caller. Do **not** go back to an animgraph-only pose (`b_grounded=false`): `PlayerController` rewrites grounded state every frame on whichever machine simulates the pawn, so only proxies (the host's view of a client) played the reaction — a sequence is the same on every peer. During it: no attack/block/shove (`IsCombatActionLocked`), **no jump** (cleared in `PlayerMovement.PreInput`), and the **grapple detaches and cannot re-attach**. Do **not** re-add authority/proxy gates to this tick — the old stagger path had four gated tick sites and the host never expired client-owned pawns, which froze them in the fall pose and locked them out of combat. `MeleeStaggerTier` survives only as **block JSON data**, and `AirborneFlail` is gone.
11. **Punch / shield bash (F, `PlayerCombat.Shove.cs`)** — a **player ability, not a weapon one**: it works bare-handed and will become an unlockable augment. `PlayerEquipment` must **never** disable `PlayerCombat` to express "no weapon" (that killed the shove, the hit reaction, and the jump/grapple locks at once) — the sword gate is the `PlayerEquippedItem.HasAction( PrimaryMelee )` check inside `PlayerCombat.OnUpdate`, which `TickOwnerShoveInput()` deliberately runs **before**. Hand harvest / gather is **E** (`HandHarvest`); the punch is **F** (`Shove` action — testscene1's player had it overridden to `HandHarvest`, fixed 2026-11-05). Anytime **grounded** (never while the hoe is out — its F is the mode swap), **at someone or at thin air** (Mark 2026-11-05: "add the punch back in"): spends **`ShoveStaminaCost`** (10), starts the punch pose and **lands `PunchWindupSeconds` (0.4 s) later** (`ServerTickPendingPunch`, ticked with the swing runtime; a hit during it does not stop it) on whoever stands within **`ShoveRangeMeters`** (**1.2 m**, radius `ShoveHitRadiusMeters` 0.3 m — real meters via BodyHeight/1.8; shorter than the sword, so get close) flat in front at chest height — **no lunge** (removed 2026-11-05). It breaks a guard but never cancels the target's attack (`ServerApplyShoveVsBlock`). The puncher is locked out of sword / block / punch for the windup **plus `RecoveryShoveCombatLockSeconds`** (0.4 + 0.2 s, `BeginPunchActionLock` — `IsInCombatRecovery` only counts that window for a punch; no spamming), and no second punch for **`PunchCooldownSeconds`** (**1.5 s**, host `PunchCooldownRemaining` + owner prediction) while the punch pose plays on for its clip — every punch restarts the pose (`ServerBeginCombatRecovery` never skips a new punch); a swing or a raised guard after the lock clears it. Unblocked → `ShoveDamage` (3) + the victim's hit reaction for **`RecoveryDefenderShoveSeconds`** (**0.7 s**, Mark 2026-11-12); vs block → blocker hit reaction `RecoveryDefenderShoveBlockSeconds` (0.4 s). **The punch buys one free follow-up, never a chain** (Mark 2026-11-12; 2026-11-05: punches could stagger a bot forever): after it lands the puncher is free at +0.15 s and a one-handed light swing (0.2 s windup) lands before the punched pawn is free at +0.7 s, so the follow-up cannot be blocked; the 1.5 s cooldown means the next punch can't land before the victim has acted (`combat_test` checks this exchange). **Direction** (`GetServerPunchForward`, 2026-11-10): the local driver's camera; for a remote owner or a scripted pawn the owner-synced `PlayerController.EyeAngles` yaw, then the body - never `Scene.Camera`, which is the host's own view and used to aim a remote player's punch (and the suite's) wherever the host happened to be looking. **With a shield out** (as each peer sees it, `PlayerAnimation.HasShieldShown`) the same F is a **shield bash**: identical rules (no lunge either), but instead of the left-hand punch clip the shield drives into the block pose and `ShieldBashForwardMeters` (0.35 m) past it over `ShieldBashSeconds` (0.35 s), the arm following it (`PlayerAnimation.Shield.cs`); the host's punch broadcast (`RpcBroadcastShovePunch`) cues it on every peer.
12. **Facing & camera — the body always faces the mouse look** (2026-11-17, Mark: "when we spin our mouse it rotates the player. Righht now it works like valheim but i dont want that. It should basically always keep the player facing where your mouse is looking", then "prevent players from rotating too fast, the players body should have a max rotation speed (and sword attacks have no effect on that)"; the Valheim free orbit, `FreeLookOrbitEnabled` and the first-person body turn limit are deleted). `PlayerMovement.BodyTurn.cs` turns the body toward `PlayerController.EyeAngles.yaw` at most **Body turn rate (deg/s)** (`BodyTurnDegreesPerSecond`, 360 - 540 was "a tad too loose", 2026-11-17) — standing, moving, swinging (windup, release and recovery alike), blocking, first and third person. Every machine runs the same tracker on the synced EyeAngles (lazy, once per frame: `BodyYawUnwrapped` / `BodyYawDegrees` / `BodyTurnRateDegreesPerSecond`), so the host's copy of a client's body obeys the cap too; `SnapBodyYawToLook` on teleports. The stock controller never turns the body itself (`TickControllerBodyRotationOff`: `RotationAngleLimit` 180, `RotationSpeed` 0.001 on every machine); seated, gliding or in the stasis pod (`OwnsBodyFacing` false) those systems pose the body. Measured 2026-11-17: a 170° flick turned the body 104° in 0.29 s (360°/s). **No WASD body facing** (removed 2026-11-14, Last Oasis): W forward, A / D strafe, S walks backwards; ground speeds are `PlayerMovement` *Ground Speed* (walk 4.5 m/s, run 5.85 m/s forward = walk x1.3 since 2026-11-18 - Mark: "increase walk speed and make run speed like barely faster than walk speed", then "make run 20% faster instead of 10%", then "make run speed 30% faster than walk"; was 3.5 / 6.8, then 4.5 / 5.4; strafe ×0.8, backward ×0.7, `GetDirectionalSpeedScale`). **Facing is presentation, not physics**: never fight the pawn ROOT for facing — the stock controller + rigidbody interpolation re-align it every frame, and root writes visibly spazz. `PlayerAnimation.TickCombatFacingPresentation` writes the tracker's yaw (or the bow's sideways archer yaw) to the **Body renderer child only**, from OnUpdate + OnPreRender so it lands last before render on every peer (`PlayerCombat.TryGetCombatFacingYaw` and the shove facing lock are deleted: the swing, the guard and the punch all face the body's yaw now). Never reset the body's LocalRotation to identity outside the seat / wingsuit — **the physics root never rotates in this project (it keeps its spawn yaw forever)**, so root-relative resets turn the model toward the SPAWN direction. **Combat writes NOTHING to the pawn root or the camera — ever.** Any root write on a combat action moves the player's view (the controller couples them internally); that class of bug came back twice before this rule was made absolute. **Steep look-up** fades the local model + held props out over `HideBodyLookUpFadeSeconds` (1 s) — only when pitch ≤ −35° **and** the pawn is grounded **and** the camera boom trace is squeezed by a ground-like surface (`PlayerMovement.CameraBumpingGround`, hit normal z > 0.5) — and back in when any gate drops (`PlayerAnimation.HideBodyOnSteepLookUp`; owner's screen only — Tint/RenderType are not networked).
13. **First person shows your arms only** (2026-11-16, Mark: "Sword seems too close to face, no arms or feet", then "Make it so we just see our arms, no chest or legs"; `PlayerAnimation.FirstPerson.cs` + `PlayerMovement` *Camera* group). The stock controller tags the pawn's renderers `viewer` every frame in first person and the pawn camera excludes that tag, so `PlayerMovement.PostCameraSetup` lets the first-person camera draw `viewer`, nudges it **First person: camera forward (m)** (0.14) ahead of the eyes; the body follows the view at the body turn cap like in third person (the 10° first-person limit is deleted, 2026-11-17). For the owner, the body and every worn piece draw shadow-only (the ground shadow stays the whole player) and an arms-only renderer of the same model (child of the body, no animation graph) stands in: every bone is set to the body's animated pose each frame, the pelvis / spine / neck shrink to nothing behind the camera, the collarbones shrink in place, the head, legs and feet body groups are empty. Bone merge cannot do this (it overwrites every bone from the body). `SolveTwoBoneArm` (rest grip, shield) writes its bones to that renderer too, carrying fingers and the grip bone along. A one-handed sword rests in a view-space grip (**Sword grip forward / right / down (m)** 0.55 / 0.3 / 0.25, blade lean 28° forward / 15° in so the whole blade shows, following the look pitch only to ±40°); swings / wind-ups / the guard play the normal clips lifted **Swing lift up / forward (m)** 0.18 / 0.1 up the view so the strike crosses the screen, but never above **Swing lift ceiling (m)** 0.2 under eye level. **No clipping** (Mark 2026-11-16: "we should never see clipping like that"): the solve stays on for every one-handed sword in first person (seat / wingsuit excepted), the blade tip leans back from the view at most **Tip lean back limit (deg)** 15, and the blade plus the fist are pushed to **Blade clearance (m)** 0.4 from the camera before and after the arm solve; the collarbones fold into the shoulder joints so a raised arm does not stretch an armpit web. Owner only: remote peers and third person are untouched. Known: the right wind-up takes the blade out of frame for ~0.4 s; a small skin flap shows for a frame as the overhead comes down and at the right shoulder when looking straight down; the bow's own bend overrides are not mirrored; worn sleeves / gloves are not on the bare first-person arms; greatswords, bows, torches and tools have no first-person grip yet.
14. **Block (hold) — directional** (Mark 2026-11-14) — Attack2 raises the guard in the teardrop direction (L / R / U, morphed only by mouse flicks, look rotation compensated). It stops nothing until `BlockActivationSeconds` (**0.15** since 2026-11-17, was 0.1) after it rose (`ServerBlockActiveFromSandbox`; the shield / sword pose rises in the same 0.15 s at a steady rate - `make_human_sword_assets.py` `GUARD_BLEND_SECONDS` 0.15 linear, was 0.05 - Mark: "if you stand still and click block it raises the block instantly, but if we hold block and swap directions it goes as slow as it should"; testscene1 carried a stale `BlockSwitchSeconds` 0.08, now 0.15), a direction change takes `BlockSwitchSeconds` (0.15 since 2026-11-16, Mark: "make blocking .15s to swap from one side to the other"; was 0.08 — the old side covers meanwhile, `GetEffectiveGuardDirection`; a change while the guard is still rising, i.e. inside the activation time, is no switch - the guard just rises on the new side, and `PlayerAnimation` keeps `sword_guard_dir` on `GetPendingBlockDirection()` while the guard is down so the pose rises straight into its side: 2026-11-16, Mark: "when we are moving our mouse and clicking left or right to do a block it takes longer than if we just stand still"), a tap stays up `BlockMinHoldSeconds` (0.1), and it cannot rise during the owner's own swing (feint aside), in a lockout or while exhausted. A block needs, in `MeleeBlockResolution.TryResolve`: the attacker inside the front arc (the arc alone bounds the block shell since 2026-11-16: `basicplayer.prefab` carried 67.5 and `MeleeBlockPath` cut the shell at the shoulder line, so a blow from the side the marker called never blocked; the prefab is 270 now and the cut is deleted; `MeleeBlockFrontArcDegrees` 270°, angle fixed to s&box's Z-up on 2026-11-14 — it used to flatten the wrong axis), the attack ray entering the body-radius shell first, and the **guard on the side the blade comes in from** (`ResolveIncomingSide`: an attacker more than `MeleeBlockFlankDegrees` (15°; the pawn prefab's front arc is 67.5°, so the flank zone is 15°-33.75° each side) to the left / right of the defender's look yaw is blocked with that side's guard whatever the swing - 2026-11-16, Mark: "if they do overhead or anything else, right block. because they're to my RIGHT"; an attacker in front: overhead / thrust = up, else the side of the defender where the swing started; the scav guard pick, the scav swing pick and the incoming-attack icon read the same function). **The side is locked per swing** (2026-11-17, Mark: the red arrow "frequently flips to the other side last second"; the defender's own mouse flick to pick a guard crossed the 15° flank line - he chose "lock side per swing" over widening or removing it): `ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.LatchIncomingSides` resolves it once for every pawn within 4.5 m as the windup starts (and again on a heavy morph), and the host's block check (`ResolveIncomingSideLatched`), the scav guard pick and the red marker (`PlayerCombat.TryGetLatchedIncomingSide`; on a client with no swing for that attacker, i.e. a scav, the marker latches its own at first sight) all read that. Not guarding, the defender's facing is its body yaw (`PlayerMovement.BodyYawDegrees`) - the host's root yaw of a client pawn is its spawn yaw. A correct block stops all damage, costs the `block` / `blockHeavy` row's stamina (`melee_block_stagger.json`: 8 / **18** since 2026-11-16 - Mark: a blocked heavy is worse for the defender; a guard that cannot pay empties the bar) and **breaks the guard** (2026-11-16, Mark: "Guard breaks means your character is no longer blocking and need to release block and click again for it to come up"): `HostBreakGuard` → `ConsumeAuthoritativeMeleeBlock`, the same path as an emptied bar, so holding block does nothing until it is let go and pressed again. **Taking a hit breaks it too** (`HostTryStagger`, `PlayerVitals.ApplyDamageAfterArmor`). An **arrow** stopped by the guard does not break it (`ServerApplyBlockOutcome(..., breaksGuard: false)`, it only restarts the free window). Scavs let go of a broken guard and press again 0.12-0.25 s later (`EntityCombat.TickGuardOutput`, `HostGuardAwaitingRelease`). The counter window and the stagger windows are unchanged. **Chamber** (checked first, for any defender with `PlayerCombat`): the defender's own swing releases within ±`ChamberToleranceSeconds` (0.075) of the contact and **mirrors the attack** (`MeleeAttackTypes.Mirror`: overhead vs overhead, left vs right, right vs left - attack types, not positions; 2026-11-16, Mark: "it needs to mirror the attack"; scavs pick the mirrored swing too) → no damage / stamina / stagger, the incoming swing stops as blocked (`MeleeHitResult.WasChambered`), the chambering swing carries on (multi-chamber / target switch follow from the geometry); the Parry Recharge augment now pays out on a chamber. Holding the guard costs nothing, however long (2026-11-16, Mark: "Holding block shouldn't eat stamina btw, that costs nothing unless you actually block" - the old 2 s free window + 5/s long-guard drain, `StandardBlockFreeSeconds` / `LongBlockStaminaPerSecond`, are deleted); only a blocked blow costs stamina. A blocked melee blow or a hit breaks the guard, a fresh press raises it again; a blocked arrow leaves the guard up. **A raised guard blocks all stamina regen** (2026-11-16, Mark: "Raised guard blocks regen"; Parry Recharge boost included): `VitalsAuthority` skips a pawn whose `PlayerCombat.IsAuthoritativeMeleeBlocking`, `EntityVitals` a scav whose guard is up; regen returns on the normal delay once it drops. So the free window costs nothing and gives nothing back. **Arrows**: any guard direction stops an arrow from the front (`MeleeBlockContact.IgnoreGuardDirection`). **Audio cues** (Mark 2026-11-14, `PlayerCombat.CombatAudio.cs`): the host broadcasts one cue per outcome, always on the **attacker's** `PlayerCombat` - a landed hit on another player / on anything else (swing runtime `OnHit`, player = has `PlayerVitals`), blocked light, blocked heavy, chamber (`ServerApplyBlockOutcome`, routed through the attacker's combat). A swing that hits nothing is silent: the miss swoosh (cue, `SwooshSound` slot, `melee_swoosh.sound`, generator function) was removed 2026-11-16 (Mark: "just remove the whole swoosh thing for now, that's bad ... it's playing mid-other audio clips"). Inspector slots *Combat — Audio* (`HitPlayerSound` / `HitOtherSound` / `BlockedSound` / `BlockedHeavySound` / `ChamberSound`), empty = the project events `Assets/sounds/combat/melee_{hit_player,hit_other,block,block_heavy,chamber}.sound` (2026-11-16, Mark: the core flesh hit was "squelchy, like a wet sponge"; blocks should clack like a wooden block drum, the parry a special higher clack). All synthesised by `Tools/combat_sounds/generate_combat_sounds.py` (re-run it to retune; `--audition <wav>` writes every variant in a row): a fleshy thud for a hit on a player (4 variants; Mark 2026-11-16: the engine's was "squelchy", a pitched dropping sine was a kick drum, resonant modes + soft clipping "sounds like we're hitting corrugated metal", a low noise whump with a faint slap missed "we're hitting bare flesh", a bright skin smack sounded like "a trashcan being hit" - so the impact is now the engine's REAL recording, `core/sounds/Impacts/Melee/BluntWeapon/flesh-1..4.wav` (read from the s&box install, `SBOX_ROOT` overrides the path), cut to its first 55-90 ms where the low band has fallen 10 dB, 15 ms fade, 3.5 kHz low-pass: the 1.2-1.5 s recordings' wet tail was the squelch. Then the hit player's "ungh" 40-60 ms later (Mark: "Maybe the player goes 'ungh'"; `grunt(i)`, formant speech synthesis: Rosenberg glottal pulses with jitter / shimmer, ~150 -> ~95 Hz, breathy onset, creak at the end, four formants gliding from 'uh' 640 / 1190 / 2390 Hz into the nasal 'ng'; baked into the same WAV, so every player hit grunts), that clanking thud for a hit on anything else (4; "keep that for when we hit anything but another player"), two wooden sticks whacked together, CLACK (5 block variants; a rough contact plus 1-3 small chatter contacts through a **low-Q** wood body - 64 modes over 650-10000 Hz at Q 6-18, energy-equal, centred 1.7-2.3 kHz - a broadband crack and a 4th-order high-pass at 600 Hz, gone in ~20 ms), CLACK + CLUNK for a blocked heavy (3; a lower clack centred 1.1-1.4 kHz with more chatter, over a dense low-Q 170-650 Hz wooden clunk at half level - Mark 2026-11-16: heavy block and chamber were "too similar", "maybe throw in a clunk noise for blocking heavy attacks"), a double clack 'ka-TAK' for a **chamber** (2.4 kHz, then 70 ms later a harder 3.8 kHz one; fixed pitch so it is always the same sound; renamed from "parry" 2026-11-16 - Mark: a parry is a perfectly timed block, a chamber is two attacks meeting; the game has no timed parry yet); a **parry** "ting" is ready in `melee_parry.sound` - two swords meeting, not a bell (Mark 2026-11-16: the first free-steel-bar ring "is like a bell"): a sub-ms metal strike through ~45 dense inharmonic blade modes at 2.2-11 kHz weighted to ~4.6 kHz, decays 40-380 ms falling with pitch, a 4-10 kHz edge scrape for ~60 ms, high-passed at 1.2 kHz, fixed pitch but nothing plays it until the timed parry exists; the events add random pitch on top. Mark 2026-11-16 on the third pass: "You nailed the clacking sound finally!" - keep the block clacks as they are. The first pass (clean partials ringing 85-200 ms) was rejected the same day as "a xylophone", the second (Q 70-100 mode clusters + a 260-520 Hz knock resonator, 16-66 % of each clack's energy under 600 Hz) as "literal drums" - keep the clacks dry, low-Q and high-passed: no ringing note, nothing under ~500 Hz. **A click while the guard is up** (held, or inside the minimum hold after a feint tap) waits in the `AttackInputBufferSeconds` buffer and fires as the guard drops (2026-11-16: attack > guard tap > new side > attack lost a quick second click). **Incoming-attack marker, scavs and players** (Mark 2026-11-16: "I want it to work like last oasis ... the game says 'Block right' and if you do in time then great"; the 2026-11-14 "PvE only, like LO" rule is gone - another player's wind-up raises it too, read from the wind-up pose this machine shows on their body, `PlayerAnimation.TryGetShownSwordWindup`): while a scav (`EntityCombat.IsWindupShown`, every peer, from the cocked pose through the windup) within 4.5 m faces the pawn within 50°, the owner's crosshair draws a red triangle 30 px out on the side the blade will come from, pointing in (`PlayerCombat.TryGetIncomingAttackSide` → `ResolveIncomingSide`, the same function the host resolves the block with, in the guard teardrop's frame: guard toward the marker blocks). Nearest attacker only, re-resolved every 0.05 s from the static `EntityCombat.Fighters` list and the player pawns; animals never raise it. **Owner latency give-back** (2026-11-17, Mark: "when we attack each other only i can land the hit" / "arrows are barely working for him, can you make his game predict them?"): a remote owner's click reaches the host one trip late, so the host's own swing always landed first and interrupted the client's. `PlayerCombat.HostOwnerLatencySeconds()` = half the owner connection's measured `Ping`, capped at `OwnerLatencyCompensationMaxSeconds` (0.12; 0 for the host's pawn, scavs, offline, test pawns). A swing started on arrival (`HostSubmitPlayerSwing`, not a buffered / chained one) plays that much less windup on the host (the `telegraphedSeconds` path scavs use); a bow shot's host arrow flies that much first (`ArrowProjectile.HostCatchUp`, ordinary swept steps). **Arrow prediction**: a client's own shot spawns a local un-networked twin at the release (`ArrowProjectile.ClientSpawnPredicted`, same launch via `ComputeBowLaunchVelocity`, no damage, no pickup, lands where that machine's trace says); the host's networked arrow is hidden on the shooter's machine and only brings the hit tick. The spread (widest through the bow's power phase, narrowing over its accuracy phase, see *Bow*) is rolled by the owner before the intent is sent (the host cannot see a remote owner's screen to size the cone; `BowFireIntent.ClientSeed` is gone). **Resync** (Settings > Dev tools > Resync players, console `net_resync`; Mark 2026-11-16, for a desync after a code hotload): anyone asks, the host calls `GameObject.Network.Refresh()` on every networked object and repeats the host-wide dev settings (`DevToolsSettings.RequestResync`). Always on (no settings toggle yet).

**Playtest pass 2026-11-18 (Mark + Rumple, listen server) - these supersede the older wording above where they differ.**
- **Fluid block + block pivot** (Settings > Controls > *Fluid block*, on by default; `CombatControlSettings`, saved per machine in `combat_controls.json`; owner-side input rules only, the host needs none). On: a mouse flick moves a held guard to another side (as before), and **holding attack as well as block keeps the guard on its side** (`PlayerCombat.IsBlockPivotInput` / `IsGuardSideHeld`) so the look turns the body without moving the guard; that attack press is not a swing and stores none (the block button itself must be down - a click inside a tapped guard's minimum hold still queues an attack). Off: the guard keeps the side it rose on (a flick inside the rise time, `BlockActivationSeconds`, still belongs to the press) and a new side needs a release and a fresh press.
- **Physical guard** (`MeleeGuardGeometry`, `PlayerCombat` group *Combat - Block (guard bar)*, `MeleePhysicalGuardEnabled`; Mark 2026-11-23: "the block hitbox on an overhead isnt where the weapon is ... if a weapons arc were to collide with a weapon AS its going to its block position then it should count as a block"): every guard is a bar that turns with the **body** yaw - a left / right guard upright in front of that shoulder (0.35 m forward, 0.3 m to the side, 0.9 - 1.95 m high), the overhead guard level across above the head (`GuardBarOverhead*`: 1.86 m high, 0.2 m forward, 0.45 m to each side). Catch radius 0.28 m (`GuardCatchRadiusMeters`, written on basicplayer / scavT1 / bossT1). The bar also exists while the weapon travels (`GetGuardBarAt`): rising from the waist carry over `BlockActivationSeconds` with the catch radius growing 0 -> 1 (a guard pressed at the instant of contact catches nothing), and crossing from the old guard to the new one over `BlockSwitchSeconds`. In `MeleeBlockResolution.TryResolve` the side the marker called always blocks once the guard is up; any other guard, and a guard still rising, blocks **any swing** whose weapon line (swing pivot -> tip, the damage ray's line) comes within the catch radius of the bar before it reaches the body's core (flat, at or below the top of the head - an overhead still above the head has not arrived). Arrows never use the bar. An analytic bar, never a bone or a collider (the host must get the same answer for a pawn it does not render). combat_test: "a guard turned into the blade during the swing blocks it (block pivot)", "the overhead guard's bar is in an overhead's way, not in a side swing's", "a guard still rising stops a blade that runs into it; one just pressed does not".
- **Guard meet** (`PlayerAnimation.GuardMeet.cs`, `PlayerCombat.GuardMeetPushMeters` 0.4 m, `PlayerAnimation.GuardMeetSeconds` 0.3 s): `ServerApplyBlockOutcome` re-reads `MeleeGuardGeometry.TryMeetGuard` while the guard is still up - the block sparks / clack move to the point on the attacker's weapon nearest the guard, and the offset guard point -> weapon point (clamped to the push) rides the sparks RPC (`RpcPlayBlockSparks( hitWorld, meetPush )`). Every peer's `StartGuardMeet` reaches the sword hand out by that offset and back with a two-bone arm solve on top of the graph (out in the first fifth, held to the half, eased back); the sword rides `hold_R`. Skipped with a shield (the shield took the blow), during a hit reaction and while the first-person rest grip owns the right arm. Presentation only.
- **Incoming side by where the blade arrives**: `ResolveIncomingSide` first asks `MeleeGuardGeometry.TryFindBodyEntry` - a side swing that reaches the body is called on the side of the body its weapon line first touches, wherever the attacker stands (up close a small sidestep used to put the attacker past the 15 deg flank line and the marker called the attacker's side while the blade came round the other). Out of reach, or an overhead: the flank / start-tip rules as before.
- **The marker side comes from the host**: `LatchIncomingSides` runs on the host runtime only and sends each in-range defender's owner its side (`PlayerCombat.HostSendIncomingSide` -> `RpcOwnerIncomingSide`, at the windup start and on every heavy morph); a client's marker reads that before its own first-sight latch. A client's replay runtime never latches (it used to answer with its own guess, which went stale on a morph).
- **Slash ribbon on clients**: the host's swing broadcast (`FlushDeferredSwingVisualBroadcasts`) was still gated on `MeleeDebugDrawEnabled` (off on the prefab), so no replay and no ribbon ever ran on a client. The gate is `ClientMeleeSwingTraceDebug` alone now. A replay (`visualOnly` runtime) stays cocked until **this machine's swing clip fires its strike** (`PlayerAnimation.TickMeleeSwing` -> `PlayerCombat.OnSwingStrikeShown` -> `StrikeShown`), so held heavies, morphs and chained windups draw their ribbon with the strike; it ends if the clip goes away. The owner's "backup" replay start from `RpcReceiveAttackReleaseResult` (`_pendingSwingVisualIntent`) is deleted - it started on a buffered click while the previous swing still ran. scavT1 / bossT1 keep `ClientMeleeSwingTraceDebug` false (no scav ribbons on clients yet).
- **Punch pose exit lives on `PlayerAnimation`** (`TickPunchPoseExit`, OnUpdate + OnPreRender, every peer): the one-shot punch clip returns the body to the animgraph when it ends. Before, a watching peer relied on the combat recovery state, and once the puncher had been hit a single time `_ownerSuppressNetworkedRecovery` stayed set there for good (its only reset is an `Rpc.Owner`) - the host's punch froze the host's model on clients on the clip's last frame, sword swings included.
- **Body turn keeps its direction** (`PlayerMovement.BodyTurn.cs`): the body chases the look yaw counted on through full turns (`_lookYawUnwrapped`), so a look that runs more than half a turn ahead keeps the body (and the swing arc on it) turning the way the mouse went; whole turns of backlog are dropped.
- **Overhead guard pose**: `BLOCK_UP` holds the blade level above the head at 1.86 m (was 1.56 m, in front of the face), the height of the side guards' tips.
- **Combat sensitivity** (Settings > Controls, 1-10, default 1; `CombatControlSettings.Sensitivity`): scales the mouse travel a direction flip needs, attack cursor and guard alike - `DirectionFlipPixelScale` (1 at level 1, geometric to 14 at level 10: the 7 px commit becomes ~100 px) multiplies `SwingFlipCommitPixels` and the classifier's floor, `DirectionEvidenceDecayScale` (1 -> 2.5) stretches `SwingEvidenceDecaySeconds` so a slower deliberate movement still adds up. The left <-> right extra (`SwingLateralFlipExtraPixels`) is not scaled.
- **Show damage information** (Settings > Game settings, off by default; `PlayerCombat.DamageInfo.cs`): the host reports each contact of a swing to the swing's owner (`HostReportDamageInfo` -> `RpcOwnerDamageInfo`: damage, the turn damage scale that hit used, heavy / blocked / chambered) and `PlayerCrosshair` shows one line under the crosshair for 4 s ("12 damage · light · turn +10%").
- **Held chamber** (Mark: "if were holding left click when the matching attack is coming ... it should block the hit and then swing the sword after ... its kinda 2 phases"): in `MeleeBlockResolution.TryChamber`, a defender whose swing is still winding up with the button down on the host's view (`ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.IsWindupHeld`), mirrors the blow and has the attacker in its front arc chambers it; the host then releases that swing (`PlayerCombat.HostReleaseHeldSwingAfterChamber`: `HostNotePrimaryAttackReleased` + `RpcOwnerSwingReleasedByChamber`, the owner's cocked pose strikes although the button is still down), so it strikes after, as its release rules allow (a light at its windup end, a heavy never before the heavy windup). The timed chamber (a tapped mirrored swing releasing within +-`ChamberToleranceSeconds`, what scavs use) is unchanged. combat_test: "a held mirrored swing chambers the blow before it strikes", "the held swing strikes after its chamber, still held".

**Active combat helpers** (do not add parallel systems): `MeleeAttackPath`, `MeleeAttackSweep`, `MeleeAttackResolution`, `MeleeBlockPath`, `MeleeBlockResolution`, `MeleeWeaponClassCatalog` (`Code/Combat/`), `PlayerCombat.ServerMeleeAttack.cs`, `PlayerCombat.Block.cs`, `PlayerCombat.AttackCommitmentLock.cs`, `PlayerCombat.CombatRecovery.cs`, `PlayerCombat.Shove.cs`, `PlayerCombat.Durability.cs`, `AttackCombatTypes.cs`, `MeleeAttackTypes.cs`.

**Regression suite:** `combat_test` (`Code/Combat/Tests/CombatTest.cs`, play mode, any scene) — two stripped `basicplayer` pawns (controller / HUD / camera off, `DrainStaminaForTests` on, wooden club through the hotbar) fight 300 m up through `CombatAuthority.ValidateAndApplyPrimaryMelee`: full windup even when the intent claims a hold, stamina, windup / release / recovery length, stagger + its length, buffer expiry, three-swing chain with combo windups / same-direction windup / combo cap / combo-end recovery, feint (cost, lock, refused in the release), directional block (sides, wrong side, overhead, a block breaks the guard and it stays down while still held, lockout, counter windup), long guard (drains past the free window through side swaps, a blocked blow and a hit taken each break the guard, a fresh press gets a new free window, the drain returns, a raised guard regenerates nothing, regen returns when it drops), held-light feint late in the windup, a held heavy refuses the feint, heavy (hang, strike on release, damage, cost, never strikes by itself + drain), heavy morph (a tapped light refuses it, side change, strike waits the settle, the guard on the new side blocks it), blocked heavy costs the heavy row, breaks the guard and staggers vs attacker initiative, hit interrupts the victim's windup, hard stagger protection, chamber (and a mistimed miss), turn rate, exhaustion, punch rules, back arc. Hooks: `TestSubmitBlockIntent(dir)`, `HostTryFeint`, `HostTryHeavyMorph`, `HostTryStagger`, `TestSwingWindupSeconds` / `TestComboStep` / `TestSwingPhase` / `TestSwingHeavyHolding` / `TestSwingAttackType` / `TestResetExchange`. Owed after any combat edit.

**Swing / block presentation (2026-11-02, Mark: "disable the debug view lines").** The DebugOverlay attack path, block guard arc, footprint wedge and windup telegraph are off by default (`MeleeDebugDrawEnabled`, `ShowBlockVisualization`, `ShowBlockFootprintVolume`, `ShowMeleeAttackWindupTelegraph`, in code and on `basicplayer` / `scavT1` / `bossT1`); they remain inspector toggles. In their place: `PlayerCombat.SwingVfx.cs` owns one `MeleeSwingVfx` (`Code/Combat/MeleeSwingVfx.cs`, a `SceneCustomObject` drawing world triangles through `UnlitVfxGeometry` / `shaders/vfx_unlit.shader`) per pawn - a white slash ribbon that shows where the swing just dealt damage (tip edge bright, inner edge faint, 0.16 s life) and white streaks off the shield when a block lands (`ServerApplyBlockOutcome` → `RpcPlayBlockSparks`, origin from `PlayerAnimation.TryGetShieldWorld`). Every pawn feeds the ribbon from the **damage arc** (Mark 2026-11-05: "where damage was just caused ... a clean swoop"): `AdvanceAttackPath` calls `FeedSwingVfxFromDamageArc` each tick of the damage window on whichever peer runs the swing (host authority or the client replay), which fills `SlashArcSteps` (32) samples per arc between ticks, closes the ribbon at progress 1 when the window ends, and starts a new ribbon when progress goes back / the type changes / 0.25 s pass; the outer edge is the `MeleeAttackPath` tip, the inner edge `SwingSlashWidthFraction` (0.45) of the way to the swing pivot; stabs draw none. Following the placed sword instead (the old `PlayerAnimation.SlashTrail.cs` feed) painted the wrist roll and the entry keys as a messy fan. The visible blade tracks the arc within a few degrees from about a fifth of the way in; before that the forehand ribbon starts out right while the club is still coming over the shoulder. `PlayerAnimation.SlashTrail.cs` now only feeds the heavy-ready glint (`TickHeavyFlashFromHeldBlade`). Lessons: a static `Material` field survives hotload (never cache the material in a static); `Graphics.CameraPosition` is not usable inside `RenderSceneObject` (strips are two crossed quads instead of camera-facing); a shader on the `common/*.hlsl` vertex path needs `float4 vColor : COLOR0 < Semantic( Color ); >` added to `VertexInput` and renders `Vertex( pos, color )` draws fine, while the gizmo-style `system.fxc` path did not.

### Artist model import (Rumple's Tripo packs → model + material + prefab)

- **One command per pack**: `Blender/scripts/import_artist_pack.py` (or the `.bat`) takes a single-mesh `.blend` with a packed colour texture and the Pixelate / Posterize node chain and writes `Assets/models/<category>/<name>.fbx + .vmdl` and `Assets/materials/<category>/<name>.png + .vmat`. Categories: `weapons` (import_scale 0.4, hull physics), `building` (0.4 — the kit's 40 u/m since 2026-11-08, no physics: `BuildPieceCollider` boxes it), `environment` (0.4, mesh physics). Blender metres are game metres; `length=` (weapons) or `scale=` rescale when Tripo's unit-cube normalisation is not the intended size (the sword came out 1.33 m on the diagonal and is imported at `length=1.0`).
- **Texture**: the whole Base Color node chain (Pixelate UV snap, Posterize steps, Hue/Sat, Brightness/Contrast) is evaluated texel for texel in numpy at the Pixelate size (or `texture=N`; weapons use 256) and written as a straight sRGB PNG — verified against Blender's own EEVEE render of the chain (mean error 2/255). `chain=raw` skips the colour nodes and ships the painted texture downsampled: use it when the artist's Posterize wrecks a texture (the sword's brown leather grip became black / red bands at 8 steps, so `sword_reforged` is imported raw; chest and table keep the chain); the `.vmat` uses `shaders/pixel_lit.shader` with `PixelRoughness` taken from the Principled roughness. A Cycles bake was tried first and returns a flat colour for the packed Tripo textures in Blender 5.1, so do not go back to baking. Unknown colour nodes make the script exit with the node name — extend `apply_node`.
- **Frames**: the FBX import turns Blender X into engine Y (the kit's `KitMeshYaw`), so author like the kit and never compensate in the script. Weapons: PCA-straightened, handle along Blender +X with the head at +X (`head=` says which blend axis the tip points along — look at a render), **origin at the butt**; `HeldModelFit` treats an origin within 10 % of one end of the long axis as the butt, so a sword is held by the hilt even though its cross-guard is the wider end (the cross-section heuristic stays for centred models such as the axe). Building: bbox centred on the origin, width X / depth Y / height Z; paste the printed `SizesMeters` line into `BuildModuleDimensions` — that table is the collider and the ground-sit — and set the piece's `fallbackColor` to `1,1,1,1` so `BuildPiece` does not tint the texture. Environment: centred on XY, standing on z = 0.
- **Then the asset**: equipment gets `heldModel` in `equipment_profiles.json` (Rumple's sword and axe are the Reforged weapons: `sword_reforged` → `models/weapons/sword_reforged.vmdl`, `axe_reforged` → `models/weapons/axe_reforged.vmdl`; the wooden club and stone axe use their generated item models); build pieces get the model on the prefab's `Visual` child at scale 1 with the root `BoxCollider` matching the mesh-frame size (`BuildPieceCollider.Ensure` re-sizes it at runtime anyway), the `SizesMeters` row set to the printed metres and `fallbackColor` `1,1,1,1`. The **chest** is Rumple's `box1pack.blend` since 2026-10-06 (`building chest`, 0.83 × 0.71 × 0.93 m, `models/building/chest.vmdl`; the five testscene1 kit chests were re-seated +10.85 units). The nine crafting stations (workbench, drying rack, furnace, anvil, refinery, cauldron, fabricator, chemistry station, augment station) are **temp generated models** since 2026-11-08: `Tools/item_models/itemgen/items_stations.py` → `models/building/<id>/<id>.vmdl` at 40 u/m (`BuildModuleDimensions.UnitsPerMeter`), prefabs `prefabs/build/<id>.prefab` (root `BoxCollider` + `BuildPiece`, model on the `Visual` child; only the workbench and augment station carry a station component — the other seven are placeable props until their crafting gameplay exists). Rumple's `augtable1pack.blend` in `Assets/models/building/Blender files/` is still there for a real augment table. Blend sources stay beside the models (`Assets/models/weapons/blenderfiles/`, `Assets/models/environment/<biome>/blenderFiles/`, `Assets/models/building/Blender files/`).
- **Material-only packs** (`model=none`): when the artist re-saves a pack whose mesh is already in game, bake only the texture + `.vmat`. The clover tree is the case: `clovertree1.blend` is the same 1217-vertex mesh as Rumple's hand-split `clovertree/whole.fbx` (×7.5), so `environment cloverhills/bark model=none` rewrites `materials/environment/cloverHills/trees/cloverTree/bark.png + .vmat` (pixel_lit, 256 texels, his Hue/Sat chain baked in) and every piece of `clovertree_chop.prefab` picks it up through the shared bark material; the cut faces keep `bark_cap.vmat`. The 2026-10-05 axe / sword re-saves only dropped the Pixelate / Posterize chain (same mesh), so they re-import with the same options as before and `chain=raw` is no longer needed for the sword. Packs saved in Texture Paint mode are fine (the script drops to Object mode first). A texture-size comparison variant lives beside it: `clovertree128.blend` (same mesh, a pre-shrunk 128×128 image, no Pixelate node) imports as `environment cloverhills/bark128 model=none` → `bark128.png + .vmat`, and `clovertree/whole128.vmdl` is `whole.vmdl` with the bark remap pointed at `bark128.vmat`; testscene1 has it as the `clovertree_chop_128` prefab instance (ModelRenderer + ModelCollider overridden to `whole128`) 8 m east of the 256 tree.

### Tool / weapon durability + workbench repair

- **Config** lives on equipment profiles (`Assets/data/equipment_profiles.json`): `durabilityMax` (uses before broken; 0 = never wears) and `durabilityDrainSecondsEquipped` (torch/lantern passive drain: 1 tick per N seconds while in the active hotbar slot). Rules live in `ToolDurability`; wear state rides `InventorySlot.Wear` (uses consumed, 0 = fresh) through every grid, cursor drag, container, world drop, and sync RPC. `InventorySlot.CrafterName` rides the same plumbing (set at craft time in `PlayerCrafting.HostTryCraft` for equipment-profile outputs only), and so does `InventorySlot.Tier` (workbench upgrade tier, below) — **any new per-item field must be added to every one of those RPC/copy sites together**.
- **Item tooltip** (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Tooltip.cs`): hover any slot (bag / hotbar / paperdoll / container) for 0.5 s while the menu is open → content-sized popup follows the pointer on the drag layer. Shows name, recipe Type/Description, equipment stats (damage, windup/charge/reach from the item's weapon class row, bow draw, harvest tool, durability, passive drain), food effects from `FoodCatalog`, ammo data, and "Crafted by …" for tagged equipment. `ResourceDefinitionData.Description` is an optional JSON field for raw-resource flavor text.
- **Ticks (host only)**: melee — 1 per swing that connected (hit or blocked), applied in `ServerMeleeAttackRuntime.TrySendCompletion`; bow — 1 per shot fired (`ServerTryFireBow`); build hammer — 1 per place that actually stood (a piece that instantly collapses under structural integrity is free) and 1 per repair (`BuildAuthority`); torch/lantern — passive drain via `EquippedToolDrainAuthority.HostTick` from `CombatAuthority.OnUpdate`, plus per-hit like any melee weapon. **Free swings never cost durability.**
- **Broken (wear ≥ max)**: cannot swing (`CanStartPrimaryAttack` owner-side + `ServerCanBeginMeleeAttackAction` host-side), cannot fire, cannot place/repair structures, and **cannot be equipped** — `PlayerEquipment.TryResolveHotbarEquipResourceId` refuses broken stacks, and since wear changes raise `HotbarChanged`, a tool breaking mid-use auto-unequips (MainHand empties until repaired). Item stays in inventory.
- **Slot visuals** (`ResourceCatalog.ApplyStackVisual`): every durability item shows a thin bottom bar — full tan/yellow when untouched, shrinking with wear, entirely grey when broken — plus a semi-transparent red X overlay while broken. Renders in every grid (bag, hotbar, containers, drag ghost).
- **Arrows** (sheet 2026-11-08): recipe `damage` is the arrow's own pierce damage, added to the bow's (`AmmoCatalog.GetAmmoDamage + weaponDamage`): wood 7, fire 7, flint 10. The fire arrow also carries `burnDamage` 15 over `burnSeconds` 5: on a creature hit (`ArrowProjectile.TryApplyHit`, and Deadshot's instant hits) `BurnEffect.HostApply` puts a host-only `BurnEffect` on the victim that deals the total in one-second `DamageReceiver.TakeDamage` ticks - same number for players, animals and raiders; players also get the HUD-only `onFire` status ("On Fire!", `status_effects.json`, no `healthPerSecond`). A new hit restarts the burn, no stacking.
- **Repair**: free, at the **workbench** (`Workbench` station, `prefabs/build/workbench.prefab`). E opens the crafting page in workbench mode — recipe list filtered by `CraftingRecipe.AppearsAtStation` (explicit `stations` array; default: `requiredStation` when set, else `workbench`) — with a header button repairing the most-damaged tool (hotbar + bag) to full per click via `PlayerCrafting.HostTryRepairMostDamagedTool`.
- **Upgrade tiers** (Mark 2026-11-08: "players aren't limited to just the weapon they first crafted"): weapons, tools and armor carry `InventorySlot.Tier` (1 = as crafted, old stacks with 0 read as I); rules in `EquipmentUpgrade` (`Code/Inventory/EquipmentUpgrade.cs`). Upgradable = any equipment profile with `durabilityMax`, `damage` or `armor` > 0 except the `grapple` / `wingsuit` slots (those never show a numeral). Each tier adds **10 %, rounded, on top of the previous tier, never less than +1** (`ScaleStat`: damage 8 → 9 → 10 → 11 → 12, durability 100 → 110 → 121 → 133 → 146, leather chest armor 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → 7 - the +1 floor is Claude's call so small stats do not round back to themselves); armor pieces grow armor only. `MaxTier` = 5 (I–V) and the cost of one upgrade = the item's crafting recipe once more (`BuildCost`) — both are placeholders Mark has not tuned yet. Read sites take the stack, never just the id: `ToolDurability.GetMax(in slot)` / `ClampWear(in slot, …)`, `PlayerCombat.GetMeleeWeaponBaseDamage` and the bow / deadshot weapon damage scale by `GetActiveWeaponTier()` (the active hotbar stack), `PlayerEquipment.Armor` sums `EquipmentUpgrade.GetArmor(profile, stack.Tier)`. **Workbench UI**: the crafting page grows a **Craft / Upgrade** tab row under the header while a bench is open (always opens on Craft; closing the bench drops back to Craft). Upgrade lists every upgradable stack in the hotbar then the bag (row key `grid:slot`, rows re-listed when ids / tiers change, checked 4×/s); the detail box shows the name with its roman numeral, `Stat: current >> next` rows (next in green, "Max tier" at V) and the recipe cost; the header button reads `Upgrade (LMB)` and sends `PlayerCrafting.OwnerTryUpgradeEquipment(gridId, slot, resourceId)` → host `HostTryUpgradeEquipment` validates once (sheltered bench in reach, upgradable stack below max, materials; `allCrafting` waives bench + cost), consumes the recipe and calls `PlayerHotbar/PlayerInventory.HostSetTier`. Every slot icon (`ResourceCatalog.ApplyStackVisual`) shows the numeral in its top-right corner for upgradable gear (Mark 2026-11-09; the stack count stays bottom-right); the tooltip adds a `Tier N` line and shows damage / armor / durability at the stack's tier.

### Armor (worn paperdoll pieces)

- **Slots**: `Head / Chest / Arms / Hands / Legs / Feet` are the six armor-set slots; `Cloak` (capes) is a seventh worn slot that contributes armor points and weight but not to the set divisor. `EquipmentSlot` is appended-only — indices ride the equipment RPCs.
- **Data** (`equipment_profiles.json`): `statModifiers.armor` (points), `armorWeight` (`light` / `medium` / `heavy`), `armorSet` (set id, e.g. `leather`), `clothing` (citizen `.clothing` path shown on the pawn while worn). Leather set is three pieces — `armor_leather_head` (Cap, 1 armor), `armor_leather_chest` (Chest, 3 — covers arms + hands), `armor_leather_legs` (Pants, 2 — covers feet) — light, crafted from `crafted_leather` (2 / 10 / 6) at the workbench. A set does not have to fill all six slots.
- **Aggregate** lives on `PlayerEquipment.Armor.cs` (`TotalArmor`, `ArmorSetId` / `ArmorSetPieces`, `HasArmorSetBonus`) and is recomputed **once per paperdoll change**, never per hit or per frame. MainHand is excluded (it mirrors the hotbar, it is not worn).
- **Damage**: `PlayerVitals.ApplyDamageAfterArmor` → `ArmorMitigation.Apply(D, A)`: `D ≤ 0 → 0`; `A ≤ 0 → D`; `A < D/2 → D − A`; else `D² / (4A)`. 100 dmg vs 20 → 80, vs 50 → 50, vs 100 → 25; 20 dmg vs 50 → 2. Applies to every physical hit that reaches `ApplyDamageAfterArmor` (melee, arrows, block chip, animals). World hazards that call `VitalsAuthority.TryApplyDeltas` directly (DoT trap, bear-trap catch) bypass armor. Entities keep their own flat `EntityVitals.ArmorFlat`.
- **Drawing an item** (Mark 2026-11-12, `PlayerEquipment.Draw.cs`): anything picked from the hotbar into a hand - weapon, shield, tool, food - takes `PlayerEquipment.DrawSeconds` (**0.2 s** since 2026-11-17, Mark: "make swords equip in .2s without any bar across the bottom? Also ok to equip while running" - every hotbar item; the 1 s draw, the sprint hold-back `IsDrawBlockedBySprint` and the HUD draw bar `PlayerScreenHud.BuildDrawBar` / `DrawProgress01` are deleted). The owner empties that hand at once and puts the item in only when the draw is done, so every attack / block / tool / held-model path waits on its own and the host only ever hears of the finished item. Putting an item away is instant; re-selecting what is already in (or being drawn into) a hand, or a wear / count change, never restarts it. `EquipFromHotbarSlot` (tests, hacks) stays instant. Not host-validated yet (a modified client could skip the wait).
