- **Gear costs no speed** (2026-11-17, Mark: "players should not be slowed down when they equip armor or weapons"): the 2026-11-12 flat penalties (weapon out, shield out, chest armor, feet armor - 10 % each), `ComputeGearSpeedScale` / `GearSpeedScale` and the paperdoll `Speed NN%` readout are deleted. Only aiming a bow (an arrow drawn, or ADS) still slows walk and run, by `BowAimSpeedPenalty` 0.20 (an action, like the melee footwork; `ComputeBowAimSpeedScale`). Ground speeds: walk 4.5 m/s, run 5.4 m/s (walk +20 %, 2026-11-17), the `Walk` action (Caps Lock) toggles a slow walk at `WalkToggleMetersPerSecond` 1.6. Carry and tow still multiply.
- **Crouch** (Mark 2026-11-12): engine 26.11.10 gave `PlayerController` a grounded duck delay (`DuckDuration` / `UnduckDuration`, the controller only ducks on the ground after the key is held that long; in the air it is instant). basicplayer.prefab keeps both at **0** - with the defaults (0.4 / 0.2) the pawn could only crouch airborne. The `sneak` console hack now holds the crouch key from `PlayerMovement.PreInput`, so it ducks the controller too.
- **Sets**: `ArmorSetPieceThreshold` (default **3**) on `PlayerEquipment` — reaching it flips `HasArmorSetBonus` and fires `armor_set_equipped` (owner-local, match = set id, rising edge). No bonus effects exist yet; pieces 4–6 are reserved for minor per-piece extras so players can leave augments visible without feeling incomplete.
- **Visuals**: host publishes `PlayerEquipment.NetworkedWornClothing` (`|`-joined clothing paths); `PlayerAnimation.WornClothing.cs` dresses the body via `ClothingContainer.Apply` on every peer and marks the created children `NetworkMode.Never`.
- **UI**: tooltip shows Armor / Weight / Set lines; the paperdoll has a fixed-height `Armor N · Speed NN%` readout under the grid.

### Shelter, comfort, status effects

- **`ShelterProbe`** (`Code/Building/ShelterProbe.cs`) is the global "is there a player-built roof over this spot / is it walled in" answer. Traces filter on the `buildpiece` tag (prefab tag, re-asserted by `BuildPiece`), so caves, terrain and dug holes never count. Roof = structural Roof **or Floor** family above (upper storeys are ceilings); wall = any structural family except Beam. Callers cache: pawns re-probe on `PlayerVitals` **Comfort - Comfort check interval (s)**, static stations on `BuildPiece.WorldVersion` (bumps when a placed piece enters / leaves the world on any machine). Never per frame.
- **Comfort** (`PlayerVitals.Comfort.cs`, host-probed, `[Sync] ComfortLevel` / `ComfortSources`): ticks add up — +1 under a roof, +1 near a lit campfire (`Campfire` **Warmth range (m)**), +1 enclosed (all 8 horizontal probes meet a wall, **Wall hits for enclosure**) **only under a roof**. Fire alone 1, roof + fire 2 (open-concept), roof + walls 2, roof + walls + fire 3. Walls without a roof give nothing. Effects end by time, and systems drop them by event through `HostRemoveStatusEffect` (potions, cures). Each tick banks **Rested minutes per comfort tick** (default 2) on the `rested` effect. The timer **holds at full while comfort is active**; when comfort drops (to any lower level) it counts down from the banked value (10→4 ticks starts at 19:59), and a lower level only holds it once it has fallen to that level's value. Host re-times the expiry only past **Rested top-up slack (s)**; the HUD renders the held value exactly from `ComfortLevel`.
- **Stamina regen** (Mark 2026-11-14 melee spec; host `VitalsAuthority`): `StaminaRegenIdlePerSecond` 20/s standing, `StaminaRegenMovingPerSecond` 15/s moving, after the drain's delay — combat drains pass their own (`StaminaRegenDelayAfterAttackSeconds` 0.5, `StaminaRegenDelayAfterBlockSeconds` 0.3, `TryApplyDeltas( regenDelaySeconds )`), everything else waits `StaminaRegenDelaySeconds` 1 s. **Exhaustion**: an empty bar sets `PlayerVitals.IsExhausted` until it refills to `ExhaustionRecoverStamina` (20) — no swing, feint or guard meanwhile; movement stays free. Costs: light 10, heavy 20, block 8, feint 5, punch (`ShoveStaminaCost`) 15.
- **Status effects** (`PlayerVitals.StatusEffects.cs`, catalog `Assets/data/status_effects.json` via `StatusEffectCatalog`): timed buffs / debuffs whose modifiers are stat changes — `maxHealthPercent`, `maxStaminaPercent`, `foodDurationPercent`, `healthPerSecond`, `staminaPerSecond`. Host list mirrored through one `[Sync]` string (rewritten only on add / remove / re-time). Pool caps are assembled in **one** place, `PlayerVitals.HostRecalculatePoolMaxes()` = (base + food bonus) × status multipliers; `PlayerFood` no longer sets caps itself, and while rested it pushes the food-duration bonus onto slot expiries in 5 s chunks. Per-second modifiers batch at 0.25 before an authority delta; negative HP goes through `VitalsAuthority.TryApplyDeltas` so death / logging behave like a hit. Death clears every effect (comfort re-applies rested within a check). Console: `status <id> <seconds>`, `status <id> 0`, `status clear` (`GameHacks`).
- **HUD** (`StatusEffectsHud`, built by `PlayerScreenHud.BuildVitals`): fixed-height icon strip **above** the health / stamina bars (bars never move), mm:ss timer per icon, buff = green frame, debuff = red. While any menu is open, the soft cursor over an icon shows a tooltip (name · timer, description, modifier lines, comfort sources). Icons live in `Assets/ui/status/`.
- **Drying rack** (`DryingRack`, `prefabs/build/drying_rack.prefab`, build cost 10 rope + 5 bone glue + 10 wood; workbench costs 10 wood + 10 plant fiber; Mark 2026-11-08): **no menu**. E on a built rack (owner intent → `RpcHostUseDryingRack`, host checks **Use range (m)** 4 + 1 m slack once) first collects every finished output into the inventory, then pulls one applicable item per free slot straight from the inventory - 3 slots, one item each. Each slot finishes after its process's game-clock hours from `Assets/data/drying_rack.json` (`DryingRackCatalog`: `resource_hide` → 2 `crafted_leather`, 6 h; jerky from meat, 12 h, goes there when a jerky item exists). Hours = `EnvironmentDayNightCycle.TryGetGameHours` ((DayNumber − 1) × 24 + cycle fraction × 24); a scene without a cycle counts 60 real seconds per hour. Slot contents are host-only, `[Sync] DryingCount / ReadyCount` drive the HUD prompt (`DryingRack.PromptText`: Load / Collect (n ready) / full). Not persisted. The leather recipe keeps `stations: ["drying_rack"]` so it lists in no menu (it only defines the item); `CraftingStations` (campfire proximity recipes) does not know the rack. The other six temp stations (furnace, refinery, cauldron, fabricator, chemistry station, augment station) have no recipes and **cost nothing** for now - Mark: "can be worked on later ... just make them free for now"; the anvil keeps a 10-wood placeholder (not in the sheet).
- **Workbench needs a roof**: `Workbench.IsSheltered` (cached on `BuildPiece.WorldVersion`). Exposed bench → prompt reads "Workbench needs a roof", E does nothing, and an open bench closes when its roof is torn down. Host commit check: `PlayerCrafting.HostTryCraft` requires a sheltered bench within **Use range (m)** for bench recipes (not hand-craftable, not campfire-gated); `allCrafting` waives it.

### Doors (swinging leaf on the wall slot)

- **`BuildDoor`** (`Code/Building/BuildDoor.cs`, on `Assets/prefabs/build/build_wood_door.prefab`): the frame (jambs + header) is the piece's static solid — a `ModelCollider` from the frame vmdl (`BuildPieceVisual.UsesMeshCollision` lists doors), so the doorway is a real hole. The old full-module `BoxCollider` filled the doorway in; never put one back. The leaf is authored on the prefab as `DoorHinge` → `DoorLeaf` (leaf vmdl + `BoxCollider`, tag `doorleaf`); `BuildDoor` lays it out once from the model bounds (hinge at one jamb, equal side gaps, sill gap from **`BuildModuleDimensions.DoorOpeningHeightMeters`**) and only ever yaws the hinge.
- **Swing direction is chosen per open**: the host reads the user's position in door-local space and yaws the leaf toward the *other* face (`SwingYawSign`, `[Sync]`). There is no fixed hand — that is what made earlier doors open into the player. `IsOpen` is `[Sync]`; every machine animates the hinge from the synced state (**Door - Swing speed (degrees/s)**, **Door - Open angle (degrees)**), so proxies never fight a networked transform.
- **Leaf collider is keyframed, never `Static`**: a Static body moved every frame does not follow the swing. `BuildNavMeshSync.MarkSolidCollidersStaticInBounds` skips `doorleaf` objects; nav already includes keyframed bodies. Each toggle schedules `BuildNavMeshSync.OnBuildPieceChanged` — closed leaf blocks the mesh (an entity with no route breaches the door like any wood piece; `DamageReceiver` on the root resolves leaf hits through the hierarchy), open leaf lets the path run through.
- **Use**: `PlayerInventoryInteraction.Door.cs` — look + E (`Use`) within **Door - Use reach (m)**, owner trace → host commit (`HostToggle`); a client toggle goes through `RpcHostToggleDoor`, and the host re-checks reach once (`IsWithinUseReach`, reach + one module of slack). HUD prompt reads "Open Door" / "Close Door"; chest / station / workbench focus wins over it. `ShelterProbe` still counts a closed door as a wall (the leaf hit walks up to the `BuildPiece`); an open doorway is a gap, so the room is not enclosed on that ray.

### Foot traps (bear traps)

- **`BearTrap`** (`Code/Environment/BearTrap.cs`) on the hammer-placed pieces `trap_small` ("Foot Trap") and `trap_large` ("Bear Trap") in `data/build_pieces.json` (`Assets/prefabs/build/trap_*.prefab`, sizes in `BuildModuleDimensions`; `BuildPieceNavPolicy` treats `trap_*` as walkable so the plate never carves the nav mesh). The prefab is a thin solid base plus a `Trigger` child (`BoxCollider`, Is Trigger). Armed, the host polls the trigger's `Touching` list at 10 Hz — no per-frame work, no listeners on child colliders.
- **Who it holds**: `TrapSize` (`None` / `Small` / `Large`) per creature, and each trap holds the band **Trap - Smallest creature it holds** … **Trap - Largest creature it holds**. Players are `Small`. Animals read `trapSize` from `data/animal_behaviors.json` (birds / fish `none`, dunewyrm `large`, the rest `small`; a missing key means small). Enemies come from `EntityArchetype` (`Boneback` large, scav / howler small). Small trap = small only; large trap = small **and** large by default (narrow it on the prefab).
- **The hold lives on the movement owner**, never on the trap: `PlayerMovement.HostSetTrapped` (`[Sync] TrapLocked` — no move / jump / roll / grapple, look and swing still work; walk + run are muted like the wingsuit so WASD cannot creep) and `EntityLocomotion.HostSetTrapped` (`IsTrapped` — both brains stop the agent and return early, the body is pulled to the trap centre on catch and held there each fixed step; players are pulled the same way on the owner (XY only, camera untouched); a trapped scav still swings at a target in reach). Players hold **Trap - Player hold (seconds)** (5), animals / enemies **Trap - Animal / enemy hold (seconds)** (10); the pawn also gets the `trapped` debuff for the HUD timer. Death, respawn, or being moved more than 2.5 m releases early.
- **Lifecycle**: **built disarmed** → E to arm → catch → release leaves it **disarmed** again. Look + E (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Trap.cs`, owner trace → `HostUse`, client via `RpcHostUseTrap` with a one-time host reach check): "Arm Trap" / "Disarm Trap" / "Open Trap" (frees the victim early; the victim cannot open it themselves). Whoever arms it — and anyone already standing on the plate — is ignored until they step off. **Trap - Damage on catch** (default 0) bites through `VitalsAuthority` / `EntityVitals` when set.

### Circuits (wire stripper, nodes, lever → light)

- **Data**: a build piece joins a circuit only when `data/build_pieces.json` says `"circuitEnabled": true` **and** its prefab carries a `CircuitNode` (`Code/Circuits/CircuitNode.cs`: **Circuit - Kind (sphere colour)**, **Circuit - Sphere offset (units)**) plus one `ICircuitDevice` component (`CircuitLever`, `CircuitLight`, …). First pieces: `circuit_light` ("Light Bulb", 0.3 m cube, `CircuitLight`) and `circuit_lever` ("Lever", 0.2 × 0.2 × 0.3 m, `CircuitLever`); sizes in `BuildModuleDimensions`, `BuildPieceNavPolicy` keeps every `circuit_*` piece off the nav mesh. Gates will be 0.15 m cubes.
- **Wires are invisible and two-way**: a wire is stored on one of its ends (the source end when there is one) as one `[Sync]` string (`CircuitLink.Encode`, `guid:cable;…`), so every machine draws the same graph and late joiners get it for free, but power travels both directions along it — direction of the drag never matters. Cable colour (red / blue / green, `CircuitCable`) is a label only — every wire carries the same signal.
- **Solve** (`CircuitRegistry`, host, event-driven): the wire graph is undirected; each connected group of nodes is **one circuit**, live when **any** source in it is on (two levers on one circuit = OR; it is only off when every lever is off). Every node's `Powered` is its circuit's state; its device turns that into `Output` (`ICircuitDevice.ComputeOutput`: lever returns `IsOn`, light mirrors `Powered`). Anything that changes the graph calls `MarkDirty`; the next host frame floods each circuit once. No per-frame work while quiet. Both `Powered` / `Output` are `[Sync]`; devices read them for visuals on every machine.
- **Wire stripper** (`tool_wireStripper`, `EquippedItemActions.Wire`, `Code/Player/ToolWireStripper.cs`, prefab `prefabs/tools/wire_stripper_tool.prefab`): with it out, `CircuitWireOverlay` draws a 0.3 m sphere (`CircuitNode.SphereDiameterMeters`) in each circuit-enabled object within **Wiring - Show range (m)** (kind colour: switch yellow, light blue, door teal, trap red, gate lime, sensor purple, power white), 10% bigger under the crosshair, plus a line per wire in its cable colour. Hold Attack1 on a sphere, walk, release on another → `PlayerInventoryInteraction.Circuits.cs` sends intent, host validates once in `CircuitRegistry.HostToggleLink` (both circuit-enabled, not self, under 50 m, pawn within **Wiring - Reach (m)** of the released sphere) and lays the wire — or removes it if that pair was already wired. A wire dragged from a sink to a source (bulb → lever) is flipped onto the source, so drag direction only matters between two non-sources. Sphere picking is a ray-to-centre test against cached positions, no trace. Attack2 opens the cable colour menu (`Code/UI/Circuits/WireCableMenuHud.cs`, same overlay pattern as the build menu).
- **Lever**: look + E (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Lever.cs`, owner trace → `HostToggle`, client via `RpcHostToggleLever` with a one-time host reach check); prompt "Switch On" / "Switch Off". **Light**: `CircuitLight` tints the bulb and switches a `PointLight` it builds once from **Light - Light color** / **Light - Light radius (m)**.
- Not yet: generator / power model, gates, timers, sensors, buttons, pressure plates, doors and traps as sinks — pending Mark's answers on colour meaning, power model, pins, and hand-opening a wired door.

**Spheres and wires (2026-11-02).** Node spheres use `UnlitVfxGeometry.TintMaterial` (`shaders/vfx_unlit_tint.shader`: renderer tint, unlit) so they read at full brightness in any light. Wires are drawn by `CircuitWireVfx` (world strips in the cable colour) instead of DebugOverlay lines; a link that appears while the stripper is out plays a 0.7 s connect pulse - both spheres pop to 1.6× and whiten, a white spark runs source → target down a glowing wire, then the plain coloured line stays (`CircuitWireOverlay` diffs each node's link set against what it has seen; the first sighting of a node never pulses).

### Shields (offhand, left forearm)

- **Items**: `shield_wood` (Wooden Shield, hand recipe — 1 wood for now like the club), plus `shield_scrap`, `shield_tower`, `shield_reforged`, `buckler_reforged` from Mark's item sheet (items + profiles, no recipes yet). Profiles in `equipment_profiles.json`: `slot: offHand`, `actions: ["Block"]`, `hotbarEquipable: false`, `heldModel`, `durabilityMax` 100, armor 0 — a shield is paperdoll storage (drag onto the **Off** slot), never a hotbar item. Only the wooden shield has a model; the other four reuse `models/weapons/shield_wood.vmdl` as a stand-in. No block bonus yet: block outcomes still come from `melee_block_stagger.json` alone, and worn slots take no wear.
- **Model** (`Blender/scripts/create_shield_wood.py` → `models/weapons/shield_wood.vmdl`, `materials/weapons/shield_wood.png/.vmat`, 256 px pixel_lit, 0.73 m across): seven flush planks of varied width with painted seams, iron rim + rivets, domed boss, grip bar + leather forearm strap behind. **Convention for every shield model**: engine +Z = outward face, +X = up the forearm toward the elbow, origin ON the forearm centre line (0.045 m behind the back plane) — the code puts the origin on the arm, so there are no per-item angles. Blender +X lands on engine +Y, hence the −90° export yaw in the script.
- **Presentation lives on `PlayerAnimation`** (`PlayerAnimation.Shield.cs`, commandment #1): `OnPreRender` after the sword and torch. Every peer reads the offhand id it can see (local slots on host / owner, `PlayerEquipment.NetworkedOffHandResourceId` Sync on other peers — same rule as the main hand) and builds a `NetworkMode.Never` prop. **Rest**: strapped to the LEFT forearm — up = `hand_L` → `arm_lower_L`, face = body-left made orthogonal to the forearm, centre at **Shield - Rest: along forearm** (0.5). **Guard up** (`PlayerCombat.IsGuardShown` = owner's held block or the host-confirmed block): the shield glides (**Shield - Raise / lower time** 0.12 s, smoothstep) to the body centre — **Shield - Block: height** 0.62 × body height, **Shield - Block: forward** 0.42 m along the block basis yaw, **Shield - Block: tilt back** 8° — the same frame the green block guard shell is drawn in, so the shield sits inside the guard effect; the forearm axis goes horizontal (elbow out left, hand toward the middle) and the centre sits **Shield - Block: left offset** (0.08 m) toward the shield arm so the hand stays within the arm's 0.5 m reach. **Arm follows** (Mark 2026-11-02): the hand's placement against the strap is sampled at rest and carried along; `SolveShieldArm` is an analytic two-bone IK (upper / forearm swing by the minimal rotation from their animated direction, elbow toward **Shield - Elbow pole**, hand takes the shield-relative rotation, fingers follow) written as bone overrides. Rules learned the hard way: the sword graph declares `ik.hand_left.*` parameters but has NO node reading them (`SetIk` is a no-op); `SetBoneTransform` takes RENDERER-LOCAL transforms (a world-space write lands root-offset away); read the chain with `TryGetBoneTransformAnimation` (world, pre-override) - the plain getters return last frame's override and the arm compounds off into space; children follow an overridden parent on their own; `SceneModel.ClearBoneOverrides()` is the only clear. **Block stance for the sword** (`PlayerAnimation.MeleeSwing.cs`): while the guard is up `ResolveSwordPose` targets the left-shoulder rest (`IsGuardStanceShown`), presentation only - the block guard direction and the attack cursor are untouched. Hidden during the hit reaction, ledge mantle, seated, wingsuit and while a `twoHanded` main hand (bow) is out — the slot keeps the item (`ApplySlotLocal` no longer empties OffHand on a two-handed equip; that line used to delete the item).
- **Shield blocks alone** (Mark 2026-11-02): the owner input gate in `PlayerCombat.OnUpdate` opens for a `PrimaryMelee` main hand OR an OffHand item with the `Block` action (`HasOffhandBlockItem` → `PlayerEquipment.OffHandHasAction`); only the swing path (click-fire, queued click, direction lock, cocked snap) stays behind `hasMeleeWeapon`. The block channel, guard direction, state machine, block viz and the shield raise all run bare-handed or with a tool out. Host block resolution never checked the item, so nothing changed there. The crosshair teardrop shows with a shield alone only while the guard is up.
- **No thrust weapons** (2026-11-14): the spear swings left / right / overhead like the rest (its narrow 34° lateral arc and long `reachLateralMeters` stay).
- **Bola** (`PlayerCombat.Bola.cs`, `BolaProjectile`): stacks to 10 (`crafting_recipes.json` maxStack); holding Attack2 spins it for as long as it is held (no auto-release), a release after `BolaMinChargeSeconds` throws 4-12 m by charge; drawn 2x in flight; roots a `TrapSize.Small` victim only when it lands on the legs - the lower 45 % of the victim's bounds height (at least 0.6 m), read once at the hit.
- **Hotbar item** (Mark 2026-11-02): shields are `hotbarEquipable` and live on the hotbar like weapons; selecting one makes it the OffHand selection (`PlayerHotbar.OffHandSlotIndex`) while the primary stays, so spear + shield or sword + shield is two clicks and "no shield" is a hotbar without one. The paperdoll **Off** square (and **Active**) mirror the hotbar - never storage (`PlayerEquipment.IsHotbarDrivenSlot`). `EquipmentCatalog.IsHotbarOffHandItem` / `IsHotbarHandItem` are the tests.
- **Dev**: `equip <id>` handles both hands (a shield lands in slot 2 so slot 1 keeps the weapon); `block [seconds]` holds Attack2 like `swing` holds Attack1 (`GameHacks.ApplySimulatedInput`).

### Dev tools page, static 3-hit combo + Q special (Mark 2026-11-06)

- **Dev tools page** (Settings → Dev tools, `DevToolsSettings`, saved to `dev_tools_settings.json` in `FileSystem.Data`): **per player** — *Static 3-hit combo* (`StaticMeleeCombo`, each client its own; console `melee_combo_static 0|1`). **Host-wide** (Mark 2026-11-06; only the host or offline may change them, clients see them greyed with "host only"; the host's last choice is saved and republished on the next session) — *Stamina drain* (`InfiniteStamina`, default infinite: `PlayerVitals.InfiniteStamina` replaces the old per-pawn `InfiniteStaminaDebug` prefab flag and terrainTest's `InfiniteStaminaOnPlayable`; console `stamina_infinite 0|1`) and *Lock daytime* (`DayLocked`: every peer's `EnvironmentDayNightCycle` jumps to noon and holds; console `day_lock 0|1`). They reach clients through `WorldNetworkSession`'s `[Sync(FromHost)]` `DevInfiniteStamina` / `DevDayLocked` (testscene1's NetworkManager); a scene without that component runs them locally. New flags: a property on `DevToolsSettings`, a button row in `GameSettingsMenuSection`.
- **Combo** (`PlayerCombat.StaticCombo.cs`, owner): the mouse no longer picks the attack. Quick clicks go **right → left → overhead** (`StaticComboSwingDir`: `SwingDirs.Right` / `Left` / `Up` — crosshair arrows left, right, up); two quick clicks are right → left, one is always right. "Quick" = the click lands while the previous swing is still going (it queues, one follow-up as in free aim) or within `ComboChainWindowSeconds` (0.6 s) after it ended — hit or miss. The crosshair attack arrow is hidden in this mode (`ShowsAttackTeardrop`) unless the guard is held; block keeps its mouse direction.
- **Heavy**: a long hold turns that step heavy (normal heavy rules) and **ends the combo**; light, light, heavy = a heavy overhead. After the overhead or any heavy the next swing waits `ComboEndPauseSeconds` (0.2 s) once the chain is free (a click in the pause waits in the queue). Raising the guard resets the combo.
- **Damage**: the overhead as the third quick swing hits for `ComboFinisherDamageMultiplier` (×1.25; ×2.5 when held heavy). The owner sends `AttackReleaseIntent.ComboStep`; the host recounts on its own clock (`HostResolveSwingDamageScale`, window + 0.3 s latency slack) and only pays the bonus when it also saw the two quick swings before — a client claiming step 3 out of nowhere gets plain damage. The scale rides on the swing runtime (`GetMeleeDamage( isHeavy, scale )`).
- **Q special** (`SpecialAttack` input on Q, static mode only, on the ground, not grappling): the weapon class `specialAttack` in `melee_weapon_classes.json` — `"stab"` on `oneHanded` (sword, club; axe / torch / lantern share the class): `MeleeAttackTypes.Stab` with its own `specialWindupSeconds` (1.2 s) and `specialDamageMultiplier` (×2 light), never heavy, light stamina; it starts the next click at the right attack. Host: `CombatAuthority` rejects a special the class lacks or off the ground (`PlayerMovement.HostSeesStandingOnGround`, the roll's lenient check) with `RejectSpecialNotAllowed`. Dev: `special_attack` presses Q once.
- **Tests**: `combat_test` cases 1c / 1d (combo finisher bonus, a lone step 3 gets none, light-light-heavy, special stab windup + damage).

### Bow (archer stance, arrow read, Mark 2026-11-06)

- **Input / host** (`PlayerCombat.Ranged.cs`): hold Attack1 to draw (`BowFullDrawSeconds`), release to loose; right-click while drawing cancels. **The draw is two phases** (Mark 2026-11-18: "first half of the windup to be power (range/damage) and the 2nd part can be accuracy"), split at **Combat — Bow - Power phase share of the draw (0-1)** (`BowPowerPhaseFraction`, 0.5; the full draw time is unchanged): *power* (0 .. split) takes launch speed (`BowMinDrawPower` → 1) and the bow's damage share (`BowMinWeaponDamageContribution` → weapon damage) from min to max while the aim cone stays at `BowMaxAimConePixels`; *accuracy* (split .. 1) holds full power and narrows the cone to the plain crosshair. Two helpers are the only mapping: `PlayerCombat.BowPowerFraction( draw01 )` and `BowAccuracyFraction( draw01 )` - `ComputeBowLaunchVelocity` (host arrow and the client's predicted twin), the host damage in `ServerTryFireBow`, `ApplyBowInaccuracy` (the owner's spread roll, cone size from `BowAimConeRadiusPixels`) and the crosshair all read them; the intent still carries the raw draw (`BowFireIntent.Charge01`, clamped 0-1 by the host), never a power or accuracy number. **Crosshair** (`PlayerCrosshair`, bow ring): through the power phase the wide white ring fills yellow from the bottom up (`RingFillUi`, a clipped copy of the ring, panels only); once full the whole ring is yellow and closes in over the accuracy phase. The draw pose (`PresentationBowDraw01`) still follows the whole draw. The owner publishes the draw (`[Sync] BowDrawSynced`, twentieths) and `[Sync] BowShotCounter` (+1 per loose, not per cancel) so every peer poses it. **The arrow leaves the bow** (`PlayerAnimation.TryGetBowNockWorld`) and flies toward whatever is under the crosshair (camera ray, 120 m; nothing there = its far point) - it no longer spawns at the camera. The host replaces an origin more than a body height from the chest with the hand estimate (the one anti-cheat check on that path).
- **Stance** (`PlayerAnimation.Bow.cs`, commandment #1 - the archer stance yaw overrides the body tracker's in `PlayerAnimation.TickCombatFacingPresentation`): while drawing, the Body child turns **Bow - Stance: turn right of aim** (90°) right of the aim (root / camera / EyeAngles never touched), the look limits keep the chest square to it (`BowStanceWeight` scales `aim_body`) while head and eyes turn to the target. Both arms are bone overrides from the shared `SolveTwoBoneArm` (the shield's solve, now returning the solved hand): `SolveArmForGrip` lands the GRIP bone (`hold_*`) on its target with a second pass for the offset that turns with the hand. Left grip = anchor + aim × **Draw: length** (0.64 m); the string hand runs from the bow to the anchor (head bone + **Draw: anchor from head**) with the draw and flicks back **Release: hand flies back** for **Release: follow-through** after a loose. `CurlFingers` closes the bow hand (**Hands: bow grip curl**) and hooks the string fingers (**Hands: string hook curl**) about the knuckle line. Blend in / out **Stance: blend** (0.12 s). Hidden in hit reaction, mantle, dodge roll, seated and wingsuit.
- **Props**: the profile `heldModel` is `simple_bow_held` (Tools/item_models: simple_bow's limbs without the string, same seed). Engine axes of the generated bows: +Y tip to tip, +Z the belly (`BowRotation`). The string is two thin cylinders tip → nock → tip, straight at rest. The nocked arrow is the preferred ammo's own item model (`ArrowProjectile.AttachArrowVisual`). At rest the bow hangs upright in the left hand.
- **Arrow look**: flying, stuck and nocked arrows are the ammo's item model (`item_world_models.json`, length along model +Y) on an `arrow_visual` child, the root unscaled (the old brown scaled cylinder is only the fallback). Loose arrows get a shaft-sized box from `ShaftLengthMeters` / `ShaftThicknessMeters`.
- **Where it went** (`ArrowFlightTrail`, every peer, local object): a semi-transparent white streak trails the arrow and fades along its length (`StreakSeconds` 0.15 - a comet tail, not a static line; Mark: "should sorta fade out as it flies"); on landing a small white star pops and fades (`StarSeconds` 0.4, drawn like the sword's heavy-ready glint star). Both hold a constant screen size. The host syncs `ShooterId`, `ImpactKind` and `ImpactPosition`, and keeps the spent arrow hidden 0.3 s so the landing reaches clients. A creature or player hit gives the shooter's crosshair a red X tick (`PlayerCrosshair`, `PlayerCombat.ArrowHitConfirm01`, 0.3 s). No aim-arc preview - that is the Trajectory Guide augment's job.

### Hotbar banks, hand selection and the key map (2026-11-02)

- **Two banks** (`PlayerHotbar`: `BankSize` 10 × `BankCount` 2 = `SlotCount` 20, a flat slot array so every pickup / craft / drag path is unchanged): **Alt** (`HotbarBank` action) toggles `ActiveBank`; the HUD (`HotbarHud`) shows the bank on screen with **two dots** to its left (lit dot = the bank up; top dot bank 1, bottom bank 0) and, while the **Inventory / equipment page** is open (`MenuPanelFlags.Inventory`), **both banks stacked** (`SetShowBothBanks`, bank 1 above bank 0). `PlayerHotbarBindingsStore` pads older 10-entry binding files.
- **Selection** (`PlayerHotbarController`): number row **1-0** = slot of the bank on screen (`SelectBankSlot`), wheel and mouse4 / mouse5 step the PRIMARY within that bank (`StepActiveSlot`) and pass over shield slots - the wheel never touches the offhand, a shield is equipped only by its own key / click (Mark 2026-11-02: scrolling across sword + shield just flashes the sword) - clicks as before; keys work with the menu open (not while typing a pin name). `ActiveSlotIndex` is the highlight / "selected on the hotbar" item (seeds, food). `SetActiveSlot` classifies the pick: a **shield** sets `OffHandSlotIndex`, anything else (weapon, tool, seed, empty) sets `PrimarySlotIndex`; `PlayerEquipment.SyncEquipFromActiveHotbar` mirrors MainHand from the primary index and OffHand from the offhand index, re-validating the slot contents (moved / broken / wrong kind → that hand empties). Both indices ride the owner hotbar sync RPC.
- **Highlights**: the gold square sits on the two SELECTED slots (`PrimarySlotIndex`, `OffHandSlotIndex`) — no separate equipped squares (Mark: "it should just show the 2 highlighted items"). A number key or click on a slot that is already selected **unequips** it (`PlayerHotbar.SelectOrToggleSlot`: that hand index → -1, `ActiveSlotIndex` -1 when it was the selected slot) and the square goes dark; wheel / mouse4-5 steps never toggle. `SetActiveSlot` stays select-only for tests and hacks.
- **Key map after the move**: 1-0 hotbar · Alt bank · Z X C V G T augments · hold Tab augment wheel, tap Tab inventory · B dev supply (`DevSupply`; H is SpawnBoss) · Voice (V), Drop (G) and Walk (Alt) actions unbound (nothing read them; the controller's alt move is `run`).
### Item authority (the host cursor, 2026-11-10)

- **Rule:** an item only reaches a slot if the host saw it leave another one. A remote client's drag-and-drop RPCs carry **slot indices only** - never a resource id, count, wear, maker or tier. The item in transit is the **host cursor**, one `InventoryCursorStack` per pawn on `PlayerInventory` (`Code/Inventory/PlayerInventory.HostCursor.cs`, `HostCursor`): a take credits it (`HostCursorMakeRoomFor` + `HostCursorCredit`), a place spends it by passing `ref HostCursor` to the same `HostTry*` / stack rules the host's own pawn uses. The client still predicts with its local cursor on `PlayerInventoryInteraction`; when the two disagree the host wins and re-sends the real slots (`PushSlotsToOwner`, chest `PushContentsToPeers`, `PushEquipmentToOwner`, augments `PushFullStateToOwner`). The host's own pawn and offline play never touch the host cursor.
- **Every grid goes through it:** bag and hotbar (`HostCursorPickupAll / TakeOne / TakeHalf / PlaceHeld / FinishDragDrop / DropOne / PlaceHalf`), a chest (`ContainerInventory.HostCursor*( cursor, … )` - the cursor is the **calling** player's, found with `PlayerInventory.FindForConnection( Scene, Rpc.Caller )`), the paperdoll (`PlayerEquipment.HostCursorPickupFromSlot / HostCursorPlaceIntoSlot / HostSwapSlots`), the augment bank and sockets (`PlayerAugments.HostCursorPickupBank / PlaceHeldBank / PickupInstalled / PlaceIntoInstalled`, `HostSwapBank / HostSwapInstalled`), and world drops (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.HostCursorDropAtPlayer / HostCursorDropInWorld`). Each `[Rpc.Host]` is two lines: the caller must own the pawn, then the matching `HostCursor*` method - the tests call those methods directly. There is no "set this slot to X" RPC any more (`RpcHostSetEquipmentSlot`, `RpcHostSetBank`, `RpcHostSetInstalled` and the stack arguments on every place RPC are gone).
- **Hands:** MainHand / OffHand mirror the hotbar. The owner sends only the item id (`RpcHostSetHandSlot`); `HostSetHandFromHotbar` equips the host hotbar's own copy (wear, maker, tier) and refuses an item the hotbar does not hold. A hand slot is never a source of items.
- **Leftovers:** menu close and quick-move leftovers send `RpcHostSettleCursor` - the host cursor goes back to the bag and what does not fit drops at the pawn's feet (the client only predicts and clears its cursor; it never spawns the drop). A cursor stack the client no longer shows is settled the same way before a different item is taken, and before the death loot tally (`DeathLootBag.HostSpawnForDeath`). The dev supply's surplus is thrown away with `OwnerDiscardCursor`.
- **Stack rule added:** `InventoryStackRules.SwapDragToSlot` refuses when the source slot is not empty (the displaced stack would overwrite it). **Paperdoll / socket rule tightened:** placing from a stack of two or more onto an occupied slot is refused up front (it used to equip one and delete the piece already there).
- **Grapple:** `ServerTryAttach` no longer accepts a hook the client reports (`HostAcceptClientGrappleEquip` is gone); the hook must be in the host's Grapple slot. `HostEquipGrappleDirect` / `HostEquipWingsuitDirect` are host decisions only (terrain-test kit spawn).
- **Not covered:** a chest RPC does not check the caller's distance to the chest (the client closes the view past **Container - Keep-Open Range (meters)**, the host does not re-check), and no item state is saved between sessions.
- **Shift-click out of a chest / storage fills the hotbar first** (2026-11-17, Mark: an item with an assigned hotbar slot "just gets put in the 0th slot of my inventory ... make shift clicking from chests and other storages check the hotbar assignments and fill them"): `PlayerInventoryInteraction.TryQuickMoveToExternalStorage` → `TryCrossGridQuickMove(..., hotbarFirst)` places the taken stack on `PlayerHotbar.SlotsTakingFromStorage` (the slots a pickup fills: hotbar stacks of it with room, then the empty slots bound to it) before the bag. Each place still goes through the host cursor.
- **Test:** `inventory_test` (`Code/Inventory/Tests/InventoryTest.cs`, gate `.claude/hooks/inventory_test_gate.py`).
- **Scripted pawns and hotbar bindings:** a `NetworkMode.Never` clone counts as a local pawn, so its `PlayerHotbar` would load from and overwrite the player's saved `hotbar_bindings.json`. Every suite sets `PlayerHotbar.UsesSavedBindings = false` on its clones right after the clone (`combat_test`, `anim_test`, `inventory_test`); a new suite must do the same.

### Carry weight (Mark 2026-11-10, prompt 23)

- **Data**: `weight` on a `resources.json` row or an `equipment_profiles.json` profile, in 0.1 steps. Unset resolves in `InventoryWeight.UnitWeight`: anything with an equipment profile or a single-stack resource row (gear) = **5**, stackable goods = **0.1**. Explicit today: wood 1, hardwood 1, stone 2. Tools, weapons and armor all sit on the gear default.
- **Total** (`InventoryWeight.TotalCarried`): inventory grid + both hotbar banks + worn paperdoll (MainHand / OffHand skipped — they mirror hotbar stacks), rounded to 0.1. Cached on `PlayerMovement.CarryWeight` (`PlayerMovement.CarryWeight.cs`) and recomputed only from `InventoryChanged` / `HotbarChanged` / `EquipmentChanged` — never per frame.
- **No speed cost** (2026-11-16, Mark: "Can you stop the whole item weight reduces speed thing?"): weight is a readout only. The Carry Weight group (`Max Carry Weight`, free fraction, full-load / overweight / grapple scales), `IsOverweight`, `CarryWeightSpeedScale`, `CarryWeightGrappleScale()` and `ComputeLocomotionSpeedScale()` are deleted; the paperdoll `Speed NN%` readout went with the gear penalties on 2026-11-17.
- **UI**: the Inventory menu shows `Weight 37.5` under its title (fixed height). Pickups are not blocked by weight and nothing is slowed by it.

### Grapple gear: gauntlet, rope, arm (Mark 2026-11-19)

- **Owner**: `PlayerAnimation.Grapple.cs` (Grapple Gear group), last tick of `OnPreRender`. Mark: "the grapple just kinda appears out of nowhere but it should be connected to the players left arm ... some model that wraps around the players arm ... the players arm should move to follow it". The debug-overlay rope (`DrawRopeIfNeeded`, `DrawDebugRope`) is gone.
- **Who has one**: `[Sync(FromHost)] PlayerMovement.GrappleGearResourceId` (host reads the Grapple slot / event loan four times a second, writes on change) — peers cannot read slots. The profile's `heldModel` is the gauntlet (`basic_hook` → `models/tools/grapple_gear/grapple_gauntlet.vmdl`). Hidden only with the wingsuit out.
- **Gauntlet**: local `NetworkMode.Never` prop on the left forearm, rest frame from the animation pose like the shield (+X up the forearm, +Z outer side, origin on the forearm line), then carried by this frame's left-arm solve — `SolveTwoBoneArm` records the forearm it leaves (`_leftArmSolve*`), so the gauntlet also follows the raised shield and the bow stance. Muzzle = `GrappleMuzzle*Meters` (mirrors `MUZZLE` in the Blender script); `PlayerMovement.ResolveLeftArmWorldPoint` (tow physics) reads it through `TryGetGrappleMuzzleWorld`.
- **Arm**: while attached (not in a hit reaction, mantle or seat) `SolveGrappleArm` points the left arm straight down the rope line (97 % reach), blended in over **Arm raise time (s)** 0.08, clamped to **Arm reach cone (deg)** 125 around forward-left. It solves after the shield and the bow, so it wins the left arm; `TickGrappleArmRelease` clears the overrides at the top of the next frame once the blend is back to 0 (before the other solvers write, so the shared `ClearBoneOverrides` costs them nothing).
- **Rope + hook**: up to 24 copies of `grapple_rope_segment.vmdl` (1 m along +X, tiling twist) stretched muzzle → hook eye, the bow-string pattern; `grapple_hook.vmdl` sits in the barrel when stowed, flies out at **Shot speed (m/s)** 220 on attach, reels back over **Reel-in time (s)** 0.14 on release (the physics rope is attached the instant the host accepts — the flight is presentation). Slack (rope length − distance) hangs as a parabola of that arc length.
- **Assets**: `blender -b --python Blender/scripts/create_grapple_gear.py` → `Assets/models/tools/grapple_gear/` (one fbx, three vmdls, 256 px `grapple_gear.png` atlas + 64 px tiling `grapple_rope.png`, pixel_lit).

### Grapple on players + landing skip (Mark 2026-11-16)

- **Player tether** (`PlayerMovement.GrapplePlayer.cs`, Grapple PvP group; Mark: "only stay connected for 1s at most, and slow the players down 50% for that 1s"; playtest 2026-11-18: "grappling other players slows you down as well. not just the person you grappled" - the shooter is no longer planted): hosts and clients take the same path — the shooter's client names the pawn it aimed at (`RpcRequestAttach` intended id), the host validates it (`TryValidatePlayerAttach`) and sets `[Sync(FromHost)] GrappleAttachPlayerId` on the shooter and `GrappledByPlayerId` on the target. The host breaks the hook after **Player Tether (s)** 1 (`_playerTetherEndsAt`, checked in `TickGrapplePlayerTargetValidity`, which runs for proxy shooters too). Meanwhile the target's owner scales walk + run by **Hooked Player Speed (x)** 0.5 (`HookedSpeedScale` in `ApplyArmorLocomotionScale`) and loses its own rope plus the 3 s re-grapple lockout; the shooter keeps full walk / run speed, jump and sprint (Mark 2026-11-18: "Full speed"; the old plant, `HoldPlayerTetherShooter` and the walk / run mute on `IsPlayerGrappleAttach`, is gone; `grapple_test` case "the grappler keeps full speed while the hook holds"). Nobody is pulled — the old victim reel (`TickGrappledByPlayerPull`) is gone, so the rope just stretches between them. Damage, a hit reaction or the shooter's grapple key still break it early.
- **Landing skip** (`PlayerMovement.GrappleSkip.cs`, Grapple Landing Skip group; Mark: "players kinda skip along the ground" after a grapple): `OnLanded` used to cap ground speed to the run speed on the first touchdown (`SanitizeHorizontalToDesignRun`, the roof-jump guard) — that, not friction, was what stopped a grapple release dead. Now a rope released **in the air** arms the skip (`ArmGrappleSkipOnRopeEdge` from `DetectGrappleAttachEdge`); each touchdown at **Min Speed (m/s)** 8 or more keeps **Speed Kept Per Skip** 0.85 of the ground speed and hops back up at **Bounce (x fall speed)** 0.35 of the fall speed, clamped to **Min Hop** 1.5 / **Max Hop** 5 m/s (`PreventGrounding` 0.1 s so the controller lets go). Below the min speed the landing is the normal one (sanitize + land scrub) and the skip disarms; a new rope, wingsuit, trap, seat or ledge pull disarms it too. Pawn units: `BodyHeight / 1.8`.

### Ledge grab (grapple + jump onto build pieces / static objects, Mark 2026-11-10 / 11)

- **Owner**: `PlayerMovement.LedgeGrab.cs` (was `PlayerMovement.GrappleLedge.cs`), Ledge Grab group. Space vaults onto a standable lip within **Reach Up (meters)** 2.2 above the feet and **Forward Search (meters)** 1.2 ahead; the guided pull (`TickLedgePull`, **Pull Speed (m/s)** 6) rises beside the face to lip height, then in onto the stand, with the Citizen `ledgegrab_pullup_01` on every peer (`PlayerAnimation.BeginLedgeMantle`). `IsLedgePulling` gates the rope draw / winch / re-attach.
- **Grapple** (**Enabled (grapple)**): hanging on the rope, Pro scheme, any solid geometry; the rope breaks the same instant.
- **Jump** (**Enabled (jump)**): airborne without a rope — jump, then Space again — onto the lip of a `buildpiece`-tagged object (2026-11-10) or any other **static, non-terrain** collider (2026-11-11, Mark: "ledge grab and vault onto other square objects like the rock spires"): spire / boulder sections, props, cave ledges. `IsJumpLedgeSurface` decides per hit — build piece yes; `terrain` (heightfield + voxel columns), `water`, `player` no (a hillside would steal Space from the air hop / wingsuit on every slope); otherwise the body must be `PhysicsBodyType.Static`. The traces hit everything (pawn ignored), and a hit that fails the rule is a miss, so a lip behind a hill or a pawn is never reached through it. Runs in `PreInput` right after the dodge roll gate, so it takes Space before the augment air hop and the wingsuit deploy, but only when such a lip is in reach; otherwise the press falls through. Not while the wingsuit is out, swimming, held by a player grapple or in a hit reaction. Reach: a ground jump (~1.3 m apex) + **Reach Up** 2.2 m tops out at lips ~3.5 m over the ground — boulders, a spire stump whose upper sections were mined (lowest cut 5.5–7 m) only from a grapple swing or a higher stand; a whole spire is a 33–46 m needle with no top to stand on. Dev: `jump_press` presses Space for one frame (consumed at the top of `PreInput`), so the grab can be driven from the editor.
- **Search** (`TryFindLedgeStand`, on the press only): directions = camera look, rope (grapple only), pawn facing. Per direction a forward face probe (at 0.15 / 0.5 / 0.85 of reach) finds the face and down-traces 4 u behind it — the 0.25 m wall top falls between the fixed 1/3-step down-traces that follow — then every candidate needs walkable normal (z ≥ 0.7) and standing capsule clearance. The standard jump (`JumpSpeed` 300, ~51 u apex) does not clear the 2 m wall; the grab is how you get over it.

### Chest lid, twig, camps, nests, hive box, build tabs (Mark 2026-11-10, prompts 24-30)

- **Twig pick-up** (prompt 24): `twig_v2.vmdl` + `twig_v2.vmat` (bark 128 px / 0.5 m) from `Blender/scripts/create_twig.py` replace the blocky `low_poly_stick`; `samplestick.prefab` (the scatter's near-tree stick) and `stick.prefab` point at it at scale 1. Authored in metres, vmdl `import_scale 0.4`; `SIZE_SCALE` 3 in the script makes it 1.79 m long (Mark 2026-11-17: 3x, scaled in the model).
- **Engine axes on the Blender FBX export we use** (`-Z forward, Y up`, measured on the chest): engine X = **-Blender Y**, engine Y = **Blender X**, Z = Z. Scripts that care author in engine metres and rotate -90 deg about Z on export (`create_camp_props.py`, `create_bird_nests.py`).
- **Chest lid** (prompt 25): `Blender/scripts/split_chest_lid.py` separates Rumple's open chest into `chest_body.vmdl` + `chest_lid.vmdl` (lid closed, still in the body frame — the exporter re-centres a mesh whose origin is moved). `chest.prefab`: `Lid` child at the hinge (-10.15, 1.78, -3.93 u) > `LidMesh` at minus that > renderer; `ChestLid` (`Code/Building/ChestLid.cs`) swings `Lid` about local Y by -60 deg (what Rumple modelled). Host counts viewers from `PlayerInventoryInteraction.OpenContainerView / CloseContainer` (`ChestLid.SetViewing` → `[Rpc.Host]`, dead connections pruned) and syncs `IsOpen`; peers ease the angle only while it moves. `chest.vmdl` is gone.
- **Feral scav camp** (prompt 26): `enemy_camps.json` `scav_camp_clover` = campfire at the origin, three thatch huts (`camp_hut_a/b`, doors = local +X) facing it, a 9.5 m fence ring (`camp_fence_a`, every third `camp_fence_b` snapped) with six gaps, six scavs round the fire. Hut / fence rows in `build_pieces.json` carry `"hidden": true` (stamped by camps, never offered by the hammer). `EnemyCamp.DebugRings` now defaults off (`campRings 1` to see them). Models: `Blender/scripts/create_camp_props.py` → `models/environment/camp/`, textures `materials/environment/camp/` (thatch, wattle, wood + rope band, embers; 256 px / 2 m).
- **Campfire model**: `furniture_campfire.prefab` draws `models/building/campfire.vmdl` (stone ring + logs, stones on `rock_gunmetal`) with an `Embers` child (`campfire_embers.vmdl`); `Campfire.Embers` names the renderer that takes Lit / Unlit tint, so the stones and logs stay their own colour.
- **Bird nests** (prompt 28): `nest_ground` / `nest_tree` prefabs (`Blender/scripts/create_bird_nests.py`, `prefabs/environment/cloverHills/resources/`) are hand-harvest `ResourceItemDefinition`s: E gives 0-2 `food_egg` + 0-4 `resource_feathers`, one press, respawn 900 s. Ground nests are a forage cluster (`Vegetation - Forage - Ground nest cell (m)` 120, chance 0.35); tree nests sit in a limb fork like the beehives (`Tree nest chance per large tree` 0.03, 2.8-4 m up, 0.9-1.6 m out, fork yawed back at the trunk; kind `treenest`, always drawn). `BirdNest` on the tree nest keeps a static list; every host tree-down path in `ChopableTree` (`FellToStump / FellCrown / SplitIntoPieces / BreakAndDrop`) calls `BirdNest.HostNotifyTreeDown`, and a nest within `Tree Reach (m)` 4 of the trunk rolls its yields as world drops and depletes through the normal harvest broadcast. `food_egg` is a plain resource row (egg model `models/food/egg/egg.vmdl`, icon from `Tools/item_icons/draw_item_icons.py`); no food effect yet.
- **Hive box** (prompt 29): wild hives now also drop `resource_beehive` (1, 100 %, weight 2); `beehive_box` build piece (Crafting tab) costs 1 beehive + 6 wood, prefab `beehive_box.prefab`, model from `create_camp_props.py`. It is a box only — no honey production yet.
- **Build menu tabs** (prompt 30): `BuildPieceData.Tab` (`"tab"`: `building` / `crafting` / `furniture`, default building) and `Hidden`; `BuildMenuTabs` (order + `Shows`) in `BuildPieceData.cs`; `BuildMenuHud` draws All / Crafting / Building / Furniture under the title and rebuilds the grid per tab (repair tool on every tab). Today: stations, machines and vehicles = crafting; chest, bed, campfire, traps, circuit parts = furniture; everything else = building.

### Held lights (torch, lantern)

- **Data**: an equipment profile's `heldLight` block (`HeldLightData`: `anchor` = `torchTip` | `lanternHang`, `radiusMeters`, `color`) makes the main-hand item a light source. `light_torch` (torchTip, 12 m) and `light_lantern` (lanternHang, 9 m) in `data/equipment_profiles.json`.
- **Presentation lives on `PlayerAnimation`** (`PlayerAnimation.HeldLight.cs`, commandment #1): every peer reads the main-hand id it can see (local slots on host / owner, `PlayerEquipment.NetworkedMainHandResourceId` on other peers), looks up the profile and builds its own local prop (`NetworkMode.Never`, like the demo stick) from `OnPreRender` after the sword. Torch = Citizen `holdtype=HoldItem`, right hand (`HoldPose.HoldItemRight` → `HoldItem_RH_Pose_Standing_01` / `HoldItem_RH_Hand_Basic`), a vertical dev-box stick with the hand at **Held light - Torch grip along length** from the bottom, a flame box and the `PointLight` at the tip. Lantern = no hold type (relaxed arms), a box hanging **Held light - Lantern hang (m)** below the right hand with the light inside. Radius converts once through `TerrainWorldUnits`; prop sizes through `BodyHeight / 1.8`.
- **Swinging a torch** hands the body back to the melee pose for the swing window (`HasActiveMeleeSwingPresentation`, cocked pose included): the box-sword shows, the torch prop is hidden, then the torch pose returns. Hit reaction / ledge mantle drop the prop for their window like the sword.

**Reach, brightness, wet (2026-11-02).** `heldLight.brightness` multiplies the light colour (torch 2.2, radius 20 m; `HeldLightData.ResolveLightColor`). A torch is doused - lamp and flame glow objects disabled, stick stays - while the pawn carries the `wet` status or the flame point is under water (`PlayerAnimation.HeldLight.cs` `ApplyHeldLightDoused`, probed 5×/s through `WaterProbe.IsPointUnderWater`: terrain sea / lake / river surfaces via `TerrainWorldManager.TryGetWaterDepthMeters`, else any `water`-tagged trigger). `wet` (`status_effects.json`) is host-set by `PlayerVitals.Wet.cs`: chest under water or `PlayerController.IsSwimming` → expiry now + `WetDrySeconds` (30 s), refreshed only once it has drifted 3 s. `StatusEffectCatalog` now picks up JSON edits like `EquipmentCatalog` (hash check every 2 s).

### UI theme (every HUD element and menu)

- **One theme behind the whole UI** (Mark 2026-11-08: the grey boxes "look like a child made it in MS Paint"; a professional AAA look, interchangeable later). `Assets/data/ui_themes.json` holds the themes (`ember` default - charcoal, warm off-white text, brass accent; `frost` as the swap proof); `UiTheme` (`Code/UI/Theme/`) loads it, `UiStyle` applies it to code-built panels (`Surface`, `Text`, `Button`/`SetButtonState`, `Slot`/`SetSlotState`, `Bar`, `KeyCap`, `Tab`, `ScrollBar`, `Backdrop`, `AddTitle` …) and `UiThemeStyleSheet` generates the same theme as classes (`ui-frame`, `ui-title`, `ui-button:hover` …) for the razor main menu and load screen. Textures (grain tile, corner bracket, vignette, divider) come from `Tools/ui_theme/generate_ui_theme_textures.py`; the tab icons from `generate_menu_icons.py` (white line-art, tinted by the theme). `ui_theme <id|reload>` switches / re-reads; the choice is per machine (`FileSystem.Data/ui_theme.json`). Rules and the full API: `Code/UI/docs/UI_THEME.md`. **No panel names a colour, font, radius or border of its own** - add a theme field instead. Data colours (resource tints, cable colours, hoe swatches, boss form colour, map pins, crew colours) stay data.
- **Scaling**: every HUD / menu root sits under a `ScreenPanel` with `AutoScreenScale` on, so all sizes are 1080p design pixels and follow the window / fullscreen height; the crafting column uses `vh`, the main menu / load screen cards use `%` with min / max widths.

### Crafting menus + dev hacks

- **Item models on world drops** (Mark 2026-11-03: "use the models the claude cloud agent made for all the items instead of just spheres"): `data/item_world_models.json` maps an item id to its model - the generated set from `Tools/item_models` (`Assets/models/<category>/<name>/<name>.vmdl`, 40 u/m) plus the wooden shield. `HeldStackWorldDrop.ApplyResourceVisual` swaps the drop's dev rock for that model at scale 1, untinted (`ItemWorldModelCatalog`, read once and re-checked for edits at most every 2 s); an unlisted id keeps the tinted rock. A value may be a list of variants, one picked per drop: `resource_stone` → `models/resources/stone/stone_drop1-4_v1` (0.12-0.19 m gunmetal stones from `create_mineable_rocks.py`). Since 2026-11-03 (Mark: "add the missing models yourself") every id in resources / crafting / equipment / food JSON has a model: 202 more generated ones (fish, metal-tier weapons / tools / armour, ores and crafted parts, meats and animal parts, cooked food, seed pouches, a trophy head per creature and enemy, cyberware modules - real-world size, tiny things scaled up to ~0.18 m+; table in `Tools/item_models/README.md`), and `sword_reforged` / `axe_reforged` / every shield drop as their existing held model. The wooden club and stone axe now hold their own generated models (`equipment_profiles.json` heldModel; they held `sword_reforged` / `axe_reforged`). **Drop collider** (Mark 2026-11-05: dropped items "just kinda float"): `HeldStackWorldDrop.EnsurePhysics` gives a drop whose model has physics a `ModelCollider` on that model - every item vmdl carries a `PhysicsHullFromRender` single convex hull - so it lies on its real shape; the old sphere (0.34 x the largest dimension, clamped 5-14 u) held long or flat items up off the ground. Only the tinted fallback rock keeps a sphere. Measured: 262 item drops, 261 within 3 cm of the ground (a spear 4.4 cm, leaning on a neighbour).
- **Loose items have no colliders** (Mark 2026-11-23: "items that are loose on the ground (rocks/twigs/mushrooms etc, and any player-dropped model) doesnt have colliders"). *Scattered pick-ups*: the prefab carries none and `ResourceItemDefinition` **Auto Ensure Trace Collider** is off on every loose pick-up (pebble1-3 + rockpickup1 lost their authored `ModelCollider`, which the Collision Range toggle used to switch back on; sticks, daisies, hobbit's leaf, red mushrooms, night crawlers, wild wheat, fiddleheads, feathers, femur, raspberry bushes have the flag off; `create_redwood_plants.py` writes it off). Picking needs no collider: `PlayerHandHarvest.UpdateFocusedNode` falls back from its view ray to the `ResourceHarvestRegistry` scan (range + `LookConeDegrees` 18 + line of sight to 8 u above the origin, `HandHarvestTargeting`), the path sticks always used. A new loose pick-up prefab: no collider, flag off. Still on the auto collider: beehives and blueberry / leafy bushes (their models have no physics, so nothing solid comes of it) and the two nests (their models carry a hull, so a nest is solid). Mineable rocks and trees are untouched. *Drops* (`WorldDroppedResource`): the hull / sphere + `Rigidbody` from `HeldStackWorldDrop.EnsurePhysics` (and a loose arrow's box from `ArrowProjectile.ApplyLoosePhysics`) exist only for the fall. When the host freezes the drop (asleep, or slow for 0.2 s) or the magnet takes it, it sets the synced `Settled` and every machine switches the drop's colliders and body off (`[Change]` callback + `OnStart` for late joiners). During the fall the `worlddrop` tag ignores `player` / `enemy` / `animal` / `vehicle` (`ProjectSettings/Collision.config` pairs). Arrows stuck in the world and the bola pickup never get a collider. Nothing traces for a drop: pickup is the registry magnet below. The corpse bag is a container opened by the Use trace and keeps its static collider.
- **World drop pickup** (Mark 2026-11-16: "when we harvest stuff it drops to the ground and then we suck it up 1s later … unless you just dropped it from your inventory, in that case it's 5s. Clients should be able to suck up materials too"): every yield lands as a `WorldDroppedResource` (hand harvest too: `PlayerHandHarvest.ServerTryHandHarvest` → `HeldStackWorldDrop.SpawnLootBurst`, singles tossed out round the plant; nests share the helper). The host flips the synced `PickupReady` 1 s after the drop first touches anything (`ICollisionListener.OnCollisionStart`, or the moment it has no moving body / freezes), 5 s for an inventory drop (`TrySpawnWorldDrop( …, inventoryDrop: true )` → `MarkInventoryDrop`), the same wait for everyone. The magnet (`PlayerHandHarvest.TickWorldDropMagnet`: 2.5 m attract, 0.4 m contact, 10 m/s) runs **on the host for every pawn** and banks on contact; clients see the flight through the drop's synced transform (a client-side magnet used to move a host-owned drop the sync snapped back, so clients never reached it). A stack dumped out of an inventory lands as **one pickup per unit**, fanned out, and never clumps back (Mark 2026-11-19: easy to find, not "a tiny stick in the grass"): `HeldStackWorldDrop.SpawnInventorySingles` behind `TryDropAtPlayer` / `TryDrop`, and `MarkInventoryDrop` sets `PreventMerge`.
- **Corpse bag** (Mark 2026-11-19: "a burlap sack tied with black string. About the same width as players, about knee high, it should add a pin to the map where it is, and shoot a beacon up in the air 30m"): the death drop (`DeathLootBag.HostSpawnForDeath`, resources only, take-only container, gone when emptied) is now the sack `models/props/corpse_bag/corpse_bag_v1.vmdl` (`Blender/scripts/create_corpse_bag.py`: 0.62 m across, 0.55 m tall, origin on the ground, its physics hull is the static Use collider) carrying a `CorpseBag` component (`Code/Inventory/CorpseBag.cs`): synced owner connection id + Steam id + name. On the owner's machine only it draws the beacon (`CorpseBagBeaconVfx`, an unlit column `BeaconHeightMeters` = 30 m up, at least 3 px wide at any range, top third fades out) and the map shows a sack pin (`TerrainWorldMapFace.UpdateCorpseBagMarkers` over `CorpseBag.Registered`, icon `ui/map/pin_corpse_bag.png` from `Tools/map_pins/draw_corpse_bag_pin.py`). Not a saved pin: it follows the object and goes when the bag does. Anyone can still open the bag.
- **Personal (inventory) crafting menu** lists a recipe only when its `stations` array names `"inventory"` (`PlayerCrafting.InventoryStationId`) — Mark's 2026-11-08 recipe sheet: the *Player Pocket* set is torch, build hammer, wooden club, bola and wooden arrows (plus the fishing rod and augment core, which the sheet does not list) — plus `requiredStation` recipes while that station is nearby (`CraftingStations.IsPlayerNear`: campfire food by a lit fire). Everything else (`stations: ["workbench"]`) is workbench-only: rope, bone glue, stone axe, hoe, flint spear, wooden shield, simple bow, bone sword, grapple, fire / flint arrows and the leather clothes. Listing is UI-only; the host gates crafting on `requiredStation` (same `CraftingStations` test) and cost. Recipe `ingredients`, `outputAmount` and `maxStack` for those items are the sheet's values; `leed` (1 rope, workbench) is an equipment item (`equipment_profiles.json`, held model `models/tools/leed/leed.vmdl` from `itemgen/items_husbandry.py`, temp icon = its render) with no action yet - the multi-tame lead-and-drag behaviour is a follow-up. **Dev toggle** (Settings → Dev tools → "Recipes: all in pocket, free / stations only") flips `GameHacks.AllCrafting`, default **on**: every recipe in the pocket menu and free; off = shipped rules.
- **`GameHacks`** (`Code/Hacks/GameHacks.cs`): static, console-driven dev flags, local to the client. `allCrafting true|false` (no argument prints state), `hacks` lists all flags. `GameHacks.Version` bumps on change so cached UI (recipe rows) rebuilds. **`allCrafting` defaults on** while crafting content is being built out.
- `allCrafting` on: personal menu lists **every** recipe (locked, workbench-only, station-gated) and requirement lines read `have/0`; the owner mirrors the flag onto `PlayerCrafting.AllCraftingHack` (`[Sync]`) so `HostTryCraft` waives unlock, station and cost (output space still checked). Off: normal listing, real JSON costs, host consumes ingredients.
- `devSupply true|false` (off by default): **X** (input action `DevSupply`) or `devSupplyOpen` opens the **Dev Supply** grid beside the bag — one full stack of every `resource_*` in resources.json plus every crafting-recipe ingredient, restocked from the catalogs on each open. It is a client-local, `NetworkMode.Never` `ContainerInventory` (`TakeOnly` + `InfiniteSupply`: every take snaps the slots back to the stock) built by `PlayerInventoryInteraction.DevSupply.cs` and shown through the normal container view, so clicks / drags / shift-moves work unchanged; a shift-move remainder that does not fit is discarded, never dropped. Items reach the bag through the usual bag RPCs — the gate is the client-side flag only, like the other hacks.
- New hack flags go on `GameHacks` (flag + `Set…` + `ConCmd`), and any flag the host must honour is mirrored onto the relevant pawn component the same way — never trusted from an RPC argument.
- Host-only item hacks for testing imports and recipes: `give <resourceId> [amount]` adds straight into the local pawn's hotbar / inventory; `equip <resourceId>` binds hotbar slot 1 (slot 2 for a shield) to the item, gives one and selects the slot, so a held model (`heldModel`) shows at once without a key press; `swing [holdSeconds]` holds the primary attack action through `Input.SetAction` from the top of `PlayerCombat`'s owner input tick (`GameHacks.ApplySimulatedInput`) and releases it, so the real windup → sweep → recovery runs. This is how the sword / axe fit and swing are filmed from the editor MCP, which has no input simulation (`camera_screenshot` on a temporary `CameraComponent`).

### Build structural integrity (Valheim model, Valheim numbers)

- **Model** (`BuildStructuralIntegrity`, host-only, event-driven from `BuildAuthority` — never per frame): each placed piece holds one support value = the best any *touching* neighbor can pass it, decayed by `S_child = min(childMax, S_parent × (1 − L(θ) × 2.1))` — a **fixed one-module hop per connection**: support is budgeted in pieces, not meters travelled (per Mark: stacked thin beams max out at the same count as stacked walls). `L(θ)` lerps horizontal→vertical loss by the connection's upness (hanging = horizontal). No accumulated weight. Grounded pieces (short down-probes from the lowest oriented corners hitting static non-build colliders — terrain, rocks, trees) seed at material max. **Trees and rocks are anchors from any side**: a piece whose oriented box (plus the 15 cm contact slack) overlaps a standing `ChopableTree`, its stump or a `MineableRock` section is grounded too (`TouchesTreeOrRock`, one box overlap per piece at placement) — a beam butted against a trunk, a floor wrapped round it. Anything with a `Rigidbody` (felled log, falling crown, vehicles) never anchors. **The anchor goes with the tree / rock**: `ChopableTree` (felled to stump, crown dropped, stump chopped) and `MineableRock.HostApplyHit` (sections broken or collapsed) call `BuildStructuralIntegrity.HostOnWorldAnchorChanged( scene, boundsBeforeTheChange )`; grounded pieces inside those bounds drop their cached grounded state and `BuildCollapseRunner` re-solves their structures on the next fixed tick — what still touches the stump, the remaining sections or the terrain grounds again, the rest collapses bottom-up as usual. Chunk unloads never release an anchor (only the host's chop / mine paths do).
- **Materials** live in `Assets/data/build_pieces.json` → top-level `materials` array; pieces opt in via `materialId`. Wood = twice Valheim's reach (Mark 2026-11-16: "allow players to build much bigger and higher"): max 100, min 10, vLoss 0.0672, hLoss 0.1135 (each per-piece factor the square root of Valheim's 0.125 / 0.2 ones) → exactly 16 pieces straight up (any sizes), grounded piece + 8 out sideways, mixed paths twice as long too. **No materialId = exempt** (chest, the nine crafting stations, campfire, repair) — deliberate, do not add one to furniture.
- **Collapse**: placement is never blocked — a piece below its material minimum places, then the solver destroys it (place-then-collapse, per Mark). Demolishing re-solves the touched component (the removed piece is explicitly excluded — engine destroys are deferred, so it would otherwise keep "supporting" its structure that frame). Support below min never props others, so one Dijkstra pass lands on the post-cascade state; the doomed set is then destroyed **bottom-up, one piece per 0.12 s wave** by `BuildCollapseRunner` (first piece pops synchronously so a place-then-collapse still refunds the hammer swing). Each queued piece is re-checked at its wave — rebuilding support mid-collapse rescues whatever still solves above min.
- **Feedback**: hammer-out hover tints the aimed piece via the support gradient (blue max → red near min) and `BuildSupportReadoutHud` shows "Support N%" under the crosshair (numeric on purpose — not color-only). Values reach clients through `[Sync] BuildPiece.Support`.

### Entity AI perception + biome population

- **Perception / AI states** (reworked 2026-11-04, Mark: scav AI "seems very artificial"): Idle ↔ Wander (legs 35–100 % of `wanderDistanceMeters` on a ±100° arc, bent back toward `HomePosition` once two wander distances out; one idle in four is longer, half of them glance somewhere new). The alert meter fills from sight (150° cone, plus a 3 m **proximity sense** behind it at 60 % rate) and noise; from 30 % it **stops and looks** at the stimulus (`TickNotice`, awareness 1), full → **Chasing** what it can see (FOV + LOS, or the proximity circle), else **Searching** where it heard it — the old fallback "nearest player within 60 m" is gone. Chasing hunts the live player while it **sees** them (no cone, ≤ 2 × sight range, LOS — plain LOS at any range kept a scav "seeing" a player 120 m across grass) and for `chaseCommitLosSeconds` after; then it walks to the **last-seen spot + their velocity × 1.2 s** (≤ 6 m), footsteps it hears move that trail; reaching it unseen, or `chaseLostLosSeconds` unseen, → Searching around it (8–14 s: walk, stop and look around 1–2.5 s, try another spot 3–7 m off) → Idle. **Attacking**: `AttackRange` = the weapon class's lateral reach (`GetMeleeAttackRangeUnits`) + 12 u body radius (was a hard-coded 110 u against a 76 u blade — swings at the air); leaves past 1.6 × that. Close in (≤ 6 m) the chase goal is a **ring slot** (`EntityAttackSlots`: a bearing per attacker ≥ 55° apart, the free one nearest the side it came from, re-claimed if > 100° off, dropped past 9 m) at 0.7 × reach; at most **two attack tokens** per player — a holder steps in and swings, the rest hold their slot at 1.35 × reach facing the player. `EntityCombat` (the duel, next bullets): an opener goes out within ±25° of the target, yaw locks from the click through the release led by the target's velocity × the windup (≤ 30°), the body re-aims in the recovery (220°/s) and turns at locomotion rate between swings; the brain steps in at a walk during the windup (`IsWindingUp`) and plants the feet in the release / recovery (`IsSwingInFlight`). **Retreating** at `retreatHealthFraction`: committed — turns to fight only when a seen player is within 3 m (then not again for 6 s), ends at the flee distance / goal / 12 s, unlocks the alert meter and blocks another retreat for 15 s (it used to flip Retreat ↔ Chase every frame near any player within 30 m). `BeginAiNow` (nav settled late) keeps a chase / search / retreat already running. A hit while walking home re-aggroes once back inside the leash's max travel distance. Locomotion turns while moving (speed 30 % with the path behind → full within ~40°; it used to stop dead past 75°) and faces a look target's live position (the 2.75 s aim re-read is gone). `EntityBrain.Awareness` (Sync FromHost: 0 / 1 suspicious / 2 alerted) feeds the nameplate mark. Nav uses live **PhysicsWorld** via `GenerateTiles` when structures change; the coalesced local rebake queue is ticked by `BuildNavBakeSystem` (a `GameObjectSystem`, every scene) — it used to hang off `TerrainWorldManager`, so scenes without streamed terrain never rebaked around placed walls and agents walked through them.
- **Packs** (Mark 2026-11-05: "when multiple scavs are running in a group toward a player they get stuck on each other"): another scav / animal body is never a wall. `EntityLocomotion.ClipMovementAgainstSolids` and `TrySteerAlongWall` skip any hierarchy with `EntityLocomotion` (`IsPawnOrEntityHierarchy`, players were already skipped) - the clip held two touching bodies at their clip origins and reset each other's agents, so a pack knotted up 5 m short. Spacing instead: `KeepClearOfOtherBodies` sets a moving body back out to `MinBodySpacing` (26 u ≈ 0.65 m, within 64 u height) from any other body along the line between them (sideways for a dead-centre overlap), mover only, agent untouched, from a static list of enabled bodies (no scene scan); the agents' own crowd `Separation` steers the paths. `EntitySight` LOS traces see through `enemy` / `animal` tags (`SeeThroughTags`) - the scavs behind lost sight of the player past the front-runners' capsules and dropped back to Wander. Measured with six provoked scavs from 22 m on open ground: all six reach the player and attack, no stalls, closest pair 0.59 m. (testscene1's two starter chests in front of the player spawn are not carved into nav - scavs coming from that side stall against them.)
- **No standing on creatures** (`PlayerMovement.CreatureFooting.cs`, Mark 2026-11-05: "when i jump on a pile of them they just kinda stay there, we should push them over so i fall to the ground"): each physics step of the local driver, if `PlayerController.GroundObject` carries the `enemy` / `animal` tag (inherited by the collider children) the pawn gets `PreventGrounding( 0.1 )` and a sideways push away from that body's root (`CreatureSlideOffMetersPerSecond` 3.5 m/s, topped up only to that speed, never upward). Measured on a frozen scav: without the push the pawn stayed on its head at 1.53 m; with it, on the ground in 0.8 s. Large enemies players may stand on come later (Mark: "we can cross that bridge when we get there").
- **Scav look / alert mark** (2026-11-05): `scavT1.prefab` body groups 5441 -> **21825** - 5441 picked `Feet` choice 1 (empty, for shoes), so scavs had no feet; the player prefab also stores 5441 but its runtime body groups come out at 20737 (`Feet` 0). The red "!" over an alerted hostile shows for its first 3 s only (`EnemyHealthBar.AlertMark`, read by `OverheadNameplateHud`; Mark: "its there indefinitely rn"); it comes back on the next alert, the amber "?" is unchanged.
- **Path validation follows the ground** (`EntityBrain.PathCrossesSolid`): each nav segment is re-grounded every 96 u with a down probe before the body-height sweep, and `PathCrossesSolidCached` reuses the verdict for 1.5 s while the path's endpoints and length are unchanged (invalidated on every bake) — a dozen entities re-validating every 0.3 s was the other half of the renav lag. A string-pulled path has no point where a stair meets a floor, so the raw stair-foot→floor segment sliced through the slab from underneath and vetoed every route up a structure (`blocked … status=Complete crossesSolid=True` on every line of the log) — the entity breached instead of climbing, and the route through a hole it had just opened was vetoed the same way.
- **Stairs / roofs are routes, never walls** (`BuildPieceNavPolicy.IsWalkablePathObject`): the movement clip, wall-steer, `PathCrossesSolid` and the breach in-the-way probe all skip WalkablePath pieces — a stair riser or a roof edge is a vertical face and every normal test read it as a wall, which is why scavs breached stairs instead of climbing them. Feet-glue follows build-piece height at 600 u/s (terrain keeps its 55 u/s ease) so a 45° roof can be run up at 220 u/s without sinking into the slab. Runtime nav limits: max slope 55° (scene 40°), step 40 u (scene 18).
- **Chase goal is where the player stands**: `GetLiveChasePoint` asks `EntityNavMeshUtility.TryFindNavAtFeet` (tight box sample around the feet, ±40 u xy / ±48 u z — deterministic, never the ground a storey below) for nav under the player (terrain or a build floor / roof at any height) and paths there. Only an airborne / grappling player falls back to "the ground under their XY". Melee needs the target within `MeleeVerticalReach` (64 u) — an entity never enters Attacking against someone a floor above.
- **Scav body, sword and the world health bar (2026-11-04, Mark: "its using the outdated model and its attacking isn't animated … only have the red health bar if it's been hit before … always be perfectly horizontal and readable")**: `scavT1.prefab` wears the pawn's body, `models/player/human_male_sword.vmdl` (BodyGroups 5441, no `Dresser` - it put the host's citizen avatar clothes on it); `bossT1` is still on `citizen.vmdl`. `EntityCombat` presents the swing on every peer with the pawn's one-handed sword (Mark 2026-11-04: "Scavs should just attack with the same standard sword as we do, ultimately it should be like a wooden club"): melee_weapons holdtype, right hand, the same `sword_pose` / `sword_attack` / `b_attack` the pawn drives. Host Syncs `SwordCocked` while its host swing winds up (the cocked pose a player reads, via `Sword_Swap_Mid` when it crosses the face) + `SwingAttackType`, and bumps `StrikeSerial` when the host release opens; each peer fires the strike clip then and rests on the strike's side after 0.40 s. The guard pose (`b_sword_guard` + `sword_guard_dir`) follows its PlayerCombat's host guard (broadcast to every peer); the heavy-ready glint rides the blade (`FeedHeavyFlashBlade` from `OnPreRender`). The weapon is the held model of the pawn's own sword item, **Weapon - Weapon profile** (`club_wood`, which the `sword` ids alias to; Mark: "Make the scav use the same sword model as us"), placed on `hold_R` by the pawn's one-handed rule through `HeldModelFit` in `OnPreRender`, world scale 1 so the root's 0.85 does not shrink it. Swing timings come from the club's class (`FallbackWeaponClass` `oneHanded` on `scavT1` / `bossT1`). The overhead banner of this bullet (world panel, constant on-screen size) was replaced the same day by the screen-space nameplates (next bullet).
- **Scav duel (2026-11-14, Mark: "the scavs attacks arent as fast as they can be ... It also never blocks lol")**: `EntityCombat` no longer runs its own telegraph (0.85 s) + gap (1 s ± 40 %) on top of the class windup - it presses attack and guard through the **same host intake** a remote player's click and guard report take (`PlayerCombat.HostSubmitPlayerSwing` / `HostNotePrimaryAttackReleased` / `HostSubmitGuard`, hooks in `PlayerCombat.HostView.cs`), so every rule of the directional exchange applies unchanged: class windup, combo windup on chained hits, counter windup after blocking a light, heavy on hold, feint on a guard press in the early windup, chamber timing, blocked recovery, staggers, stamina. It reads its own and its opponent's host swing through `TryGetHostSwing` (`HostSwingView`).
  - **Difficulty ladder** (`Assets/data/scav_combat_styles.json` `levels`, easiest first; `ScavCombatStyleCatalog.Get( level )` / `Current` / `LevelCount`, `scav_styles_reload`; the file documents every field), picked by the host-wide **Settings > Dev tools > Scav difficulty** [-] level [+] stepper (`DevToolsSettings.ScavDifficulty`, saved, default 1, console `scav_difficulty 1-7`, broadcast to clients with the other host-wide flags). Mark 2026-11-15: "make the Scav ai a sliding scale ... sparring should be god difficulty ... our current standard mode ... like medium ... where the actual Scav difficulty should be, at the bottom as the dumbest combat guy" - and the ladder is how other melee fighters get scaled later. **Defensive after a hit** (Mark 2026-11-15: "Any way to check basic rupu ai and match that?" - players report a Last Oasis Rupu goes defensive for a moment once hit, holds the matching block while you charge, and walks in to swing when you drop into a block; that bait takes 3-5 at once): `defensiveSeconds` after the stagger it opens nothing and reads swings with `defensiveReactionMin/MaxSeconds` and `defensiveBlockChance`; the opponent raising a guard ends it and it swings 0.15-0.35 s later (`EntityCombat._defensiveUntil`). Levels: **1 Scav** (the real scav, matched to a basic Rupu: guards 10 % of fresh swings, 75 % for 1.4 s after a hit at 0.2-0.34 s reaction, 35 % wrong side, backsteps 10 %, 8 % second hit, 1.3-2.6 s think; the first cut at 3 % guards was "too retarded"), **2 Novice**, **3 Easy**, **4 Medium** (the 2026-11-14 standard style plus the defensive mode, "handle 2-4 scavs at once": guards 15 % (reaction 0.3-0.5 s, 30 % wrong side), never chambers, backsteps out of reach on 35 % of the swings it does not guard (reaction 0.1-0.22 s - stepping back needs no read of the side), single swings (20 % a second), 0.9-2 s think, a spacing step after 25 % of its sequences), **5 Hard**, **6 Expert** (chamber, feint, stamina reserve come in from 5; shorter defensive spells), **7 God** (the 2026-11-14 sparring style unchanged, no defensive mode, "very close to fighting rumple ... squeeze in one or 2 attacks but don't just hammer combos": human reaction 0.24-0.42 s from the click (an opener's guard is mostly in time, a chained hit's often not), guards 70 % (12 % wrong side), chambers only when its own windup still fits after that reaction (`ChamberFitsAfter` - with equal windups only from its counter window), counter 45 %, second hit 35 %, third 10 %, heavy 12 % (x2 against a raised guard), feint 10 %, 0.4-1.2 s think, spacing 35 %, and below 25 stamina it stays out of reach until it has 50).
  - **A block hands it the initiative** (2026-11-15, Mark: "they dont use their block initiative when they block the overhead" - a level-1 scav blocked 7 overheads in 25 s and swung back 0 times): blocking a light ends the defensive wait and it either counters in the window (`counterChance`, 0.67 Scav to Expert, 0.75 God - 2026-11-16, Mark: "scavs dont use their inititative enough still ... make them like 2/3 likely"; was 0.30 -> 0.75) or opens `afterBlockSwingMin/MaxSeconds` later (0.5-0.9 s Scav -> 0.2-0.4 s God). A new incoming swing does not cancel its own swing (counter or opener in its windup) when its release lands first by 0.05 s (`EntityCombat.OwnSwingLandsFirst`) - the hit staggers the attacker and cuts its swing - instead of guarding or feinting its swing to guard. Measured on overhead spam (no guard): Scav swings back after every block; Hard blocks 12 in 30 s, every block answered, every kept swing landed.
