  - **Every swing in reach is read, not only the target's** (2026-11-15, the brawl showed a level 5 taking every second attacker's hit unseen): `EntityCombat.PickThreatFoe` keeps the hostile fighters within 4 m (`EntityBrain.IsHostileFighter`, refreshed every 0.25 s) and defends the swing aimed at it that releases first; guarding a non-target it turns toward that attacker (`TurnToward`). **Group pacing**: a token handed back stays shut to the other attackers of that target for the returner's `takeoverPauseMin/MaxSeconds` (`EntityAttackSlots.Ring.OpenAt`; the returner may re-take it) - before, a waiting scav swung the instant a token came back and five kept ~1.8 swings / s on one target. Levels 5-7 now read like an experienced player (reaction 0.22-0.36 / 0.20-0.33 / 0.18-0.30 s, guard 82 / 88 / 94 %, wrong side 8 / 5 / 3 %).
  - **`scav_brawl [champLevel] [mobLevel] [mobCount] [rounds]`** (`ScavBrawl`, `ScavBrawlReferee`, `EntityBrain.Brawl.cs`; `scav_brawl_stop`): scav-vs-scav rounds 10 m in front of the host pawn - each fighter on its own `EntityBrain.BrawlTeam` (ignores players, hunts the nearest living scav of another team through the normal chase / attack states) with its own `EntityCombat.DifficultyLevel` (a `[Property]`, 0 = the host's level - the hook for scaling other melee fighters). Mark 2026-11-15: "pit like a lvl 5 scav against 5 level 1s? The level 5 should win". Measured (testscene1, 3 rounds each): level 5 beats 1 and 2 Scavs every round, 3 about half, 5 never; God never beats 5 either - two attackers swinging from different sides at once cannot both be guarded, and the AI has no positioning (lining attackers up) yet.
  - **Backstep** (`EntityCombat.IsBackingOff` / `BackstepDistanceUnits` = the opponent's reach + `backstepMeters`): the brain (`EntityBrain.BackOffFromTarget`) runs it straight away from the target while the combat keeps it facing in; a pending dodge beats an opener.
  - **Scav stamina** (`EntityVitals`: `MaxStamina` 100, the player's regen rates / delays from `VitalsAuthority`, exhaustion at empty until 20, the host's infinite stamina fills it): every combat cost of its PlayerCombat spends it (`HostTrySpendCombatStamina`, block costs in `ServerApplyBlockOutcome` - a guard that cannot pay empties the bar and drops - long block drain, heavy, heavy hold, feint) and `IsStaminaExhausted` locks its attacks and guard like a player's.
  - **Stagger**: a landed hit on a scav staggers it - `ApplyMeleeStaggerToVictim( DamageReceiver, … )` runs the victim PlayerCombat's `HostTryStagger` (swing cut, combo dropped, hard-stagger protection) and `EntityCombat.HostOnStaggered` (guard down, attack lock); it had no PlayerAnimation, so nothing interrupted it before. The hit cue plays on scavs too (`IsLivingTarget` counts `EntityVitals`). `EntityArchetype` no longer sets per-type swing timings (Boneback / Howler swing the same class and style).
  - **Debug - Log duel decisions** (`LogDuel`) prints `[Duel]` lines (each read of an opponent swing and the answer, guard side, chamber, opener / chain / heavy / feint / counter, backstep and why, catching its breath, stagger). Measured in testscene1 against console swings: standard - backsteps made the swing whiff, rare guards, single swings; sparring - guards on the right side most of the time, wrong-side guards hit through, counters, feint into the other side, a heavy at 0.73 s, spacing steps.
- **Overhead nameplates — Valheim style (2026-11-04, Mark: "minimal, compact … only displays relevant information and scales naturally with distance")**: `OverheadNameplateHud` (screen space, pooled `Panel`s under `PlayerScreenHud`'s root, built beside the boss bar, ticked in `PlayerScreenHud.OnPreRender` after the camera moved) reads every `EnemyHealthBar` (now only the per-entity source: `HealthBarAnchor` point, name without tier — Mark: "drop the T1" — health fraction, `LastHitAt`, disposition, awareness; it draws nothing) plus other players' `PlayerVitals`. Enemies / animals: hidden; name over a thin bar (no numbers) while the crosshair ray (camera forward, 6 u radius, `targetingMeters`) is on them, held `targetHoldSeconds` against flicker, and for `combatVisibleSeconds` (1.5 s) after targeting ends or after the last hit; a hostile shows an amber "?" when suspicious and a red "!" when alerted on its own. Bar colour: hostile red, neutral yellow (untamed animals), friendly green (tamed; an animal tracking / attacking the player is hostile). Other players: name within `playerNameMeters`, bar only while targeted / just after. Scale from camera distance (100 % to 3 m, 90 % at 10, 75 % at 20, 60 % at 30), opacity 1 → 0 between 30 and 40 m, `fadeSeconds` in / out; sizes are HUD px at 1080p, so resolution and UI scale apply. A camera → head trace per shown plate every ~0.12 s hides it behind terrain, rocks, trees and structures (creatures in between do not). Styles are written only when a rounded value changes; a plate is deleted with its source. Tuning: `Assets/data/nameplates.json` (`NameplateTuning`, `nameplates_reload`). The world-space `EnemyHealthBarPanel` and its per-prefab `MaxDisplayDistance` / `WorldScale` keys are gone. Gaps: entity health and `LastHitAt` are host-local (a client sees full bars and no hit window); the awareness mark is synced.

**Forage and beehives (2026-11-02, prompts 20 + 22).** `TerrainWorldManager.Forage.cs` adds five Clover Hills prop clusters (wild wheat, night crawlers, daisies, blueberry bushes, red mushrooms (`mushroom_red1-3`; night crawlers dark blue, `nightcrawler1/2_v2`); the table in the file gives spacing / chance / cluster size → clusters per km²) - `PropClusterOptions.Prefabs` picks a model variant per member - and hangs a beehive (`beehive1/2.prefab`, origin at the twig stub) from one large Clover tree in fifty (`VegetationCloverBeehiveChance01` 0.02, 3.2-4.4 m up, 1.0-1.8 m out; `TrySpawnBeehiveOnTree`). Every forage prefab is a hand-harvest `ResourceItemDefinition` under `prefabs/environment/cloverHills/groundFoliage/` (yields: wheat + seed, bait_nightCrawlers, daisy + seed, blueberries + seed, mushroom; hive → honey). Their origin sits on (or a hair under) the ground, so `HandHarvestTargeting`'s line-of-sight ray aims 8 u above the origin and counts any stop within 14 u of that as reaching the plant (the ray to the origin ended in the terrain - "blocked line of sight" - and daisies, wheat and mushrooms could not be picked). Daisies and red mushrooms scatter at scale 2-2.4 since 2026-11-19 (about 0.7-0.95 m and 0.45-0.65 m, cluster radius 1.6 m; Mark: "so small i could step on them and not know it"); the other kinds keep 0.9-1.15 (`Make( …, scaleMin, scaleMax )`). **Leafy bushes** (Mark 2026-11-19): `leafy_bush1-5_v1` (`Blender/scripts/create_cloverhills_bushes.py`, the blueberry bush recipe without berries on the shared forage.vmat; 1.00 / 1.12 / 1.25 / 1.36 / 1.48 m tall, waist to shoulder; every leaf card 0.5 m so the 64-texel sprig lands at 128 texels / m, fitted by re-running the builder, never by scaling the mesh) scatter as kind `bush`: 32 m cell, chance 0.6, groups of 1-3 across 3 m (~586 groups / km², one every ~40 m; Mark 2026-11-23: "add those randomly throughout the biome? Its empty at the moment" - was a 64 m cell, 0.35, clumps of 3-6 across 4.5 m), scale 0.95-1.05, 1.5 m off trunks. Hand harvest gives 2-3 `resource_plantFiber`, three picks, respawn 600 s (`world_leafybush`).

**Forage, stone and generated tilled soil (2026-11-02, Mark: "can you make the wheat spawn on tilled soil?" / "I dont see any stone spires in the terraintest ... our other stone models are missing as well!").** Prop cluster cells are clamped to one per 64 m chunk (`ClusterCell`), so every forage spacing above 64 m really rolls per chunk: measured with `world_find` on seed 1337 over 21.4 km² of Clover Hills, wild wheat measured **122 clusters / km²** at its first 0.5 chance (not the table's 62); Mark then cut it to 5 % ("too many wheat seeds everywhere"): chance 0.025, ~6 beds / km². `TryPlanClusterCenter` is now the one pure centre planner (jitter, roll, land, biome, optional clearing test) shared by the scatter and the ground-bed planner.
- **Wheat beds**: each wild-wheat cluster (3-6 tufts in 1.5 m) lies on a ~2.7 m-radius bed of dark tilled soil (`TerrainGroundPatch`, planned on the chunk worker by `TerrainVegetationScatter.PlanGroundPatches` from the wheat's own cluster cell + the 8 neighbours', painted into the chunk splat by `TerrainGroundSplat.ApplyTilledPatches`, kept on `LoadedChunk.GroundPatches`). No grass clumps on it (coverage ≥ 0.5), `TilledAt` answers from it on undug ground so **seeds sow on it** (`FarmingRules.IsTilledGroundAt`), and a voxel column dug there seeds its tilled paint from it (`VoxelField.SeedTilled`). Detail in `Code/Environment/docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`.
- **Stone** (`TerrainWorldManager.Stones.cs`, all under **Vegetation - Clover — Scatter Rocks**): the rock clusters (Rock Cluster Spacing 50 m / chance 0.7 / 2-5 per cluster, ~170 clusters / km²) mix the hand-harvest stones `prefabs/environment/cloverHills/rocks/environment_cloverhills_pebble1-3` + `environment_rockpickup1` (~0.2 m, compiled leftovers on `rock_gunmetal_pebble`) with the pickaxe-breakable `rock_break1-4_v1` (0.9-1.5 m; ~half the members, ~300 / km²) (**Vegetation - Clover — Rock Prefabs**, empty = those eight; `rock.prefab` stays as the held-stack drop visual); **sectioned boulders** `rock_boulder1-3_v1` (~3 m, 4-6 sections; scale 0.85-1.15, cell 64 m, chance 0.12, 1-2 per cluster, 3.5 m off same-chunk trunks, 1.2 m footprint) = **29.7 clusters / km²** — they replaced the scenery boulders `cloverhills_rock1-5` at the same density (a rock that looks mineable must be); **stone spires** `rock_spire1-3_v1` (six sections, 29-40 m tall at scale 0.85-1.15, over the 22-30 m elms, sunk 1 m at the lowest of a 4.5 m footprint ring; cell 64 m, chance 0.3) **only in clearings** — the centre and a 12 m ring must be under Forest Patch Threshold, where the planner never puts a large tree (`ForestPatchField.IsClearingDisc`) ≈ one per 200 m. Boulders and spires are `WithTrees` clusters: planted with the large trees inside Tree Objects Range (330 m), kept until the chunk unloads (`LoadedChunk.HasLandmarks`; `IsLeavingTreePlan` drops only tree / beehive plans when trees hand back to the far ring). Spires are not drawn by the far ring past chunk load. The old `mountain/rocks/environment_rockspire{,2,3}` set and the knee-high `rocksmall1-4` are gone (their untracked compiled models `models/environment/environment_cloverhills_rock*/rocksmall*/rockspire*.vmdl_c` are no longer referenced).
- **Mineable rocks** (2026-11-03, Mark: "the spire should be split into 6 or so chunks ... Each section will have to be destroyed manually. The rock cannot remain floating without connecting to bottom pieces so if theyre gone the top should just explode into stone bits and rain onto the ground" + "big boulders like 3m in diameter that are like the spires too, multiple sections"): one component, `Code/Environment/MineableRock.cs`, on the prefab root; the sections are children tagged `rock_section` (renderer + static collider each, all at the rock's origin; spire sections also `grapple`). **Sections - Grounded Sections** stand on the ground, **Sections - Section Links** (`upper-lower`) say which section rests on which — both measured from the cut faces by `Blender/scripts/create_mineable_rocks.py`, which also writes the models, the LODs (100 / 200 / 300 m), the prefabs (uuid5 guids, so a re-run keeps references) and a render per variant. `ToolPickaxe` checks the aim for a `MineableRock` before the ground (so rocks mine in scenes without voxel ground too) and sends intent through `PlayerTerraform.OwnerRequestMine` (`PlayerTerraform.Rock.cs`): the host re-checks once per swing — a `Mine` tool in the active hotbar slot and not broken, the hit within reach of the eye and inside the section's bounds (+1.5 m), pickaxe stamina — then `MineableRock.HostApplyHit` counts it; wear and stamina are charged only for a hit that landed. A section breaks after **Mining - Hits Per Section** hits (rock_break 4, boulder 5, spire 8); every section that no longer rests, through others, on a grounded one goes with it. A broken section bursts on every peer into local `ChopDebris`: the rock_break's 7 pre-fractured Voronoi pieces (**Debris - Burst Pieces**) or **Debris - Bits Per Section** stone bits (`rock_bit1-5_v1`, 0.3-0.7 m × **Debris - Bit Scale**; boulder 7 × 0.9, spire 18 × 2.0) on a jittered grid through the section's box, flung at **Debris - Debris Scatter (m/s)** and gone after **Debris - Debris Lifetime (s)** (2 / 3.5 / 5 s). The host hangs the stone on the first few pieces (`ChopDebris.OnExpired`) so `resource_stone` drops appear where they come to rest: **Loot - Mined Section Stone Min/Max** (rock_break 3-5, boulder 3-5, spire 6-9) for the section the pick broke, **Loot - Collapsed Section Stone Min/Max** (boulder 2-4, spire 4-6) for each that fell. The owner sees two **Debris - Hit Chips** (the drop stones) fly off every swing (local only). The broken sections are one bit mask (`BrokenMask`) broadcast through `WorldScatterIdentity.HostBroadcastBroken` (the felled-tree path, `CombatAuthority` RPC), remembered for the session and re-applied without a burst when the chunk plants the rock again; a rock placed in a scene (no scatter key) broadcasts its own RPC. The host must have the rock loaded (same limit as hand harvest).
- **Redwood Forest** (2026-11-16, trailer pass — Mark: "redwood trees with some horizontal branches thrown in as a variation to the tree, fir trees, ferns and fiddleheads (fiddleheads are harvestable) … raspberry bush … bigger boulders … flatter ones cuz the majority of it is buried … black bear, lynx, giant spider", then "put some deer in redwoods too, they should be the most common animal"). Large trees: **Vegetation - Redwood — Tree Prefabs** (empty = redwood1–5_v2 listed twice + fir1–4_v1, `Blender/scripts/create_redwood_trees.py`). Redwood v2 (Mark 2026-11-16: "3x taller than the elm trees and thick enough to support a tree that tall … more brush at the top … concentrated enough to sorta block the sunlight … a dark and creepy forest not wide open"): 81–101 m (elms measure 26–31 m), 4.8–6.6 m trunks with a buttress flare, 850–1,225 needle cards from ~55 % up; redwood2 / 4 keep long horizontal limbs; a 20 m log per variant as thick as its trunk. Douglas firs 18–29 m. Both fell into a log that splits on `ChopableTree`, still with the elm's chop / wood numbers. The Redwood profile plans on its OWN tree grid (`BiomeScatterProfile.CellSpacingMeters` 12.8 → 5 × 5 cells per 64 m chunk, ±20 % jitter, trunks ≥ ~7.5 m apart; 16 m measured 31 trees / ha, NN 13.7 m, and Mark still found it open: "should feel like a dense ass forest"), rolling **Redwood — Density** (0.225 since 2026-11-23, halved twice the same day - Mark: "remove like 50% of the redwood trees, far far too many", then "get rid of another 50% of the big trees"; was 0.9 — the dark under the trees is now the biome air, `data/biome_atmosphere.json`) × a clump term floored at 0.85 (`ClumpFloor01`) instead of the shared Cell Spacing / Spawn Chance (the scenes run a 10 m shared cell); `Upright` keeps the trunks vertical on slopes. Ground scatter `TerrainWorldManager.Redwood.cs`: sword ferns (16 m cell, 0.7, 2–5), fiddleheads (40 m, 0.45, 1–3; hand harvest 2–3 `food_fiddlehead`, 300 s), raspberry bushes (90 m, 0.35, 1–2; 2–4 `food_raspberry` + 30 % `seed_raspberry`, three picks, 600 s) on the forage switch / density, and flat half-buried mineable slabs redwood_slab1–5_v1 (64 m, 0.3, 1–2, `create_redwood_boulders.py`) on **Redwood — Scatter Boulders**. Animals (`biome_population.json` RedwoodForest): whitetail herds every ~150 m (the most common), black bear 320 m, lynx 260 m, giant spider 220 m in groups of 2–3; `blackbear` / `lynx` / `giantspider` prefabs and clips from `create_black_bear.py` / `create_lynx.py` / `create_giant_spider.py`, `AnimalSpecies.GiantSpider` = 21 with the wolf's behaviour numbers at the spider clips' speeds (walk 1.2 / sneak 1.4 / run 7 m/s), silk loot; bear and lynx got pelt / meat loot rows.
- Dev: **`world_find <kind> [radius m] [park]`** lists the nearest planned clusters of any kind (`wheat`, `spire`, `boulder`, `rock`, `daisy` …) or `camp` sites from the camera with a ready `terrain_cam` line (`park` parks the fly camera at the nearest).

### Structure breaching (entities vs build pieces)

- **Pieces have hit points**: `materials[].health` in `Assets/data/build_pieces.json` (wood 100) → `[Sync] BuildPiece.Health`, seeded on the host at placement / start. Furniture without a `materialId` has none. **Only entities damage structures** — `CombatAuthority.MayApplyMeleeDamageFromAttackerToReceiver` refuses build pieces for attackers without an `EntityBrain`, so a player's sword on a wall is not a hit (no swing consumed, no durability); players use the hammer. Every destructible build prefab carries a `DamageReceiver` (authored on the asset); `DamageReceiver.TakeDamage` routes to `BuildPiece.HostApplyDamage`, and at 0 HP the piece leaves through `BuildAuthority.HostRemovePiece` — the same nav rebake + structural re-solve as a hammer demolish, so knocking out a load-bearing piece collapses what it held. Hammer hover readout shows `HP x / max` next to support.
- **`EnemyAiState.Breaching`** (`EntityBrain.Breach.cs`): entered from Chasing the moment a **fresh** mesh has no complete route to where the player stands (goal off nav, path missing / partial, or through a solid — `StartNotFound` is our own footing and does not count), after `BreachAfterBlockedSeconds` (0.3 s). A wedged fallback (complete route, body not moved for `ChaseWedgedSeconds`) remains. The 72 u body-height in-the-way probe (`RememberPieceInWay`) only remembers a piece ahead as the preferred target; the same probe while walking to a breach stand retargets onto whatever blocks the walk. Then it walks to a stand beside the piece (nav-projected probe off each horizontal face, must be inside `StructureAttackRange` = 60 u of the surface with a complete path) and hits it **until it is dead**. `EntityLocomotion.ClipMovementAgainstSolids` keeps the body sphere a full radius off any wall and treats a started-solid sweep as blocked — a flush start returned a zero normal that read as "nothing to slide on", which was how entities tunnelled through walls. Breach decisions log as `[Breach] <entity>: …`, piece damage as `[BuildPiece] …`, executed rebakes as `[BuildNav] …` (read `sbox/logs/sbox-dev.log`). Then it walks to a stand point beside a piece (nav-projected probe off each horizontal face, must still be inside `StructureAttackRange` = 60 u of the surface with a *complete* path), hits it `BreachSwingsPerPiece` = **3** times (counted on `EntityCombat.AttackCycleFinished`), marks it recently-hit (20 s) and moves to the next piece — so a whole building comes down over time. All candidates hit → next lap over the same pieces.
- **Plain nav only** (Mark, 2026-09-09): the mesh is correct, so pathing finds doors and stairs by itself. The two-leg detour search and the drop-in (walk above the player and jump down) were removed with their files — they produced the "runs off, turns round, comes back" whenever the mesh was stale. **Breach trigger is immediate**: on a fresh mesh, no complete route to where the player stands (goal off nav, path missing / partial, or through a solid) → pick a piece after `BreachAfterBlockedSeconds` (0.3 s, absorbs one bad query). A wedged fallback (complete route but the body has not moved for `ChaseWedgedSeconds`) remains. **Stay on the piece until it is dead**; wood `health` is 30 so a piece dies in three scav hits (10 each) — "3 hits" is material health, not a swing counter.
- **Baked tiles never saw the buildings**: nav tiles cache a heightfield, and in a scene that loads baked navdata (testscene1) that cache comes from the bake, not live geometry (`NavMeshTile.IsBakedHeightField`). A plain `GenerateTiles` rebuilt tiles from that cache, so placed walls never carved and upper floors never got a mesh (`nav_probe` on a floor at z≈102: "nav at feet NO" while nav bounds reached z 40000). A full `Generate(physics)` does rasterise the buildings (`nav_regen` → "nav at feet YES"); incremental `GenerateTiles` never carved a fresh wall in this scene even after every tile was live (36 "path leaks through a solid" after the load-time generate). Hand-built scenes also get a **nav bubble** (Mark): the load-time pass waits up to 15 s for a player pawn, then sets custom bounds ±6000 u (150 m) around it — the scene's whole-world bounds (1 km²) made every structure regenerate ~5 s. Beyond the bubble there is no nav in a test scene. So in **hand-built scenes** (no `TerrainWorldManager`) a **structural** change (piece placed / removed — never a spawn-settle or chunk request, those are dropped there) runs one **full** regenerate (`BuildNavMeshSync.RegenerateFullFromPhysics`, ~0.7 s in testscene1, logged as "full regenerate finished in Ns"), debounced 1.5 s after the last placement (`StructuralDebounceSeconds`) and capped 6 s after the first, removals 0.5 s in `full` mode; in `tiles` / `request` mode (cheap) 0.25 s / 1 s cap / removals 0.15 s so the mesh tracks placements almost live (each full regenerate is a visible hitch — per Mark "super laggy when they renav", so a building session is one bake), one at a time (a request landing mid-generate waits), **unloading only the tiles around the change, then `Generate`**: Generate rebuilds missing tiles from physics and reuses every cached one — no unload was 0.7 s but stale (no mesh on any new piece, not even a ground floor slab), unloading everything was correct but 6 s over this scene's 1 km² bounds; local unload is both correct and quick. Settle requests cannot re-trigger it. While the mesh is **stale** — a structural rebake queued (debounce) or a generate running, `BuildNavMeshSync.IsNavStale` — a chasing entity tracks the player on foot and a breaching one walks to its stand on foot (`TickManualStepToward`, which sweeps its own body sphere and slides along walls — the locomotion clip measures from an origin the feet-glue resets every frame, so it never saw this step and entities walked through walls; **no time cap** — the old 8 s cap froze every entity once the player spam-placed pieces, since each placement extends the stale window) instead of sprinting in place on a frozen agent, and no detour / leak logic runs off the stale mesh: a path through a wall placed a moment ago is not a leak, it is a mesh that has not caught up (that was "runs off, turns round, comes back"). Measured: a structure regenerate takes ~5 s in testscene1 even with a local unload — `Generate` is not cheap; `nav_regen_local [half]` (unload + `GenerateTiles` around the player) is the A/B for a faster incremental path. The load-time pass (`EnsureLiveNavOnce`, retried until the nav bounds are non-empty) and `nav_regen` do the same. Treating settle requests as regenerates produced a loop: off-nav entity → settle bake → regenerate → tiles rebuilding → still off-nav → …, one regenerate per second. `nav_mode full|tiles|request` (`BuildNavMeshSync.RebakeMode`) switches the structure rebake at runtime: `full` = whole-mesh Generate after a local unload (known correct, one hitch), **`tiles` (default, per Mark — regenerating the whole plane for one wall is a waste)** = unload + `GenerateTiles` for the tiles around the change, `request` = unload + `RequestTilesGeneration` (the engine rebuilds those tiles over frames, no single hitch). `TickTileVerify` samples the baked area 1.5 s after a tile rebake; if it holds no nav (seen once while the mesh was still baked) it logs a warning, regenerates fully and keeps `Full` for the session. There is no engine call to "notify an obstacle" without regenerating tiles. Streamed worlds keep tile bakes. Console `nav_show [radius]` draws nav samples around the player for 12 s (the engine's `DrawMesh` only renders in the editor). **Never `UnloadTiles` before an incremental `GenerateTiles`**: unloaded tiles do not come back, leaving holes that `EnsureAgentOnNavMesh` re-projected agents out of (the 10 m teleport). `EnsureNavBoundsCover` still raises the Z extent when a bake reaches the ceiling. Console: `nav_draw 1|0`, `nav_probe`, `nav_regen [extraHeight]` (full unload + regenerate — small scenes only), `nav_radius <r>` (agent radius, then `nav_regen`).
- **The chase-only crawl** (trace: `Wander real=88 wish=88` fine, `Chasing real=37 wish=220 max=220`, `nav` flipping): `NavMeshAgent.IsNavigating` reads false for the frames after a `MoveTo` while the path is computed. `RunPathCheckTo` took that as "not navigating" and re-issued `MoveTo` every think, so the agent never settled; in those gaps `EntityLocomotion.UpdateBodyFacing` turned the body toward the look target (the player) instead of the path, and when the path resumed the forward-only gate crept at ~17 % for the misalignment — 37 u/s. Fix: a MoveTo issued within `MoveIssueSettleSeconds` (0.6 s) to a goal within 96 u counts as already navigating, and facing keeps the last wish heading for 0.6 s across the gap. Wander never showed it because it has no look target.
- **A MoveTo from off-mesh does nothing**: trace showed 20 s of `block=moveIssued real=0 wish=220 nav=False` standing on a floor tile and on the stairs — the agent's internal position had been nudged off the mesh (clip / landing) and every `MoveTo` silently failed. `RunPathCheckTo` now re-places the agent beside the body (`EnsureAgentOnNavMesh`) when the previous MoveTo had its settle time and the agent still is not navigating (`block=agentReplaced`). The trace carries `agentOff` (agent vs body distance), `targetOff` and `bodyOnNav` to prove it.
- **A failed re-snap must never park the agent**: landing (`EntityLocomotion.FinishFall`), bake-complete and Landed (`EntityBrain`) used to leave `NavMeshAgent.UpdatePosition` off when `EnsureAgentOnNavMesh` failed — harmless while that snap searched 1024 u, fatal once it became strict: paths kept being issued, the body never moved, and the wedged fallback breached every 9 s with `status=Complete` (the "slowly walks / lags" regression). Now the snap tries a tight box then a 2.5× wider one, and on failure the agent keeps driving from where it stands (it clamps to the nearest poly). Any `EnterState` ends manual stale-mesh tracking (`EndManualChase`) so `UpdatePosition` is restored across state changes. `nav_trace 1` logs a `[Trace]` movement snapshot per entity every 0.5 s (state, real / wish / max speed, agent flags, stale, path status, block reason).
- **No snapping across walls after a rebake**: `EntityNavMeshUtility.EnsureAgentOnNavMesh` (called on every `OnNavBakeComplete`, landing, settle) now only moves an agent within a ±48 u tight box that is reachable without crossing a solid; it used to take a random sample out to 1024 u and set `WorldPosition`, which yanked an entity across a freshly placed wall or 5 m away when the wall it was hitting fell ("walks clean through", "teleports away"). Spawn placement passes `maxSnap: 1024` and keeps the wide search.
- **Lone upper floors vanish** (probe: ground YES, stair YES, floor NO): Recast drops isolated regions under `Config.MinRegionArea` voxels. A single 2 m floor loses its edge ring to the ledge filter and 16 u to agent-radius erosion; at a coarse voxel size what remains is below the minimum and is culled, while stairs merge into the ground region and survive. `CellSize` / `CellHeight` / the region thresholds are documented but **not accessible** from game code (CS1061); `AgentRadius` is — erosion is radius-based and Recast derives cell size from it, so a smaller radius keeps more of the piece. Verify with `nav_radius 10` → `nav_regen` → `nav_probe` on the floor before baking a value into `EnsureBuildTraversalSettings`.
- **Piece choice** (per Mark): candidates are only pieces that relate to the **player** — on the eye→player line (what they are behind), the piece the entity is pushing against on the way to them (`NotifyChasePhysicsBlocked`, 3 s memory, and only if it is closer to the player than the entity is), or **part of the structure the player stands on** (`FindPieceUnder` → `BuildStructuralIntegrity.GetConnectedPieces`, the solver's touching-neighbour closure; event-driven, once per pick); the `BreachSearchRadius` (400 u) rule only applies when the player is on the ground. An unconnected structure beside the one they stand on is never a target. Never a piece that is merely near the entity: the other half of a split base, or the house next to the tree the player grappled into, gets no swings. Score = flat distance **from the player** − 3 × Support ("what is holding up the weight"); when the player is above melee reach, pieces under their feet within 150 u come first (the supports of what they stand on). No candidate → no breach, the chase carries on. The chosen piece is re-tested every recheck (`IsPieceStillRelevant`): the moment the player moves to another structure it re-picks at once rather than finishing the wall. Only pieces intersecting the swing band (12–90 u above the feet) qualify, so floors underfoot / overhead are never targets — the walls and beams under an upper floor are.
- **Crowd rules** (Mark, 2026-09-10): `BreachClaims` (host static registry — every brain runs on the host, so this is the "is this piece available?" check; no RPC) allows **three entities per piece**; a fourth takes another piece of the same structure. A claim carries a slot (0..2) that offsets the stand along the piece face (0 / +38 / −38 u) so claimants spread out instead of shoving each other through the wall. Claims release on piece change, on leaving Breaching, on death and on destroy; dead or re-targeted claimants are pruned on query. **Nothing to breach → go home**: no route to the player and no structure relating to them for `ReturnHomeAfterSeconds` (3 s) → `ReturnHome`: alert cleared, walk to `HomePosition`, then the normal idle/wander (`[Breach] … returning home`). "Every candidate piece is full" is not "nothing to breach" — it waits.
- **Leaving**: every 1 s (and immediately when any piece changes or the entity takes a hit) it re-asks: LOS + in reach → Attacking; complete path to the player that does not cross a solid (`PathCrossesSolid` — physics is the judge, not the "too short = leaking" length heuristic, which rejected the real route through a fresh hole for a whole lap) → Chasing. **After its own piece goes down it holds** until that kill's rebake has actually landed (bake-complete that started ≥0.25 s after the kill; cap 8 s; never while `IsNavGenerating`) — piece removals schedule an *urgent* bake (0.2 s, `BuildNavMeshSync.OnBuildPieceBoundsChanged`) and `OnNavBakeComplete` triggers the recheck at once — so the chase resumes through the hole instead of the next piece being picked off the stale mesh (the 1.5 s hold lost that race against a 6 s regenerate). The piece is tracked by health regardless of who breaks it. The 30 s unseen clock is held during breaching (being behind cover is the point); the chase only drops when the player leaves `ChaseAbandonRange` or dies. Nothing hittable → back to Chasing, where blocked detection re-arms.
- **Population** (`Assets/data/biome_population.json`, `BiomePopulationCatalog` / `BiomePopulationScatter`): density by world-grid `spacingMeters` + `spawnWeight` per biome (not per-chunk); `respawn: false` = mini-boss (permanent death). Hooked from `TerrainWorldManager` on chunk load **inside collision range**. Clover Hills default: ~1 `scavT1` per **250 m**. Optional `near` anchor reserved for later. A row with `"camp"` stamps a whole enemy camp per cell instead (*Enemy camps* below). `BiomePopulationCatalog` / `EnemyCampCatalog` re-read their JSON at every world load (`BeginWorldLoad`) - the editor keeps statics across play sessions and hotloads, so a JSON edit used to wait for an editor restart.
- **terrainTest lighting** (2026-10-13): the FlyCamera carries the lightingTest post stack (Tonemapping HableFilmic, auto exposure 1–3, comp 0.1; Bloom additive 0.25 / threshold 1; AmbientOcclusion 1.5 / radius 64), an `AerialPerspective` on the DayNightCycle object wired to the cycle's Aerial perspective slot (2026-11-08; it replaced the root `Fog` GradientFog 0.72,0.76,0.84 / 1–12 km — see ENVIRONMENT.md "Aerial perspective"), and the Sun's volumetric fog off — `cycle_toggle` then gives the same look as lightingTest. The DayNightCycle group ships ENABLED since 2026-10-21 (Mark: the short daylight cycle should be running; the river water mirrors the dome's colours and needs it).
- **World grass** (`TerrainWorldManager.Grass.cs`): one engine Clutter component in infinite mode, 307 m ring of 25.6 m tiles, clumps planted on the chunks' own height grids, Clover Hills only; `grass_blade.shader` thins by camera distance (full 80 m, 15 % kept by 170 m, culled at 300 m). Grass tiles are invalidated only when a chunk FIRST meshes — never on a LOD remesh (promote 128 m / demote 256 m), which blanked the clumps for a frame and read as pop-in while travelling (2026-10-13).
- **Grass is lit like the ground (Mark 2026-10-21: "it should have the same properties as the terrain so it doesn't stand out")**: `GrassScatterer` tilts every clump instance onto the ground normal (the height field returns the full normal); `grass_blade.shader` stands the blades back upright about the root and lights them with that normal the way `pixel_lit` lights `terrain_ground.vmat` — Lambert sun + local lights (6 bands), no specular, roughness 1, engine ambient + AO. Only Base / Tip colour and Root Darkening differ from the ground. `TiltWithGround`, `NormalUp` and `GrassRoughness` are gone.
- **Generated objects are never scene content (2026-10-20)**: every GameObject the world manager creates from the seed — `TerrainChunk`, its `veg_*` clones and `RiverWater`, `TerrainFar`, `FarTrees`, `WorldGrass`, `RuntimeAuthorities`, the `FlashProbe` — carries `GameObjectFlags.NotSaved`. A save made while play mode was running (2026-10-19 19:51) wrote the whole streamed world into terrainTest.scene (7 MB: 127 chunks, 812 far chunks, 1270 trees); the saved copies had no runtime meshes and loaded as green walls, and the editor showed trees. The scene must stay empty: the world is produced at play from the seed. If a scene ever shows chunks or trees in the hierarchy, strip them (the 2026-10-20 cleanup script pattern: drop objects named TerrainChunk*/TerrainFar*/FarTrees/WorldGrass/RuntimeAuthorities) and find who created them without the flag.
- **Tree / prop object ranges (2026-10-20, Mark: "FPS is trash, 200 fps regardless of mode")**: large trees exist as GameObjects only on chunks within **Vegetation - Tree Objects Range (m)** (330 = Tree LOD 3 At, so the hand-over to the instanced ring swaps identical LOD3 meshes); a chunk entering the range plants them (`ScatterLargeTreesOnChunk` → spawn → hand-over at the last clone), a chunk a chunk-width past it hands them back (`BeginTreesLeaving`: the ring draws at once, the objects go `FarUnloadOverlapFrames` later, `TickTreesLeaving`). Rocks / sticks are planted only once their chunk is within **Prop Objects Range (m)** (192 = collision range) and stay until the chunk unloads (they are pickups). `PopulateChunk(..., largeTrees, props)` carries the flags. `WorldScatterIdentity` now REMEMBERS felled stages and harvested nodes for the session (`BrokenStages` / `DepletedKeys`, `ReapplyRemembered` right after `Configure` in `SpawnPlanned`, cleared in `UnloadAll`): a re-planted tree comes back as its stump — before this a felled tree regrew on every chunk unload / reload. Objects at spawn: 1 508 → 482 trees, 714 → 107 rocks, 409 → 74 sticks. The ring list is rebuilt every 6 frames (`FarRebuildEveryFrames`; 2 cost 5-11 ms per rebuild at 10 k instances while flying). **Far-ring landmarks (2026-11-03, Mark: the spires "pop in from too close up")**: a `PropClusterOptions.FarRing` cluster (the stone spires) is planned by the far planner too (`EnumerateFarRingLandmarks`: the same `ScatterPropClusters` run on the worker into its own `[ThreadStatic] _plans`, so position, lean and scale match the real one) and drawn as the prefab's `_far.vmdl` with the plan's full rotation (`FarTree.Rotation`); a sectioned rock's `_far.vmdl` is the whole rock's (`rock_spireN_v1_far.vmdl`: every section's lod3, no physics, written by create_mineable_rocks.py `write_far_vmdl`). `AuthoredScale` is locked now that workers reach it.
- **Frame-time ceiling, measured 2026-10-20 (uncapped `fps_max 0` + `fps_max_inactive 0`, fly camera, spawn forest, 26.10.13a, in-editor)**: standing 98-118 fps (8.4-10 ms), flying 55 m/s 83 fps (9.9 ms, worst 20-24). CPU: Render 4.0 ms (scene submission + 4 sun cascades: sun shadows off saves 1.9), UI 1.8 (HUD), Update 1.2 (manager ~1.1), Editor 0.8 (editor overhead, gone in a standalone build), Animation 0.7 (AI off saves 1.0); GPU 5.5-7.9 ms (grass ClutterBatch ~1.4, far ring 0.7, impostors 0.25, the rest opaque scene + shadows + AO + bloom). 200 fps needs ≤5 ms on BOTH: not reachable in-editor with the current HUD, 4 shadow cascades, AO, bloom and distance AI animating; the levers are a standalone build, 2 cascades / shorter shadow distance, pausing animgraphs beyond ~100 m, HUD cost, grass density. `terrain scatter hidden` in `perf_bisect` also hides the far ring + impostors, so its saving is mostly theirs, not the objects'.
- **Far impostors are BAKED into the far chunks (2026-10-20, replaces the instanced card list)**: the far-tier worker plans the cards (`PlanFarImpostors`, chunk-local) and turns them into one vertex / index array pair (`BuildFarCardGeometry`, 20 verts / 30 indices per card from `FarCardTemplate`); `FinishFarChunk` makes a second `Mesh` with `materials/environment/cloverHills/trees/far_tree_card.vmat` and `BuildModelFromGeometry(..., extraMesh)` adds it to the SAME model as the ground, so the far chunk's renderer carries no `MaterialOverride` any more (each mesh keeps its own material). Zero per-frame CPU: the 32 k-instance `DrawModelInstanced` list cost 1.45 ms every frame (perf_bisect). Per renderer attributes, read only by materials with `PixelFarCard 1` (pixel_lit): `FarCardFade` (Bayer screen-door, same code path as TreeFade) and `FarCardTint` (gamma multiply on the vertex paint). `TickFarCardFades` fades a chunk's cards toward SHOWN when its centre is past Far Trees Range − half a far chunk and toward HIDDEN inside it (a far chunk overlaps the near zone; without this the baked cards stood among the real trees) over Far Trees — Fade (s), re-asserting the attribute every 30 frames (a hotload drops it). Card normals arch outward + up (a crown's upper half), not straight up. `Far Impostor Tint` (0.576 / 0.591 / 0.678) comes from **`far_card_calibrate apply`** (Code/World/Terrain/TerrainFarCardCalibrator.cs): three 640×360 captures a few frames apart (as is / ring hidden / cards hidden) attribute every changed pixel to the ring or the cards and print cover % + mean RGB per screen band; `apply` multiplies the tint by (ring / card)^(1/2.2) — run it 4-5 times (the tonemapper is not linear) until the ratio prints ~0.98. Run it from a still camera that sees the ring / card boundary (terrain_cam … 260 m up). Mark 2026-10-20: "see the transition??? its so bad" — the cards had up normals and no tint, so they rendered twice as bright as the lit LOD3 crowns.
- **Far cards are textured + normal-mapped, and calibrated in two halves (2026-10-20, Mark: "VERY square ... the way the lighting hits them makes them clearly not a part of the other trees")**: `Blender/scripts/create_far_card_textures.py` (plain Python + numpy, no Blender) writes `materials/environment/cloverHills/trees/far_tree_card_{color,mask,normal}.png` (256²): rows 0-191 a ragged round crown blob (ellipse + sinusoid rim + bites, leaf-clump colour noise from the elm leaf percentiles), rows 205-255 a bark strip; the normal map is mostly RANDOM leaf facings per 3 px clump (hemisphere around +z) with a 0.35 sphere bias — the crown is a cloud of leaf cards and that distribution, not a smooth sphere, decides how it takes the sun at every hour (a smooth sphere measured 0.5x-1.5x the ring through the cycle). Each quad carries its own U tangent, its face normal and white vertex colour; `far_tree_card.vmat` = pixel_lit, alpha-tested, `PixelFarCard 1`, specular / roughness like the leaf material. **Why one tint could never work**: a flat card takes ~6x the DIRECT light a sparse LOD3 crown does per unit of albedo, but both take ambient 1:1 — a tint that matched noon left the cards 2x too dark at night. So pixel_lit has `FarCardDirect` (scales the card's direct diffuse only; `Far Impostor Direct Light` 0.166) next to `FarCardTint` (`Far Impostor Tint` 1.015 / 0.939 / 1.048, ≈ 1: the texture colours were right). Calibrate with the cycle ON and PAUSED: `cycle_night` → `far_card_calibrate apply tint` ×4 (ambient half), `cycle_day` → `far_card_calibrate apply direct` ×6 (sun half), repeat once. Measured after: midnight 1.02-1.04, noon 0.92 / 1.00 / 0.94, late afternoon 0.77 / 0.93 / 0.77 (low orange sun: the cards still take ~25 % more of it in R/B — the one residual), dusk crossover noisy. Far cards also skip screen-space AO in the shader (the ring draws after the AO pass and is never occluded; the cards are in the prepass and occluded themselves) — necessary, not sufficient.
- **Leaf direct-light cap + the three card knobs (2026-10-20, Mark: "a lot of white in them when I'm up high")**: pixel_lit `PixelDirectMax` caps N·L for sun and local lights; `elm_leaves_v76.vmat` (and the template in create_elm_tree.py) sets 0.55 so a leaf card facing the sun square-on no longer takes the full 3.2x sun and tonemaps to white from above. Far cards then needed re-fitting, and the fit now has three per-renderer knobs: `Far Impostor Direct Light` (0.15 under the scene's fixed sun; the cycle's tilted noon wanted 0.5 — the response to sun angle is the residual, sun / moon diffuse only), `Far Impostor Ambient Light` (0.78, sky ambient only — a card's horizontal face normal takes more sky than a crown's every-way leaves) and `Far Impostor Tint` (white). **Calibrate on the boundary band only**: `far_card_calibrate` now averages only the screen bands that hold both ring and cards (marked *), because fog legitimately blues the 2.4-8 km cards more than the 0.3-2.5 km ring — the whole-screen ratio read 0.75 / 0.88 / 0.69 by day while the boundary read 0.96 / 0.94 / 0.98. Also: the calibrator holds the wind (leaves sway between captures), takes four captures (as is / ring off / cards off / both off) with a 4-level change threshold and a consistency test, and waits 16 frames after each ring toggle (the ring list rebuilds every 10). Measure 25 s after `cycle_day` / `cycle_night` — the sun blends over TransitionSeconds. Card material: specular 0, roughness 1 (specular 1 made no measurable difference).
- **Far cards stand on the real trees (2026-10-20, Mark: "pick out a tree from 3 km away and fly toward it")**: `PlanFarCards` runs `TerrainVegetationScatter.EnumerateTrees` — the scatter's and the LOD3 ring's own planner — for the 16 near chunks inside each far chunk on the far-tier worker, so every card is one real tree at its exact position, yaw and scale (card = 22 m x the tree's ScaleMul). The cover-based planner (`PlanFarImpostors`, `Far Impostors Per Cell`, cover from `ApplyFarCanopyTint`) is gone. Card -> ring -> object hand-overs are therefore the same tree all the way in.
- **Tree LOD jitter**: each tree scales its LOD distances by 0.9-1.1 (`TreeLodState.Jitter`, hashed from its position) so a forest never swaps levels as one wave when the camera rises straight up; the cross-fade is 0.5 s (was 0.35).
- **Fades are only for things without LODs (Mark 2026-10-20: "trees should be always present ... spawn in the new one THEN remove the old, no fade")**: a large tree's LOD change is a one-frame model swap on its renderer (no gap, no dither — the cross-fade, `TreeLodFadeSeconds`, `tree_lod_fade`, the second renderer and the shader's `TreeFadeInvert` are deleted); the far ring and the cards hand over hard with their overlaps (**Far Trees — Fade (s)** is 0). Boulders, spires and beehives dither-fade in over **Small Object Fade (s)** (0.5) when they spawn (`BeginSmallFadeIn` / `TickSmallFades`); saplings fade in at Sapling Visible Range and out past it (`SaplingState.Fade`), and since 2026-11-03 so do the small pickups — rocks, sticks, forage — at **Pickup Visible Range (m)** 100 (`IsPickupPlan`, `LoadedChunk.Pickups`, the shared `TickRangeVisibility`; Mark: "Yes, at 100 m"). They were planted out to Prop Objects Range and stayed drawn until the chunk unloaded; measured saving ~0.15 ms for ~255 renderers. A pickup switched back on re-applies its remembered harvest / break (`WorldScatterIdentity.ReapplyRemembered`). Prefab materials must be pixel_lit for the dither to show; others just pop.
- **Per-frame costs the 2026-10-20 suite found (and the rule each one teaches)**: `EquipmentCatalog` / `MeleeWeaponClassCatalog` re-read and hashed their JSON on EVERY `EnsureLoaded` (1.1 ms + 36 MB/s of string garbage with the pawn standing, through `HasGrappleEquipped` from the HUD) — catalogs check the file at most every `JsonChangeCheckSeconds` (2 s), like the build and crafting catalogs already did. `InventoryScreenPointer.TryResolveViewCamera` walked every ancestor's whole subtree for a camera; from a HUD object that reaches the scene root it was a full-scene walk every frame a menu or prompt held the cursor — it caches the camera per source object now. `EnemyHealthBar` ran a `PlayerVitals` scene query twice per bar per frame — one static lookup every 0.25 s. `PlayerScreenHud` has `hud_profile 1` (allocation-free struct scopes) that prints each sub-tick's ms/frame once a second; `perf_scenario N` parks the fly camera in suite row N and holds it so perf_cpu / perf_gpu / perf_bisect can dissect one row. The grapple control-scheme prompt (`GrappleControlSchemeStore.RequestChoice`) opens on EVERY grapple equip, saved choice or not — that is what the pawn row of the suite measures (prompt open, cursor captured).
- **Grass GPU cost = the clutter ring, and the ring is 12 tiles (2026-10-20)**: `ClutterComponent.Infinite` ignores `ClutterDefinition.TileRadius` and streams 12 tiles around the camera (565 generation jobs at 40 / 150 / 300 m alike), so **Grass — Range (m)** picks the TILE SIZE (`range / 12`, snapped to the engine options → 77 / 153 / 307 / 614 m rings); default 150 m (ring 153 m, half the ground clutter cost: 3.9 → 2.2 ms GPU on the two passes) until 2026-11-19, now **77 m** (Mark: "halve the grass ring but make the blades fade in instead of a hard pop"; code default + main / terrainTest scenes). `grass_blade.vmat` thinning moved inside the ring (Thin Full 35 / Far 58 / Far Keep 0.35 / Cull 68 m) and a clump crossing its keep threshold now grows out of the ground over `ThinGrowBand` (0.15 of the keep share) instead of appearing whole. Every clump in the ring runs the vertex shader even when thinned / culled (500 m straight down with the old 307 m ring: 5.8 ms of collapsed blades), so `grass_blade.shader` now returns right after the keep test for culled clumps — the branch is uniform per clump.
- **`perf_suite` is the fps acceptance test (Mark 2026-10-20)**: six scripted scenarios from the fly camera's spot (`TerrainTestFlyCamera.BeginScript` turns the heading at a rate, holds metres-above-terrain or an absolute height), 1.5 s warm-up + 5 s measured each, PASS / FAIL against 5.0 ms. Since 2026-11-04 every frame is scored as max(CPU work, GPU) — CPU work = wall time minus the engine's `Idle` timing (the vsync / fps_max wait; `PerformanceStats.FrameTime` read from game code is the WALL time), GPU = `PerformanceStats.GpuFrametime` — and the table has cpu / gpu columns, so a vsynced window still gives real numbers; `perf_bisect` / `perf_types` report CPU and GPU savings the same way. Park the fly camera at the scene start pose first: the suite anchors wherever the camera is. Run it with the caps lifted and the mouse untouched; the pawn row spawns and removes the pawn itself. Tree LOD distances are now 180 / 270 / 330 m so a swap happens where the tree is small on screen (Mark: hard swaps near the camera were "so distracting").
- **Saplings pop at one distance (2026-10-20)**: `Sapling Visible Range (m)` 150 on the manager (50 → 80 on 2026-10-21, → 200 then 150 on 2026-10-22: Mark wanted 2-3x as far, then "way too many" from above). The small-tree layer is planted by distance, not mesh detail (`WantsSaplings`): once a chunk has its large trees and its centre is within the range + ¾ chunk, far-LOD or not (saplings sit on the sampled height, not the mesh). `SpawnPlan.Sapling` instances spawn DISABLED and `TickSaplingVisibility` enables each one only while the camera is within the range (3-D distance, same in and out, no hysteresis — Mark: the chunk-promotion distance was "massive hysteresis"). Chunks past range + ¾ chunk are skipped once hidden. Saplings never cast shadows or have colliders.
- **Authoring**: the far card is NOT a per-tree asset. It is one generic textured crown the runtime scales (`Far Impostor Height (m)`, hash 0.85-1.25) and tints; a new tree model needs nothing for the cards — only its `<model>_far.vmdl` for the LOD3 ring (create_elm_tree.py writes it). A model-faithful card (pine silhouette, autumn colour) would mean baking the card textures from that model: either an in-engine bake (SceneWorld + SceneCamera RenderToTexture from 2-4 sides, colour + mask, normals by three-light photometric stereo) or a Blender camera render — not built; the generic blob is right for the elm at 2.5-8 km (1-6 px per tree).
- **Far ring rebuild is dirty-driven (2026-10-20)**: `BuildFarCommandList` runs only when `_farDirty` — a plan cached / evicted, a hand-over frame reached (`_farHandoverDue`), a chunk `Unloading` / `TreesLeaving`, a fade in progress, the camera a chunk-quarter away or 10° turned from the last build — at most every `FarRebuildMinFrames` (10) and at least every 90. Standing still it runs about never; flying it is the remaining managed garbage (the engine copies every transform span into a fresh `Transform[]` per draw, ~0.5 MB per rebuild at 11 k instances — every 3 frames that was 50 MB / 4 s and 8 ms GC pauses). The gather tests distance / back-cull BEFORE the `_loaded` lookup (10 k cached plans, most beyond the range).
- **Entity ranges, two pairs (Mark 2026-11-04: "Let them leave the world! They should despawn at a certain range, all entities should!", then "Make all ferals/ascendants at 300/350m and animals can be 100/90 for now")**: `EntityChunkMemory` holds the only copies, each a despawn / return pair in metres — **enemies** (`EntityBrain`, every `EntityKind`: feral, robot, ascended) `EnemyDespawnRangeMeters` 350 / `EnemyReturnRangeMeters` 300 (what scavs already did; `EntityBrain`'s `Sleep Range (m)` property is gone), **animals** (`AnimalBrain.Dormancy.cs`) `AnimalDespawnRangeMeters` 100 / `AnimalReturnRangeMeters` 90; a record picks its pair by `Species` (`DespawnRangeMetersFor` / `ReturnRangeMetersFor`). Awake past the despawn range from every viewer → memory or sleep; asleep / remembered → back inside the return range (the gap keeps the 2 Hz checks from thrashing; on streamed terrain the return is also capped at `Collision Range (m)` 192, so enemies really return at 192 m). Biome population never clones a cell that is already past its despawn range: `EntityChunkMemory.TryRememberUnspawned` files it as a full-health record (`CurrentHealth` 0 = full, home = the spot) — in practice animals only, since population runs inside Collision Range — and a plain population respawn out of range does the same (camp guards respawn live and go to memory on their own first check, leash kept). Fly-around 1.6 km square at 55 m/s with every entity at 100 m: animals 23 → 159 and climbing before, flat 0-7 after.
- **Entity chunk memory (Code/Entity/EntityChunkMemory.cs, Mark 2026-11-02: scavs not tracking a player and outside the player-entity range write their location and health to server memory and despawn)**: the same 2 Hz range check has two outcomes. An entity with no viewer in range that is NOT tracking a player (`EntityBrain.IsTrackingPlayer` = has a target or is Searching / Chasing / Attacking / Retreating / Breaching / Raiding) and is not stunned, dead, a raider (ever — `_wasRaider`, the raid session's straggler rule owns them) or a boss (`BossEntity`, its own abandon despawn) is handed to `EntityChunkMemory` (`GameObjectSystem`, host only) as an `EntityMemoryRecord` — prefab (`EntityBrain.SourcePrefabPath`, set by `EntityEnemySetup.Configure`'s new `prefabPath` argument from every spawner: population, camps, raids, bosses, spawn button), type, tier, max + current health, world position, yaw, home, leash, biome population slot (key / respawn / delay) — filed under its terrain chunk (`TerrainChunkStreaming.WorldToChunkCoord`; a 64 m grid in scenes without a `TerrainWorldManager`), then destroyed. A population slot is marked `Remembered` in `BiomePopulationRegistry` so a chunk reload does not spawn a second scav at the slot's origin (cleared on restore / death). The system ticks at 2 Hz: a record whose spot has a viewer within its return range (enemies 300 m, animals 90 m; capped at `Collision Range (m)` on streamed terrain) AND standable ground under it (physics trace — on streamed terrain that is the chunk collider coming online inside Collision Range, exactly where fresh population spawns) is cloned back: `BiomePopulationScatter.ClonePrefab` → `Configure( …, record.MaxHealth, prefabPath )` → `SetHomePosition` / `SetLeash` → `EntityVitals.RestoreHealth( current )` (also survives a `ResetToFull` from a late OnStart) → slot re-bound → `HostNetworkSpawn`, at most 2 restores per tick. Viewers are every player pawn, or the scene camera when there is none (shared helper `EntityChunkMemory.AnyViewerWithin`). In-memory only: `TerrainWorldManager.UnloadAll` clears it with the population registry; `entity_memory` dumps it. An entity that IS tracking a player (or has no source prefab) beyond the range still sleeps as before:
- **Entity animation on clients** (Mark 2026-11-12, MP playtest: scavs slid around): `EntityLocomotion` steps and animates the body on the host only; a proxy runs `TickProxyAnim` — the citizen helper gets the velocity the synced root is seen moving at (eased), always grounded. Facing is the synced root rotation.
- **Entity dormancy (Code/Entity/EntityBrain.Dormancy.cs, 2026-10-20)** — the sleep half of the check above: no player pawn (or the scene camera, when there is no pawn) within `EnemyDespawnRangeMeters` (350 m) → `EntityLocomotion`, `NavMeshAgent` and every `SkinnedModelRenderer` off, the brain's update returns right after the 2 Hz staggered check; raiders, stunned and dead entities never sleep; wakes once a viewer is within `EnemyReturnRangeMeters` (300 m). perf_types measured 19 idle scavs at 0.6 ms locomotion + 0.6 ms skinned bodies per frame with nobody near them (UseAnimGraph off alone saved nothing — the bone update is the cost). Host only (proxies already skip locomotion).
- **Overhead nameplates** are drawn by the local pawn's HUD only (no pawn → none): the old per-entity world panels kept all 19 bars alive under the fly camera at any distance — 2.4 ms of UI per frame.
- **Perf switches (TerrainPerfBench.Switches.cs / Types.cs)**: `perf_ui`, `perf_entity_anim`, `perf_terrain_shadows`, `perf_veg_objects` (objects only, ring + cards stay), `perf_far_ring`, `perf_impostors`, all `0|1` and all rows of `perf_bisect`; **`perf_types [minCount]`** runs the bisect runner over every component TYPE in the scene (1 s each) — the table that found the scavs. Bisect noise is ±0.5 ms run to run; trust only the rows above +0.5.
- **Frame-time pass 2026-11-04 (Mark: 244 fps, "keep the game visually the same … don't sacrifice render distance")**, in-editor CPU work at the suite anchor, fresh play session: hover 9.87 → 6.05 ms, 2 m @ 55 m/s 13.79 → 7.90, 2 m @ 5 m/s 11.10 → 6.27, 500 m @ 55 m/s 10.20 → 6.17, 500 m down 9.00 → 5.45, pawn 11.78 → 7.35; GPU ≈ 6 ms in every row (pass sum ≈ 5.0, the rest is bubbles; the editor window was vsynced at 60 Hz, so the GPU likely ran at low clocks). What it was: (1) **Swim triggers were keyframed bodies** — a Collider with no Rigidbody and `Static` false is KEYFRAMED; the 89 chunk-sized SeaWater boxes (and river / cave pools) lying on the terrain colliders cost Physics 2.3 ms every frame. All water trigger boxes are `Static = true` now (a static body still follows its GameObject — `TickSeaVolumeUnderCamera` moves / rescales the camera's box — and still fires trigger events). (2) **`TrySampleGroundMeters` is the whole height pipeline (~18.8 µs)**: entity feet called it 2–3× a frame each. `TerrainWorldManager.TrySampleMeshedGroundMeters` is bilinear on the loaded chunk's vertex heights (0.3 µs; the drawn and collided surface — mean 3 cm, max 27 cm off the analytic curve), falling back to the generator where no chunk is loaded; `EntityLocomotion.TrySampleTerrainGroundZ` uses it. Per-frame callers must never use the generator sample. (3) **Chunk finish work moved off the main thread**: the grass biome mask (64 pipeline samples, ~1.2 ms a chunk) is built on the chunk's worker (`PendingChunkBuild.GrassMask`, `LoadedChunk.GrassMaskBuilt`; identical result, checked on 40 chunks), and `TryRestoreVoxelColumn` tests `AnyVoxelEditTouches` BEFORE building the 133² seed (it built the seed for every streamed chunk, 0.7 ms/frame flying). Flying, the manager went 2.38 → 1.39 ms/frame; world settle 18.8 → 10.5 s. (4) **Finished LOD remeshes were never applied while nothing loaded**: `ProcessStreamChunkQueue` returned on an empty load queue, so a still camera kept chunks 80–160 m away at 17 verts instead of 33, the six in-flight slots stayed full and the stale pass walked every loaded chunk each frame; it now drains `_pendingBuilds` too. (5) An off-screen animal nap (−1.0 ms for 29 animals) was replaced the same day by the animal range (100 / 90 m) above. (6) `EntityLocomotion` off-screen throttle: off frustum (+120 u) and > 40 m from camera and pawns → the render-rate clip + ground glue run every 4th frame, the skipped time carried into the Z ease (`_glueDtCarry`); the 50 Hz fixed tick is untouched. (7) `EnemyHealthBar` tests visibility first and syncs its world panel only while shown, writing only changed values. **Creatures were the biggest remaining cost**: 63 creatures (61 animals) measured 2.6 ms of a 7.1 ms hover frame (Update 1.6, Animation 0.45, Render 0.26, NavMesh 0.2 — the engine animates hidden bodies too, `SceneObject.RenderingEnabled = false` does not stop it), and animals never despawned (root objects that outlive their chunk; 23 → 159 on a 64 s fly-around) — fixed by the animal range (100 / 90 m). Render floor with every model, the grass and the far ring hidden is ~1.5 ms (engine); models are 1.8 ms (far tiles 799 = 0.65 — the 769 beyond 2 km only 0.38 —, voxel chunks 306 = 0.3, near chunks 343 = 0.22, trees 0.26, saplings 0.15, other 0.24). Sun shadows off now saves only 0.15 ms CPU. Under a 60 Hz vsync a frame carries 0.83 fixed ticks (≈ 0.25 uncapped at 200 fps), so fixed-tick costs read ~3× higher than they would uncapped.
- **Measured 2026-10-20 after all of the above (uncapped, in-editor, fly camera 260 m over the spawn forest, 4 sun cascades, 8× MSAA)**: standing 191-198 fps (5.05-5.24 ms, 0 hitches; Render 2.8, Update 1.0, Editor 0.6, Animation 0.3, UI 0.2; GPU 4.0-4.4, FarTrees pass 1.9-2.3), flying 55 m/s 132 fps avg (7.6 ms, p50 5.7, p99 23; hitches are GC — engine grass `ClutterInstance[]` 17 MB / 4 s + ring `Transform[]` 22 MB / 4 s — and chunk finishes). Sun shadow cascades 4 → 2 takes Render 3.5 → 2.25 ms (frame 8.9 → 6.9 at the time): the biggest lever left, a look decision (SunShadowDistance ×2 was set so the shadow ring is not at the feet). The `UI` category in perf_cpu was never the HUD (perf_ui = 0.5 ms): it was the world panels.
- **Far impostors 2.5 km → far tier edge (Mark 2026-10-20: "I should be able to see SOMETHING for these trees")**: `TerrainWorldManager.FarImpostors.cs`. Beyond the instanced LOD3 ring the forest is crossed vertex-coloured cards (crown + trunk quad pairs, 8 tris, built at runtime by `GetFarCardModel` on `materials/environment/cloverHills/trees/far_tree_card.vmat`, up-normals so every card shades as canopy). Planted on the far tier's worker from the cover `ApplyFarCanopyTint` already evaluates per far vertex (`PlanFarImpostors`: per 16 m cell, count = cover × **Far Terrain - Far Impostors Per Cell** (1.5) hash-rolled, jitter / yaw / 0.85-1.25 scale by hash, height bilinear from the far mesh; cover under 0.04 = none), stored hash-ordered on the `FarChunk` with its build time. Drawn by a second command list (`FarImpostors`, rebuilt every 30 frames): far chunks beyond the ring and inside **Far View Distance (m)**, prefix-thinned by distance (all to 4.5 km, half to 6.5 km, a quarter beyond — a hash-ordered array's prefix is a uniform subset), time-faded from the chunk's build. ~19 k cards at spawn, 0.6 ms a rebuild, no frame-time change. Past the far tier (8 km) nothing is drawn: a 22 m tree at 10 km is under a pixel, what remains is the canopy colour, so extending reach means extending the far tier (a coarser 2 km-chunk tier to the world edge is the open item). Far-tier lakes have no water surface and read as dark hollows from height (pre-existing).
- **Chunk borders must not show (Mark 2026-10-20, looking down: "I can very clearly see the chunks")**: `TerrainMeshBuilder.BuildChunkGeometry` samples a ring of raw heights one step OUTSIDE each chunk (`ChunkGeometry.EdgeRing`, 4n extra pipeline samples) and keeps the raw grid (`RawHeights`); `ComputeNormal` gives every edge vertex a central-difference normal from those shared raw samples, so two chunks shade their common border identically — the old one-sided difference clamped at the edge drew every border as a seam. The terrain material is pure diffuse: pixel_lit `PixelSpecular` 0 (no Blinn-Phong highlight, roughness forced to 1 so the engine's indirect reflection is flattest) on terrain_ground.vmat, with the chunk LOD remesh (33 vs 65 verts) as the remaining shading change at 128 / 256 m.
- **Redwood ledge cliffs are voxel ground** (2026-11-16, Mark: "90 degree drops or hell even sharper, so there's like a little overhang" — and of a rock mesh laid over the slope: "It needs to ACTUALLY be the terrain ... What happens when a player digs that wall?? Should be normal terrain"): the heightfield draws ledges as a scarp (first pass, far chunks); within 128 m a chunk a cliff band reaches becomes a voxel column seeded from its mesh with the cliff carved in by `Code/World/Terrain/TerrainLedgeCliffs.cs` (vertical face, 0.4–1.3 m overhanging lip, cap flush with the bench), so it digs, collides and paints like any voxel ground. Scheduling, border fades, the rock floor and the measured cost are in `Code/World/TerrainPreview/docs/TERRAIN_PREVIEW.md` (*Redwood ledge cliffs as voxel ground*); trees keep out of the band (`TerrainVegetationScatter.OnLedgeCliff`).
- **Streaming budgets (2026-10-13, Mark: trees must pop in without stutter, wingsuits at 120 mph later)**: terrainTest runs **Chunks - Chunks Per Frame** 1 and **Stream Mesh Build Budget (ms)** 5 (2026-10-15: a chunk finish is ~4 ms of main thread — model 0.7, scatter planning 1.4, grass + nav 1.7 — and three per frame under a 10 ms budget was the remaining ~20 ms frame once a second; 17 chunks/s at 55 m/s fits in one per frame). Tree colliders: a clone's `ModelCollider` is switched off BEFORE the instance is enabled when its chunk is outside Collision Range (`SpawnPlanned(…, collidersEnabled)`), and when a chunk comes into range its tree colliders are enabled through a queue (`TickVegetationColliderEnables`, 2 per frame) while chunk colliders come online one chunk per call (`UpdateChunkColliders`, `_colliderPassPending`) — the collider pass inside a stream refresh was 8-11 ms once a second (2026-10-15). Remaining single-clone outliers of 5-9 ms hit any prefab, even the collider-less dev-sphere rock, so they are GC pauses, not the prefab (`GC.CollectionCount` is not whitelisted, so they cannot be counted from game code). chunk SAMPLING runs on worker threads (`TerrainWorldManager.AsyncChunks.cs`, `GameTask.RunInThreadAsync`, in-flight cap, empty-result fallback to a sync build logged once); the main thread only builds the model. Scatter is PLANNED at chunk load (`TerrainVegetationScatter.SpawnPlan`) and CLONED a few per frame under **Vegetation - Scatter Spawn Budget (ms)** / **Scatter Spawns Per Frame** (`TerrainWorldManager.VegetationQueue.cs`, nearest chunk first); colliders / shadows are collected when a chunk's plans have all landed. Unloads drain **Chunks - Chunk Unloads Per Frame** at a time; LOD remeshes also sample on workers (`TryBeginChunkRemesh` → `ApplyChunkRemesh`), **LOD Remeshes Per Frame** only caps the synchronous fallback. Bisected 2026-10-14 by flying `terrain_fly 55 25` with grass / far tier / nav bake switched off in turn: none of them was the once-a-second 100-150 ms frame — the worst frame tracked the remesh count exactly (remesh 0 → 23 ms, remesh 5 → 146 ms). Nav bakes for streamed chunks are incremental now too (`nav_stream_request`). `perf_watch` prints `spawns N` per second. Measured on a 240 m teleport (perf_watch): worst frame 162 → ~110 ms; steady streaming seconds now peak at 54–75 ms against a 60 ms idle worst in the editor. The remaining teleport spike is **Chunks - Stream Max Sync Chunks Per Refresh** (synchronous builds of the chunks under the camera on every chunk crossing) — terrainTest runs it at 1 (was 4); at wingsuit speed the 512 m ring gives ~9 s of lead so async loads are enough, the sync one only guards a teleport.
- **Tree LOD cross-fade + fade-in (Mark 2026-10-20: shapes must not jump, trees must fade, never hard-pop)**: large trees no longer use the engine's LOD chain (one-frame switch, no fade). `TerrainWorldManager.TreeLod.cs` adopts every large tree when its chunk's clones stand (`CollectLodTrees` from `CollectVegetationColliders`), swaps its renderer to the single-LOD models beside the prefab's (`<model>_lod0 / _lod1 / _lod2 / _far.vmdl`, written per variant by `create_elm_tree.py`; a prefab without them keeps the engine chain, e.g. saplings), picks the level by distance in metres (**Vegetation - Tree LOD 1/2/3 At (m)**: 110 / 220 / 330, 8 % hysteresis, a slice of chunks every frame, each chunk every 0.25 s) and cross-fades a change over **Tree LOD Fade (s)** (0.35; live `tree_lod_fade`): a second `ModelRenderer` on the same object shows the old level with the inverted mask while the main one fades the new level in. The mask is pixel_lit's screen-door: `TreeFade` (1 = drawn, 0 = gone) and `TreeFadeInvert` render attributes, a 4x4 Bayer threshold per screen pixel clipped in every pass (shadows and depth prepass fade with the colour; defaults leave every other pixel_lit material alone) — a 2026-10-20 mid-fade capture shows both levels interleaved pixel by pixel. A felled tree (renderer no longer on one of its levels) drops out of the scheme. The far ring fades in TIME, not by distance (Mark 2026-10-20: a tree must go fully opaque and stay so, never sit half-drawn): a chunk's far trees fade from 0 to 1 over **Vegetation - Far Trees — Fade (s)** (0.6) from the frame they first become drawable (planned and in range) and fade out over the same time when the chunk leaves the range (`_farShownSince` / `_farLeavingSince`, 8 quantised steps, one instanced draw per model per step); the hand-over to real trees drops them at once (same mesh); turning the camera does not restart a fade (the cone cull only skips the draw). Costs: ~0.3 ms a frame, 60-90 fades/s at 55 m/s. Next steps if the remaining shape change still reads: silhouette-preserving LOD generation in `build_lods` (keep cards by spatial spread per clump and scale by local density instead of the golden-ratio rank), and pushing the thresholds out.
- **Tree LOD bands (Mark 2026-10-14, after ChatGPT's table)**: 0-100 m real prefab trees with shadows (**Vegetation - Tree Shadow Range (m)**, flipped per chunk in `UpdateChunkColliders`; colliders still follow Collision Range 192 m); 100-512 m real trees, no shadows, saplings only on full-detail chunks (<=128 m); 512 m-2.5 km impostors; 2-8 km the far tier's ground darkens toward canopy green where the forest patches are (**Far Terrain - Canopy Tint**, `ApplyFarCanopyTint` <- `TerrainVegetationScatter.SampleForestCover01`); past 8 km nothing yet (a second, coarser far tier to the 20 km world edge is the open item).
- **Far forest = each tree's own lowest LOD, drawn by our own command list** (`TerrainWorldManager.FarTrees.cs`, 2026-10-13/20): past the loaded chunks every large tree the real scatter would plant is drawn as its model's `_far.vmdl` sibling — for the elm the LOD3 render mesh alone (same bark / leaf materials, no physics), written per variant by `create_elm_tree.py` (`FAR_LOD`, `far_vmdl_text`); a prefab without one uses **Vegetation - Far Trees — Fallback Model**. Positions / scale / yaw / prefab come from `TerrainVegetationScatter.EnumerateTrees` (the real scatter's own seeded planner) on worker threads, cached per chunk 1 km beyond **Far Trees — Range (m)** (2500), turned into per-model `Transform[]` batches on the main thread. RENDERING: NOT the engine Clutter any more — a `CommandList` on the camera at `Stage.BeforePostProcess` (the obsolete `AddHookAfterOpaque` never fires in 26.10 and no managed AfterOpaque pass exists; the screenshot tool does run command lists), rebuilt every 2 frames: every cached chunk in range whose real trees are not standing is gathered per model and drawn with ONE `DrawModelInstanced` per model (per-chunk draws, 2 765 a frame, ran at 6 fps; the engine copies the span per call, so the rebuild is ~130 KB per model and the cadence halves it). HAND-OVER IS OURS, FRAME-EXACT: `ChunkHasRealTrees` = `FarHandoverFrame` reached and not `Unloading`; load = clone visible, `TryBeginFarHandover` at the chunk's LAST clone sets the hand-over 2 frames ahead (overlap of identical LOD3 meshes, never a gap); unload = `Unloading` makes the far trees draw at once, the real ones are destroyed `FarUnloadOverlapFrames` (5) later. The Clutter version could not do this: the engine applied a rebuilt tile 1-4+ frames after its generator with no way to ask, and every timing rule flashed somewhere (flash probe, 2026-10-15/20). Dev: `far_trees 0|1`, `far_trees_mode 0|1|2` (instanced / one DrawModel per tree / dev box, for render-path debugging), `flash_probe`, `terrain_stream_offset`, `terrain_stream_radius`.
- **Streamed terrain**: default is a **uniform square** around the camera (`StreamRadiusChunks`, e.g. 8 ≈ 512 m) — same distance in all directions, no look pop-in. Unload uses `StreamUnloadMarginChunks` hysteresis (keep until outside radius + margin) so load/unload don’t thrash at the same edge. Optional forward-cone mode remains for later. Entity population only inside `CollisionRangeMeters`.

### Enemy camps + spawn leash

- **Camps are data** (`Assets/data/enemy_camps.json`, `EnemyCampCatalog`): a layout of build pieces and entities as camp-local offsets in build-kit meters (40 u/m, `BuildModuleDimensions.UnitsPerMeter`, so entity offsets line up with piece footprints). `EnemyCampSpawner.TrySpawn` (host) drops the origin to ground, places pieces through `BuildAuthority.HostPlacePiece` (the shared tail of hammer placement: configure, NetworkSpawn, snap cache, nav rebake, structural solve — no pawn, no cost, no durability) and spawns entities through `EntityEnemySetup.Configure`. The `I` key (`EnemyCampSpawnButton`) stamps the test camp.
- **Generated world camps (2026-11-02, Mark: "scav camps should be placed around the world with the procedural generation. I dont see any.")**: `biome_population.json` row `scav_camp` (`"camp": "scav_camp_clover"`, `spacingMeters` 500, `spawnWeight` 0.85, `respawnDelaySeconds` 300) - `EnemyCampSites`. Each 500 m world cell rolls the weight, then tries 4 seeded candidate points (inner 70 % of the cell) against the site rules: ≥ 300 m from the world spawn (origin), centre dry land (no river / beach / wet band), 8 points at 12 m within 1.5 m of the centre height (≈ 7°) and dry, 8 points at 35 m on land with no river or wet band, and in Clover Hills the centre + a 14 m ring in a forest clearing (no trunk in the camp). The first passing candidate is the cell's camp (pure planner `TryPlanSite`, so every chunk agrees which chunk owns it); that chunk's population pass (host, inside Collision Range, after the chunk collider is up) stamps it once per session through `EnemyCampSpawner.TrySpawn( …, slotPrefix, entry )`. Measured on seed 1337: **1.4 camps / km² of Clover Hills** (30 sites in 21.4 km², ≈ one per 850 m). Every guard is a population slot `scav_camp:c:{cx}:{cy}:{i}` (`BiomePopulationSlot`): dies → respawns after 300 s through `BiomePopulationRespawnQueue` **on the camp leash** (the slot reads the brain's leash at death and `SpawnAtWorld` re-applies it), sleeps into `EntityChunkMemory` with home / leash / slot, and a chunk reload only re-spawns guards whose slot is free (`EnemyCampSpawner.SpawnGuards`); pieces stay for the session and go with the world (`EnemyCampSites.ClearWorldCamps` in `UnloadAll`; the root is `NotSaved` and keeps its pieces in `EnemyCamp.Pieces`). Layout `scav_camp_clover` in `enemy_camps.json` is a placeholder from the build kit - a 2 × 2 wood-floor platform and a campfire, five tier-1 scavs, wander 20 m / max travel 70 m (camp-local metres) - because **the camp models do not exist yet** (Mark's prompt 13: falling-apart fence held with rope, tiny thatch huts). The leash rings draw for every camp while `campRings` is on (default on). `world_find camp` lists sites.
- **Camp pieces stop drawing at 200 m (2026-11-19, Mark: "camps are always rendered once you find them, can you make those disappear at a distance?")**: `EnemyCampSpawner` sets `[Sync] BuildPiece.WorldCampPiece` on every piece it places; `CampPieceDistanceCull` (a `GameObjectSystem`, every machine, 2 Hz) switches those pieces' `ModelRenderer`s and `Light`s off past `HideMeters` 200 from the local camera and back on inside `ShowMeters` 185. Draw only - colliders, nav, health and the host's `Stamped` bookkeeping are untouched.
- **Ground pick-up ranges (2026-11-19, Mark: "dial back how much is present when on the ground")**: **Vegetation - Pickup Visible Range (m)** 100 -> 60 and **Vegetation - Prop Objects Range (m)** 192 -> 128 (code defaults and main / terrainTest scenes). `perf_types` now refuses to run as the pawn (it switches colliders off and dropped the pawn through the world); `perf_player body 1` re-enables only the renderers `body 0` switched off. `models/player/body_boxout` (the grey placeholder body) and its three disabled components on `basicplayer.prefab` are deleted.
- **Camp leash rings** (Mark, 2026-09-22): two rings **centered on the camp origin**, shared by every guard (`EntityBrain.SetLeash( center, wander, maxTravel )`; `HomePosition` stays the per-guard spawn used by breach return-home). `LeashWanderDistance` (inner ring, 24 m default): wander legs are clamped to within it (from further out, the leg heads back toward the camp). `LeashMaxTravelDistance` (outer ring, 80 m default): an alerted guard (Chasing / Attacking / Searching / Breaching) pulled past it drops its target and enters `Returning` — run speed back toward the camp origin, no alert build-up and hits are ignored until it is back within 85 % of the wander distance, then Idle → Wander as normal. Zero = unbounded (K-key scavs keep free roam). Camp JSON gives defaults; an entity row can override the distances (same center). The spawner creates an `EnemyCamp` root object at the origin; it redraws the green / red rings every frame while `campRings` is on (default on, toggle in console) — one-shot timed overlay lines vanished after 20 s, which read as "the rings disappear when I alert them".

### Bosses (screen-top bar, second form)

- **Bosses are data** (`Assets/data/bosses.json`, `BossCatalog` / `BossData`): id, `displayName`, entity `prefab`, `archetype` + `tier` (move / attack tuning through the normal `EntityArchetype`), first-form `health`, `healthBarRangeMeters` (500), and the second form as flat flags — `hasSecondForm`, `secondFormHealth`, `secondFormBarColor` as a hex string parsed by `BossHealthBarHud.ParseBarColor` (first form is always red, second form green). `BossSpawner.TrySpawn` (host) clones the prefab, runs the shared `EntityEnemySetup.Configure`, then `BossEntity.HostConfigure` binds the row. The `H` key (`BossSpawnButton`, input action `SpawnBoss`) stamps `scav_warlord` at the `BossSpawner` object in testscene1 (4450, 1500 — ~70 m from the camp spawner).
- **`BossEntity`** is authored on the boss prefab (`Assets/prefabs/entity/bossT1.prefab` = scavT1 with fresh guids + the component). It mirrors `DisplayName`, `Form`, `CurrentHealth` / `MaxHealth`, `SpawnPosition`, `HealthBarRange` (units, converted once from meters) and `BarColorHex` through `[Sync( FromHost )]`, because `EntityVitals` itself is host-local. **Second form**: `EntityVitals.LethalHitInterceptor` — a hit that would empty the pool is offered to the boss first; in form 1 with a second form it sets `MaxHealth` to `secondFormHealth`, `ResetToFull()`, flips the bar colour and returns true, so no kill credit and no `OnDied` fire. Form 2 dies normally (brain destroys the object). `EntityVitals.DisplayNameOverride` makes the world bar say the boss name too.
- **Screen-top bar** (`BossHealthBarHud`, built by `PlayerScreenHud`): name over a 480 px bar, black "current/max" label inside, fill colour from the synced hex. **Range rule** (Mark, 2026-09-22): a viewer gets the bar the moment their pawn is within `healthBarRangeMeters` of the boss — at spawn or when they later walk into range (checked every 0.25 s until engaged); once engaged it stays until the boss is defeated / gone. Nearest engaged boss wins when several are up. Every viewer also gets a 3 s full-width "<name> spawned" banner (above screen centre) the first time they see a boss, range or not. **Abandon despawn** (Mark): `abandonDespawnSeconds` (60) — the host checks once a second whether any live player pawn is inside the bar range of the boss; after that long with nobody there it broadcasts `BossEntity.RpcAnnounce( name, "despawned" )` (static `Announced( name, verb )` event → the same banner reads "<name> despawned"; the last form's death sends "killed" the same way) and destroys the object, which drops the bar. 0 = never.

### Terrain world (2026-10-07 additions)
