- **Distant terrain** (`TerrainWorldManager.FarTier.cs`): 512 m render-only chunks to 8 km, sampled on WORKER threads (`SampleFarChunk` via `GameTask.RunInThreadAsync`, **Far Terrain - Far Async Builds In Flight**) with only the model upload + GameObject on the main thread under **Far Build Budget (ms)** — the synchronous build was the once-a-second 30-46 ms frame while flying (perf_watch `phase max ms` line, 2026-10-15; every other phase under 12 ms), sunk 2.5 m under the near bubble, hidden where fully covered; far vertices within a river's reach are lowered 1.5 m under the river level (`LowerFarRiverVertices`, 2026-10-30) so the far ground carries a trough under the merged river surface instead of bridging the channel at bank height — the channel used to end in a wall along the chunk line where the loaded chunks stop; raises the camera far clip (the scene cameras had 1 250 m, the reason the world felt 2 km wide). `terrain_far 0|1`, `terrain_cam x y z pitch yaw`, `terrain_seams [minGapM]` (border steps between loaded chunks with each side's LOD / water / fine-side flags).
- **Rivers** (`TerrainPreviewRivers`, Lakes tab knobs **River …**): built with the land-disk rasters, routed downhill on a smoothed coarse height field to lakes / sea / other rivers, carved at sample time as a trough: parabolic bed under the water level, a curved slide wall **River Bank Slide Width (m)** wide up to a rim **River Bank Height (m)** above the water, then the bank blend out to the land (cut only, never raised); `RiverWater` samples are not land. Bed depth: **River Bed Depth (m)** × pools × a seeded per-river 0.6–1.2, capped per biome on the continuous Clover weight (**River Bed Max Depth, Clover Hills (m)** 4 / **Other Biomes (m)** 6 — Mark 2026-10-21: Clover rivers gentle, 4 m at most). Water = `TerrainWorldManager.RiverWater.cs`: ONE merged surface mesh for every loaded segment in `materials/water/riverwater.vmat` (`shaders/riverwater.shader`, shared code in `shaders/water_common.hlsl` — the compiler builds an include as its own module, so it sees none of the including shader's macros or parameters). Mesh data (2026-10-22): COLOR0 R = carved depth / 8 m (read as a real vertex colour — the engine's `vVertexColor` is the renderer tint, which had made every pixel "8 m deep"), G = current from the slope (calm pools on the flats, up to 3× the network's median-slope speed on steep stretches; the swim volume's fluid velocity follows it), B = biome tint axis (Redwood tannin · clear · Mountain glacial), A = half width; UV1 = bank + full biome treeline height per side. The look (Mark 2026-10-22, "Valheim done better"): the bed through the water, Beer-Lambert over a ~10 m clear distance and blurred by the path; the dome's sky by Fresnel, except where the reflected ray meets a deliberately tall proxy wall at the bank — there the colour is read from the frame buffer at that point (the real bank / trees, or the sky above them), rippled; a GGX glint from every light (sun with its shadows, moon, torches), roughness growing with distance; in the sun's shade the sheen goes (`ShadeReflection`) so the bed shows through, from the soft visibility averaged down the water column — never a darkened surface, never a crisp shadow edge; ripples from a tileable slope texture (`Blender/scripts/create_river_ripples.py` → `materials/water/river_ripples.png`) at 2 / 8 / 32 m, two-phase flow-advected so a varying current never stretches them; cloud-noise foam patches (more on fast water) + shore foam; swimmer rings dropped along each pawn's path (see round 3). Fog only on what the surface adds (the frame-buffer copy is fogged already). Swim: one "water"-tagged box trigger + `WaterVolume` per segment, owned by the chunk holding its midpoint. River banks, lake and sea shores get a darker wet band up to **Water - Wet Line Height (m)** (`TerrainPreviewSample.Wet01` → the terrain splat's wet channel, see **Terrain ground**). The carve is a pure function of world position (voxel-ready: a density field can evaluate the same sampler). `terrain_tp_river [N]`, `terrain_river_check [N]`. Full write-up in `Code/World/TerrainPreview/docs/TERRAIN_PREVIEW.md`.
- **Ocean + lakes** (2026-10-22, Mark: "big waves out deep that rock the boat, less near the shore, at the shore just a little wake and white bubbles"; "carve out the ocean and the lakes … a cheap bowl"): the carve puts lake floors down to **Water - Lake Max Depth (m)** (8) over **Lake Shelf Width (m)** (100) measured in from the shoreline (`MetersIntoLake`: the mask's excess over its gradient, sampled one raster cell apart — the old mask-fraction ramp left most lakes under a metre deep; the submerged bed IS the bowl) and the rim ocean down to **Ocean Shelf Depth (m)** (30) over **Ocean Shelf Width (m)** (900), then a drop-off over **Ocean Drop-Off Width (m)** (700) to **Ocean Max Depth (m)** (300) (`OceanDepthMeters`), the shelves on `BowlProfile` t·(2−t) — it falls away at the waterline (a smoothstep left the bed within centimetres of the surface for tens of metres and the two z-fought in stripes); beaches ease the dry-land margin down to the water over 8 m (`ApplyBeachSlope`), ground past the world edge sits at ocean-floor depth (`TerrainMeshBuilder.OutsideWorldFloorMeters`), and a sample exactly on the land edge now takes the ocean path (it came back ~100 m: a pillar on the coast). Near chunks paint every water bed as sand. All open water sits at Sea Level and all dry land above it, so `TerrainWorldManager.SeaWater.cs` draws ONE surface for the whole world: a camera-following radial grid (0.5 m underfoot, ~650 m at 40 km, no seams) in `materials/water/seawater.vmat` (`shaders/seawater.shader`). The shader recomputes the depth exactly as the carve does (the lake mask raster uploaded once as `SeaLakeMask` + the analytic shelf) and displaces the grid with Gerstner waves (Water round 5: 14 swells on the travel field + 10 chop — the text that follows is the 2026-10-22 state) along WindSystem's wind: swells grown by the water depth (Water round 4: **Sea - Swell Min / Full Depth (m)**, the old Open Ocean Ramp is gone) and the wind's base strength (**Sea - Swell Amplitude (m)** 4.5 — also stored in terrainTest.scene, which had kept 2.2 — gives ~8–9 m crest to trough at the default wind (Water round 4: a 14-wave spectrum weighted by the weather, see below), ~2.2× in a storm; 2026-10-25, Mark: open ocean like his reference — tall peaked swells with white crests, "not a big ripple"). Crest sharpness: one crest factor Q per weather keeping Σ Q·k·a at 0.85 (Water round 4; the per-wave `c_flSeaCrest` table is gone); whitecaps where the crests fold past **WhitecapThreshold** (0.35) ± a broad drifting noise field (otherwise every crest of the leading swell broke in parallel rows), only on open water (openness 0.25 → 0.7), three chop waves from wind + gusts (lakes get **Lake Chop Share**), all depth-limited to ~0.35 × depth plus **Shore Lap (m)**; each wave fades where the grid is too coarse for it. The surf is one lap function shared by both stages (`SeaShoreLaps`: the vertex surge running up the sand and the foam front + dissolving trail), distance to the waterline from world-space central differences of the depth; all foam goes through `FoamLace` (domain-warped, rotated cloud noise — thick foam solid, thin foam wisps). The grid's VS must re-project the displaced point (`Position3WsToPs`) — ProcessVertex had already projected the flat grid, which left the waves in the lighting only. `sea_probe` / `sea_tune <swell> [chop]`. `SeaWaves.cs` evaluates the same sum on the CPU — `TerrainWorldManager.SeaSurfaceHeightAt(world)` is what a boat will ride (constants must stay in step with the shader). Swim: a "water" trigger per near chunk whose ground dips below sea level, from its lowest ground to 0.3 m above sea level (waves above it are not swimmable yet). Water beds are sand in both tiers (round 3).
- **Water round 3** (2026-10-22, from Mark's screenshots): shores measure distance from the lake mask itself (`MetersToShore`: shortfall below the shore threshold / mask gradient) — the open-water distance raster (40–150 m cells) made square sea-level plateaus and square wet bands round lakes. Both water shaders read the scene depth (the Hi-Z depth chain exists in play) for the real path, real depth, a soft waterline (`EdgeSoftnessMeters`) and the real distance to the waterline near the camera, blending to the carve's own depth beyond ~150 m where the chain cannot resolve centimetres. Swimmer wakes are ring sources dropped where the pawn actually went (`TickWaterRipples` → GpuBuffer `WaterTrail`, `water_common.hlsl WaterRings`), never a velocity-driven V. **Engine gotcha:** the refraction frame-buffer copy (`bWantsFBCopyTexture`) is made only where the engine's "Refraction Stencil Layer" pass marks the water, and over shallow beds it left thin rows uncopied (exactly black) — visible as stripes across shallow lakes. Water meshes are lifted 1 m (`WaterStencilLiftMeters`, the VS lowers them) and every copy tap that comes back black is skipped (`CopyTap`). A camera-stage command-list `GrabFrameTexture` does NOT reach scene draws (tried, magenta); what works is a per-renderer grab: `Renderer.ExecuteBefore` = a CommandList with `GlobalAttributes.GrabFrameTexture( "WaterSceneColor", true )` (`WaterGrabCommands`), which both water shaders read. Water shadows sample the cascades only (`WaterShadow` → `DirectionalLightShadow::SampleCascade`): `GetVisibility` also multiplies in the engine's screen-space contact-shadow mask, which is computed from the depth buffer — the bed or sea floor behind the water, not the surface — and read the whole open sea as shadowed past a straight line (no sheen, a dark far half). Water-column taps (`WaterColumnTap`) are moved up the sun ray to 1 m above the surface, so only what stands above the water can shade it. Open-sea reflections never go below a minimum roughness (chop smears clouds into streaks; crisp clouds through the fine ripples broke into grey shards). Banks: land within 4× its height of a lake / sea shoreline (8–40 m, `ApplyShoreBank`, from `MetersToShore`) eases to sea level at the waterline — the carve used to set only the floor, so every shore above sea level was a cliff drawn along the 2 m vertex grid as a staircase of fins. The lake mask is R32F, which GPUs do not filter: both water shaders bilinear-filter it by hand (`SeaMaskBilinear` / `OpenWaterMaskBilinear`, texel centres, 0 outside — the CPU's convention); a declared bilinear sampler gave nearest texels, i.e. 40–150 m blocks of depth. Beaches: **Water - Beach Width (m)** sand band (`Beach01`), the terrain splat's sand channel. **Underwater** (`TerrainWorldManager.Underwater.cs` → `shaders/underwater.shader`, a CommandList on the scene camera at `Stage.BeforePostProcess`, no prefab component): when the camera is below the sea / lake surface (waves included) or a river's level, light reaching each point is Beer-Lambert over the water above THAT point (bluer and dimmer with depth), caustics on upward-facing ground within 30 m, the surface from below drawn by the pass (Snell's window ≈48.6° with a ripple wobble, total internal reflection outside), and the underside of the surface is a bright moving net (the caustic texture at surface scale) with the sky swimming through it, and everything fades to the water's scattered colour over the view path (6 m visibility, brighter looking up; daylight from above fades over 25 m — pale teal at 6 m, blue-teal at 40 m, navy at 150 m). **Horizon tier** (`TerrainWorldManager.Horizon.cs`): four render-only quadrants cover the whole world disc at ~350 m per vertex, sampled once per load on workers; pixel_lit `F_FAR_SINK` buries them 300 m down inside Far View Distance via the per-renderer `FarSinkCircle` (15 m across the ring the far chunks may or may not reach, true height 400 m past it) — without it nothing was drawn between 8 km and the coast. The camera's far clip covers the world's diameter. Shores seen from high up (Mark's "fov glitch") switched source with camera height: within ~150–300 m the water's edge comes from the scene depth, beyond it from the carve's lake mask — which the nearest-texel bug drew as 40–150 m blocks. Dev: `terrain_tp_lake`, `terrain_paint_probe`, `terrain_paint_line`. Far rivers join the merged river surface out to Far View Distance; the far tier paints water beds as sand. The far terrain tier is no longer sunk on the CPU: `terrain_ground_far.vmat` (pixel_lit `F_FAR_SINK`) drops it on the GPU only inside the near-chunk square (`SeaNearRect`, easing over 4 m from the edge), so there is no step where the near square ends and the water needs no sink of its own. Chunk meshes carry 1.5 m skirts (render mesh only, both windings) against T-junction cracks, all normals come from raw-sample central differences, and chunk bounds include negative z (bounds starting at 0 culled sunk chunks — "square chunks taken out"). Caustics: `Blender/scripts/create_water_caustics.py` (photons through a rippled surface) → `water_caustics.png`, faded by depth and pixel footprint. Dev: `cycle_pause`, `sea_probe`, `sea_tune`, DebugView on both water materials.
- **Terrain ground** (2026-10-24, Mark: biome borders stair-stepped or smeared — "weird areas where the texture kinda blurs"): a per-pixel biome blend, pixel_lit `F_TERRAIN_SPLAT` on `terrain_ground.vmat` / `terrain_ground_far.vmat`. The vertex says what the ground IS (`TerrainGroundSplat.Fill`): COLOR0 = Clover / Redwood / Amber shares of the land weights + the Mountain weight; TEXCOORD0 = sand (1 on lake / river / sea beds, `Beach01` on shores) + wet (`Wet01`); TEXCOORD1 = far-forest canopy darkening (`ApplyFarCanopyTint`) + the tier (0 near, 1 far, 2 horizon). The pixel picks the biome as `TerrainPreviewBiomeResolver` does (Mountain from 0.5, else the largest share), softmax-sharp with the shares pushed about by noise (`Splat Edge Wander`, `Splat Edge Sharpness`, `Splat Noise Meters` on the material), so a border is an organic edge a few metres wide on every tier — never the vertex grid's stairs nor a 100 m smear. Five 512 px ground tiles over 4 m = 128 texels / m (`materials/terrain/ground_{clover,redwood,amber,mountain,sand}.png`, all from `Blender/scripts/create_ground_textures.py`, 2026-11-03), point-sampled up close and filtered past a texel per pixel; each tile is sampled twice (the second turned 40°) and the two swap in noise patches so the 4 m repeat never shows; the tiles carry no blotch coarser than ~30 cm (the script divides it out) and the shader adds the large scale — macro brightness at ~360 m and ~30 m (`Splat Macro Variation`) and a warm-dry / cool-lush hue drift (`Splat Macro Hue`). Azure Coast is a coast overlay, not a ground (it shows the land under it plus the beach); Blackwater has no land weight and draws near-black. **Engine gotcha:** a `Feature` needs a `StaticCombo` in EVERY stage that tests it — `S_FAR_SINK` was only a feature, so `#if ( S_FAR_SINK )` in the VS compiled to nothing and neither the far-tier sink nor the horizon burial ever ran (the unburied horizon tier bridged over river channels and drew sand halos round lakes). The horizon burial circle is scene-wide (`HorizonSinkCircle`) and applies to tier-2 vertices only. The vertex-paint path (`TerrainChunkBiomeDisplay`, `TerrainGroundTextureMask`, **Clover Ground Texture Blend**) is gone. Dev: `terrain_horizon 0|1`.
- **Rivers never cross themselves** (2026-10-24, Mark: a river whose water stopped in a point and carried on as a dry channel): the meander could swing a bend back onto its own route, and where the two passes met at different levels the carve followed only one — the other's water lay under the ground. `TerrainPreviewRivers.CutSelfCrossings` cuts any loop whose ends come within 3 × the max half width and rounds the neck. `terrain_river_check` now also reports carve gaps on the centre line, segments whose water is under the ground and segments dropped as open water over dry land; `river_surface_probe x y [r]` lists the segments near a point and whether the water surface has them.
- **Shores and river mouths** (2026-10-24, Mark: "square edges" near the water; "a wall of height … clearly breaks them up, they need to blend together … a script for river bleed into big body of water"): the old mask-band easing (`TerrainPreviewLakeShoreHeight.ApplyDryLandNearLake`, a band of the 40–150 m bilinear lake mask) is deleted — it drew straight-edged ridges and lips near lakes; `ApplyShoreBank` now decides from the smooth shore distance alone (no mask-band gate). Under open water the river channel carries on (`TerrainPreviewPipeline.CarryRiverBed`, lakes and the rim ocean): bed parabola at the river's level, min with the bowl, same per-biome cap — no sill across the mouth. Water (reworked 2026-10-25, Mark: "the river to ocean/lake blending system is what needs to be updated"): `TerrainWorldManager.PublishRiverMouths` finds where each river first crosses into open water and publishes it with the channel's half width, carved depth and a hand-over length (1.5 river widths, 20–60 m) — `RiverMouths` / `RiverMouthInfo`, scene-wide. `shaders/river_mouths.hlsl RiverMouthAt` makes the LAKE / SEA surface own the mouth: its depth includes the channel over the hand-over stretch (so the sea grid covers the last stretch of river), it fades in up the channel while the river strip fades out (hardware alpha), and there is no surf there. The river strip stops 1 m past the shoreline (`RiverMouthOverlapMeters`) and its end vertex samples depth / tint at that cut, not at the segment end out in the lake (that shaded the last river piece as deep water — a dark slab). Chunks holding a shoreline or a river channel keep full mesh detail at any stream distance (`ChunkTouchesWater`, cached per coord; vegetation keeps the distance rule) — 4 m far-LOD steps drew sand slabs and water steps at mouths. Dev: `river_mouth [n] [back] [height] [side]` lists mouths (lake / ocean) or parks the camera upstream of one. The river's line itself (`TerrainPreviewRivers`, 2026-10-25): `EndAtWater` stops it where its channel first touches open water and sends it 60 m straight out down the ground's slope (the meander had pushed some ends along the coast — a river straddling the shoreline, cut lengthwise by the sea's edge); `ApproachMouth` eases the level down to the lake / sea over the last stretch (≤ 500 m, ≤ 45 % of the river, measured from where the level first reaches the water, on (d/W)^1.6, cutting up to 3× the fine-cut limit through the shore bluff) — the level used to follow the steep shore bank, a 14 m drop in 65 m, a ramp of water into the flat lake. All 21 mouths surveyed by eye after these (2026-10-25). Later the same day (Mark's next round): **width from shape** — `BendAndMouthWidths` replaces the random width: 0.75× on straights to 1.6× in tight bends (≈ 50 m radius, turn rate smoothed over ±3 points), and an estuary flare over the last 180 m to 2.4× at the shoreline (pools on top); **ripples carried by the current** — the strip's UV.x is TRAVEL (metres ÷ local current multiple, `RiverTravel`, cached per network), scrolled at one constant FlowSpeed (0.9 m/s), so each ripple moves at its local speed with nothing stretching: the two-phase scroll (FlowCycleSeconds, deleted) restarted every 3 s and surged; **rapids** — current ≥ 1.8× the median-slope speed fades in white water streaked down the flow, milky aerated colour and 70 % less see-through (`RapidsStrength`), river bank foam a continuous broken band (it was evenly spaced dashes); **sea ripples** — their drift is integrated on the CPU (`SeaRippleTravel`), the shader's time × gust-dependent speed jumped the whole pattern back and forth with every gust; **mouth foam** — `RiverMouthFoamKeep` clears the lake / sea surf along each river's corridor out past the shoreline; **lake waves** — (superseded in Water round 4: swell only over deep water, Swell Min / Full Depth; lakes keep their chop). `terrain_river_check` also prints each river's steepest stretch. Both water shaders set `DepthWriteEnable false` — the river strip and the lake surface lie at the same level and whichever drew first hid the other by depth (bare bed showed where the river faded). The sea grid only ducks under the ground more than two grid spacings inland (`SeaLandMeters`), so the triangle crossing the waterline stays level (it tilted under the river strip in a sawtooth). The river has its own frame grab (`RiverGrabCommands`). Third round (2026-10-25): **squares at water level** — `TerrainPreviewCoastalSmoothing` took its distance to water from the 40–150 m lake raster, which reads 0 across whole cells the lake does not cover, and drained that land to sea level in cell-shaped squares; a first fix took the larger of that and the continuous outline; both are gone — Water round 4's traced shoreline replaces them, and `terrain_coast_scan` replaced `terrain_find_steps`; `terrain_bank_probe x y` prints the shore inputs at a point. **Mouth level hold** — `ApproachMouth` keeps the river at the lake / sea level over the hand-over stretch (`MouthHandOverMeters` 60, ≤ half the window) before ramping up, so the river strip and the sea surface meet at one height where the shader cross-fades them (a step there showed the bed through both). **Fast water** (Mark: "the white rapids stuff is trash … I wanted like bubbles and a way to show it's fast flowing"; long streaks then read as white road markings) — from 1.15× the median-slope current: foam clumps a few metres long (cloud noise 16 m along × 5 m across, gated by a 64 m field) riding the travel coordinate, their own foam broken into soft wisps drawn out down the current (NOT the world-aligned `FoamLace`, which cut it into flecks banded across the flow), and round bubbles in them (`BubbleLayer`: hashed 0.4 m and 0.2 m cells, one bubble at most per cell, thin bright rim + glint, wrapping with the 64 m travel tile; past a pixel or two a layer becomes its even average cover — gating that per cell drew white squares). More clumps, foam and bubbles toward the rapids (≥ 1.8×), which stay milky and less see-through. (Clumps superseded in Water round 4 by standing-wave white water; `BubbleLayer` stays.)
- **Water round 4** (2026-10-25, Mark: squares "fix that for the love of god … follow along the whole coast", "a massive hole in the earth", "weird seams between the chunks", "where rivers converge needs major work", waves "always 8 m apart … weather to affect them", "1 wave right in the middle" of a lake, lights "SO dim" under water, trees floating under water, rapids "should be horizontal white like the painting", rivers "NOT so straight"):
  - **Shore distance is a traced shoreline** — `TerrainPreviewLakeShoreDistance`: marching squares on every lake-mask cell that straddles the threshold (8× subdivided, bilinear), only next to the speck-filtered open-water raster, exact point-to-segment distance on a 16 m grid (seeded, then swept), signed (+ dry, − in a lake), read bilinearly. Replaces the mask's shortfall ÷ gradient (it collapsed to 0 over whole patches where the mask lies flat at the threshold — water-level plateaus walled by 7 m cuts, and 100+ m walls on mountains near borderline mask). Users: `MetersToShore`, `MetersIntoLake`, `CoastalSmoothing` (the open-water raster distance and `SampleDistanceToOpenWaterMetersSmooth` are deleted), `IsSubmergedByLakeMask` (now also needs the field inside), and seawater.shader (`SeaLakeShore` texture, R32F, bilinear by hand). `terrain_coast_scan [step] [band] [min step]` walks every coast on worker threads and lists height cuts (8 460 → 2 625 sites, worst 133 m → 18 m mountain noise, water-level sites 86 → 25, all of them designed river bank walls).
  - **Rivers**: meanders on top of the FBM wander — wavelength ~12 channel widths but never under 160 m (8 route points; shorter zig-zagged narrow streams into star-shaped pits), loop ≤ 2.4 widths and ≤ a fifth of the wavelength, full below 1 % grade, gone past 6 %. **Confluences**: a tributary ends on the main river's FINAL centre line (`JoinRiver`; the routing cell it reached had since been meandered away), its level eases onto the main river's over the last ~120 m (`MeetRiverLevel`); the carve takes every channel that reaches a point (`SamplePartner` with segment pairs precomputed where carve reaches overlap — `LinkConfluences`; the nearest-only lookup switched profiles along a line, a wall out of the water); the ground paint and the underwater check ask both rivers; the tributary's strip is split into rows over its last two segments and fades out right at the other river's bank (`RiverJoinFade`, carried in TexCoord1.y's hundreds — vertex colour is sRGB-converted and scrambled a packed channel); bank foam follows the real depth (no foam line across an inlet). `Network.RiverJoins`, `terrain_tp_river` lists "flows into", `river_join_probe`. Current is averaged over five segments (a slope tripling in one segment switched white water on along a straight line).
  - **White water** = standing waves: broken white bands ACROSS the channel that stay put (travel coordinate, not scrolled), churn racing through them, foam shed a few metres downstream, bubbles only in the white, a stationary 170 m world field making stretches rough or glassy; fed through the shared foam lace. Replaces the 5 m-tile clumps (repeated in columns).
  - **Sea**: 14-wave spectrum (`SeaWaves.Waves` = shader `c_vSeaWaves`): nine swells 22–178 m at spread headings and random phases, five chop. Weather = WindSystem base strength (`wind_base <0-1>`): `SeaWaves.Spectrum` weights the fixed components by a log-normal around a peak wavelength (35 m calm → 70 m default → 160 m storm) and scales the total (0.3× → 1× → 2.8×) — never changes a wavelength, so weather changes never slide the pattern; one crest factor Q keeps Σ Q·k·a at 0.85; published as `SeaSwellA/B/C`. Swell only over deep water: **Sea - Swell Min / Full Depth (m)** 10 / 30 (replaces Open Ocean Ramp and the lake openness ramp) — lakes (≤ 8 m) keep their chop.
  - **Under water**: visibility 6 → 20 m, daylight reach 25 → 40 m; only daylight is dimmed by depth (scaled by the daylight value), so torches / lanterns / bulbs keep their light; the pass runs after the far-tree ring (order 1000 vs −100), which used to draw on top unfogged.
  - **Chunks**: chunks touching water and their neighbours are full detail; borders between two such chunks keep full detail (`ResolveFineBorderSides`) — snapping to the coarse lattice flattened a river channel crossing a border into a ridge. **Far Terrain - Far Sink (m)** 2.5 → 12.
  - **Fly camera**: `TerrainFlyCameraAltimeter` (elevation + height above ground / water depth).
  - **Sea, later the same day** (Mark: "the frequencies are too matched and perfect so it's just the same repeating pattern"; shore waves "going the long way … water never crosses a certain point, it should be sloped based, height based"; a distant river "reflection" blob): 24 waves — 14 swells 17–192 m + 10 chop 2–15 m, log-spread with jitter (no harmonics) — each modulated by its own **wave-group envelope** (0.15–1 over 6–14 of its wavelengths, drifting at the group speed; `c_vSeaGroups` / `SeaWaves.GroupEnvelope`, amplitudes ×1.3 to keep the height, the crest budget for the envelopes' peak) and evaluated at a gently **warped position** (`SeaWarp` / `SeaWaves.Warp`, ≤ ~4 m over 60–110 m) so crossing trains never lock into a lattice. Swell amplitudes publish as `SeaSwellA..D` (D = a12, a13, crest Q, total). **Shore**: the chop fades out in the shallows (0.15× under 0.5 m deep, full from 4 m; mirrored in `SeaWaves`); **breakers** — crests parallel to the waterline rolling in over 0.2–6 m deep, two per lap period, peaked and shoaling (`SeaBreakers`, **BreakerMeters** 0.5); **laps run up the sand by height** — within **RunUpMeters** (15, × shore energy) of the carve's waterline the surface is NOT ducked or discarded: each lap's front rushes in from ~10 m out and up to the reach as a sheet tallest at the front (**ShoreSurgeMeters** 0.45), then drains, and the terrain hides the sheet wherever the ground stands higher — so a lap runs far up a flat beach and a little way up a steep one; ocean rim shores full energy, lakes a quarter, × the weather's swell (`SeaShoreEnergy`). Breakers and laps are not in the CPU mirror (a boat in the surf zone rides the open-water sum only). **One swell carries all the way in** (2026-10-26, Mark: "I should be able to follow a single wave all the way to the shore where it kinda fizzles out"): on the ocean the swell is no longer depth-gated (openness 1 everywhere past the land edge; Swell Min / Full Depth now apply to lakes only). Over the last `SEA_BEND_OUTER` 1200 m → `SEA_BEND_INNER` 150 m of shelf each swell's phase is blended from the wind phase into a shore-bound one (`SeaSwellPhase` / `SeaWaves.SwellPhase`: equal to the wind phase on the bend circle, then advancing straight in toward the world centre — the ocean coast is the land-edge circle), so every crest stays one continuous line that curves toward the beach (its along-shore tilt is kept, so oblique swell peels along the beach); its travel direction follows. Toward the beach the sea becomes one train: the strongest swell (`SwellState.Peak`, published `SeaSwellE.x`) carries the root-mean-square of all swells (`SeaSwellE.y`) in its own wave groups (the sets), the others fade to a fifth, a swell heading out to sea arrives at 35 %; it shoals (up to 1.5× under 20 m), is depth-limited against its own height at 0.45 × depth, and breaks — under 3.5 m its crest sharpens toward folding (Q·k·a → 0.5) and whitens (`swellBreak` foam, 0.4–5 m deep). Ocean laps are timed by that swell's crests reaching the stretch of shore (`SeaSwellArrival`, one lap per crest, as tall as its group) and fade in / out so the timing seam along the shore draws nothing; `SeaBreakers` now serves lakes only. All of it is mirrored in `SeaWaves.SurfaceHeightMeters` except the laps. **Glints** (both water shaders): cap falls from 40 to 4 with the pixel footprint (1.5 m), faded at grazing views (N·V < 0.15), NaN / Inf zeroed — a far river showed bloomed white blobs with a purple smear.
- **Water round 5 — the sea rebuilt on a travel field** (2026-10-26, Mark: the ocean "doesn't look so great … opus got super confused"; wants water physics "completely real", waves washing up the shore, big deep-ocean waves): the shore "bend" had been a LERP OF TWO PHASES (`SeaSwellPhase`), which is not a wave — its gradient is a spurious wavenumber that drew slow wide bands across the whole shelf, and the "one train near the beach" hack (13 of 14 swells cut to a fifth) made the sea parallel ridges. Now every swell's phase is k × (dir · warped p + detour) − ωt with the detour from **`SeaSwellTravelField`**: a first-order fast march of |∇S| = n(d) (n = c_deep / c(d) for a 90 m reference swell) from the upwind half of a ring 1.8 km past the land edge inward over the carve's ocean depth, 40 m nodes, built on a worker thread in ~0.3 s, uploaded as RGBA32F `SeaSwellField` (R detour, GB ∇S, A exposure = exp(−detour / 2.5 km × ln 2) — the lee of the island is calm, waves wrap round it along the shore, nothing is blended). Lakes and inland cells read as plane waves; the march continues four cells onto the land (index 8) so the run-up strip has values, and that land march is left out of the exposure. The swell heading is LATCHED per session (`_seaSwellHeading`, rebuilt only on a > 25° change — the wind wanders ±10°; real swell comes from distant weather); the chop follows the live wind. Per swell: amplitude × exposure × its own linear shoaling coefficient (`SeaShoal`); the set is capped at the breaking height **0.39 × depth** (`SEA_BREAKING_RATIO`, H/d 0.78) + Shore Lap — crest estimate 2 × rms of the shoaled amplitudes — and the excess is the `breaking` amount: crests sharpen toward folding and `swellBreak` foam appears on the crest tops. Grid 384 → 512 segments (spacing 0.0123 r), fade relaxed to 4 spacings, spectrum width 0.6 → 0.9 so the long swells carry height offshore. **Weather**: `_seaEnergy01` follows WindSystem base + 0.6 × gust over **Sea - Swell Energy Follow (s)** (40) — gusts never reached the swell before (a held `windtest 4` builds a storm sea in a minute). Shore distance is now `SeaShoreX` (land-edge circle / traced lake shoreline, one lookup — the depth-gradient estimate cost four depth evaluations per pixel). `SeaBreakers` is lake-only and skipped on the ocean. Shore knobs moved from seawater.vmat to the component (**Sea - Shore Surge (m)** 0.45, **Run-Up Reach (m)** 15, **Lake Lap Period (s)** 7, **Shore Foam Width (m)** 4, **Lake Breaker Height (m)** 0.5 → `SeaShore` / `SeaShore2`). All of the sea's depth / wave / shore code lives in **`shaders/sea_waves.hlsl`**, included by seawater.shader and by the new **foam + wet-sand memory** (`TerrainWorldManager.SeaFoamMemory.cs`, `shaders/sea_foam_memory.shader`): a 512² RGBA16F ping-pong under the camera (640 m square, texel-snapped), blitted from a command list on the scene camera at `Stage.AfterOpaque` (`SetRenderTarget` / `Blit` / `ClearRenderTarget` — Push/PopRenderTarget are not public in 26.10.13c) — R = foam where crests break, laps run and whitecaps fold (fades over 3.5 s), G = wet where water stands now, on the sand only while a lap's sheet is there (dries over 45 s). seawater.shader adds R to its foam (**MemoryFoamStrength** 0.6, faded over the rect's last 40 m and past a footprint of 2–8 m); pixel_lit's `SeaWetMemory` darkens the terrain splat by G within 0.8–1.6 m above sea level (`SeaFoamRect` / `SeaFoamInfo`). Whitecaps scale with the weather's height factor and vanish once a pixel covers metres of water (they averaged into white slabs on the horizon); the lace is taken at the displaced point (at the grid point the Gerstner sideways shift smeared it into marbled swirls). Swim: the sea trigger box under the camera follows the drawn surface every frame (`TickSeaVolumeUnderCamera`); the rest stay flat at sea level + 0.3 m. `SeaWaves.cs` mirrors all of it (`WaveSum`, cached `Spectrum` — it allocated two arrays per call every frame); `sea_probe` prints the field (detour, travel, exposure), the sea energy and the breaking height. Verified by eye: windward beach from 70 m (crest lines parallel to the shore breaking white in bands), from the grass at eye level, lee coast calm with the swell running along it, deep sea at 25 m default and storm; NOT yet: rivers (unchanged, still flat and muddy — next), lakes after the change, swimming on the moving trigger, boats, perf_suite, the foam lace up close (still reads busy).
- **Voxel digging prototype** (2026-10-26, Mark: "I wanted voxel terrain so I can dig out chunks of it! I want this game to be a digging game for sure" — chose TRUE voxels with caves and tunnels, and simulated water that flows into holes later; "do voxel terrain in a new scene for now"): `Code/World/Voxel/` + `Assets/scenes/voxelTest.scene` (the standalone prototype) and, since 2026-10-29, the live world (**voxel columns**, end of this bullet). `VoxelField` = one dense grid of solidness samples (metres, > 0 ground, capped ±4 m) seeded from a height function (procedural hills, or the world recipe's sampler via `TerrainHeightQuery`), 0.5 m cells, 32³-cell chunks (16 m), 8×8 across × as many up as the heights need (origin 24 m under the lowest ground, 8 m head room); rounded-puck brushes (a circle of the radius with a flat floor h = radius × Brush Flatness 0.55 under the centre and a top 2 h up so the well cuts through a higher surface instead of leaving a lip — the lips were the 'tears'; fill is the mirror; true signed distance `PuckDistance`) dig (`min( f, d )`) / fill (`max( f, −d )`) and report the dirty chunks (a border sample dirties both sides). `VoxelMesher` = naive surface nets per chunk (no marching-cubes tables): one vertex per straddling cell at the mean of its edge crossings, one quad per crossing edge, the ring of cells one past the chunk on its low sides meshed too so border quads find their vertices (duplicated, never shared — no cracks); normals from the field gradient; splat inputs by depth under the ORIGINAL surface (sand on the first 1.5 m of a dug wall, rock past 4–8 m) so `terrain_ground.vmat` paints soil and rock with no new material. Engine winding: with the base sample solid the quad is (a, c, b), (a, d, c) — the other order culled every top face and showed the hills from inside. `VoxelTerrain` seeds on a worker thread (`SeedAsync`, 8.5 M samples in ~270 ms), one child object per chunk (`ModelRenderer` + static `ModelCollider` from one `ModelBuilder` with collision + trace mesh), 6 builds in flight / 3 models a frame, brushes host-authoritative (`RequestBrush` → `[Rpc.Host]` radius + bounds check → `[Rpc.Broadcast( HostOnly | Reliable )]`), the edit log `WorldSaves/<World Name>/voxel_edits.json` replayed on load (exact and tiny; chunk snapshots when edits get numerous). `VoxelDigger`: Attack1 digs / Attack2 fills where the scene camera looks (fly cam or pawn), Reach 8 m, brush 1.5 m. Console (`VoxelCommands.cs`, on whichever `IVoxelBrushTarget` the scene has — the prototype or the world) `voxel_dig [r] [x y z]`, `voxel_fill`, `voxel_flatten`, `voxel_slope`, `voxel_brush <r>`, `voxel_info`, `voxel_reset`, `voxel_probe <x> <y> [axis]`. Dig / fill are a FLATTENED scoop (**Brush Flatness** 0.55 = vertical radius share — Mark 2026-10-27: a perfect sphere "will feel weird"); **The rake** (Reload / R, modes `Flatten` / `Slope`, `voxel_flatten` / `voxel_slope`, F swaps; Mark 2026-10-29: "EXACTLY like enshrouded" — flat or slanted at the max walkable 45°, a circle shows where, the click "fills the hole into the same height as the surrounding terrain or flattens out a lump"): `VoxelField.TryPlanFlatten` reads the surface on a ring just outside the brush (median height → horizontal plane; least-squares plane with grade ≤ `MaxSlopeGrade` 1 → slope), `Flatten` writes that plane's signed height inside the circle — ground above cut (≤ 2 r + 1 m), air below filled down to the first existing ground so caves keep their roof — fading over 0.5 m; the digger draws the ring on the planned plane each frame (blue / orange, red = nothing to level). The blur, the levelling-to-aim smooth and the grid tighten before it are gone. Block style is gone too: the dig / fill brush is a true signed-distance puck (block style's hard jump beside continuous ground tore the rims into shards). The edit log carries `Mode`. voxelTest ships with **Save Edits** OFF (Mark: the scene resets when play ends). 2026-10-27 (Mark after trying it: pieces left floating): `VoxelField.RemoveFloating` flood-fills the ground in the stroke's box + 16 cells (8 m; was 8 cells, which let a ledge cut loose by a second stroke reach the box edge and survive — Mark 2026-10-28, "a section of the terrain floating in the air suspended by nothing") after EVERY stroke, dig / fill / tighten, and turns every six-connected piece that never reaches the box boundary into air (pure function of the field: replay and peers agree). The chunk machinery (objects, dirty queue, builds) is `VoxelChunkSet`, shared with the world; the edit rows / log / brush-target interface are `VoxelEditLog.cs` (a rake row carries its plane so a replay is deterministic). **Voxel columns in the live world** (2026-10-29, Mark: "implement the voxel terrain in our terrain test"; `TerrainWorldManager.Voxel.cs`, `TerrainWorldManager : IVoxelBrushTarget`, VoxelDigger on the TerrainWorld object in terrainTest): a 64 m chunk the player digs / fills / rakes gets a `VoxelField` (0.5 m cells, 128 + 2 × 2 margin cells a side, Depth Below Ground 24 m under its lowest meshed point to Head Room 8 m over its highest) seeded by bilinear sampling of the chunk's own `HeightsZ` grid (neighbours' grids for the margin), splat from the same vertices (`BuildResult.Colors` / `Splat`, kept on `LoadedChunk`), baked per sample column on the main thread (~35 ms once); its `VoxelChunkSet` lives under a `VoxelColumn (x,y)` root; once the first meshes land the heightfield chunk's renderer + collider go off (`LoadedChunk.Voxel.Swapped`, tree colliders keep following range via `LoadedChunk.VegetationNear`) and the grass tiles regenerate without clumps; trees / props / scavs stay, nav is not rebaked. Seams: the margin makes both columns hold the shared ground and `VoxelMesher.Clip` moves outside-cell vertices onto the square's edge (interpolated toward the inside neighbour; corners to the corner) so both land identical edge vertices (probed 2 mm). Host plans a rake stroke once over the world surface (`WorldSurfaceZMeters`), broadcasts, every peer applies the stroke to every loaded chunk within radius + 4 m, creating columns; log `WorldSaves/<WorldName>/voxel_edits.json`, replayed onto a column when its chunk loads; columns die with their chunk. A brightness step along a chunk border is the heightfield's own interior-only smoothing crease, not a seam. Group **Voxel** on the manager: Voxel Digging, Depth Below Ground, Head Room, Max Brush Radius, Brush Flatness, Save Edits, Log Edits. What is left (3-D height queries for scatter / grass / nav, water as a cell automaton on the same grid, generator-authored caves, sparse storage) and the open questions (cell size, dig yields, bedrock depth, shovel item) are in `Code/World/Voxel/docs/VOXEL.md`. **The pawn's tools dig since 2026-11-02** (`PlayerTerraform` on basicplayer, the owner-intent / host-check pattern of farming): the **pickaxe** (`ToolPickaxe`, equipment action `Mine`, profiles for the five `pickaxe_*` items, `prefabs/tools/pickaxe_tool.prefab`) chips a shallow disc **lying on the surface where you look** (`VoxelBrushMode.Chip`: the dig puck with its axis along the hit normal, Chip Radius 0.6 m, Chip Depth 0.3 m, hold to repeat every 0.5 s; Mark: "removes just a little bit at a time in a circle projected onto the surface ... not a sphere!"); the **hoe** grades (the rake) and paints grass / bare ground (see *Farming*). `VoxelDigger` stands down while a playable pawn is up (`TerrainTestPlayerSpawn.PlayableActive`). The edit row carries `Nx Ny Nz D` for a chip; `VoxelReplay.Apply` is the one replay for prototype, columns, load and peers; `VoxelBrushRings` draws the rake / surface rings for digger and tools.
- **Biome specks**: `TerrainPreviewLandDiskFields.TryGetSpeckMerge` feeds the continuous picker so live terrain matches the speck-filtered raster (Clover / Redwood / Amber / Ivory Alps).

### Vehicles (dune buggy)

- **`Vehicle`** (`Code/Vehicles/Vehicle.cs` + `.Physics` / `.Seats` / `.Fuel` / `.Health` partials) on `Assets/prefabs/build/vehicle_dune_buggy.prefab`: a host-spawned networked `Rigidbody` placed with the build hammer (`"vehicle": true` on `vehicle_dune_buggy` in `build_pieces.json`, size in `BuildModuleDimensions.SizesMeters`). It is **not** a `BuildPiece`: `BuildAuthority.HostPlacePiece` returns right after `HostNetworkSpawn` for vehicle data, `BuildPieceCollider.Ensure` leaves prefabs with a `Vehicle` alone, the nav baker skips the `vehicle` tag, no structural solve. Every drive / steer / suspension / fuel / health number is a property on the prefab's Vehicle component (meters, m/s, m/s², Hz); the prefab bakes them, so changing a code default does nothing until the prefab is re-saved.
- **Physics** (`Vehicle.Physics.cs`): four raycast springs from the wheel mounts (spring + damper from `SuspensionFrequencyHz` / `SuspensionDampingRatio` and the chassis mass); a ray that starts inside the ground or meets a surface facing away from the chassis (normal · up < 0.35 — a buggy lying on its side) is not a grounded wheel (counting it used to fire full compression and shove the chassis upright). **Tyres use GTA V's traction curve** (Mark 2026-10-21: "feel like GTA 5"; field meanings from Eddlm's 5mods handling.meta guide): grip rises with slip angle (sine) to `TyreGripCoefficient` (fTractionCurveMax, μ 0.9) at `TyrePeakSlipDegrees` (fTractionCurveLateral — 10°; GTA's usual 22.5 is too soft on this model, the body swings out before it bites), then falls linearly to `TyreSlideGripCoefficient` (fTractionCurveMin, 0.75) over the same angle again; `TyreGripBiasFront` (fTractionBiasFront, 0.48) scales front × 2·bias, rear × 2·(1−bias) so the tail stays planted. The side force is that grip × the wheel's own spring load (never more than would cancel the slip in one step), and the friction circle is **side-first**: drive / brake get only the grip the corner leaves over — an even split let throttle steal the rear's side grip and spin it out in a planar sim. Before this the side force tried to cancel all slip in one step, which saturated every tyre in any real turn, squeezed the drive force to nothing (W + A topped out at ~27 mph) and let the rear drift. Forces are applied at the **ground contact** with `Rigidbody.ApplyForceAt` (Mark 2026-10-04: rollover must come out of the physics, not a scripted roll). A hard turn loads the outside wheels and unloads the inside ones; whether it slides, tips onto two wheels or rolls over its cage falls out of speed, steering angle, slope, bumps, the Rigidbody's mass centre (z +2 u) and the track. Rigidbody angular damping is 0.3 (was 1.5, which soaked up every roll). **Rollover is entirely physics** (Mark 2026-10-21: "flipping was a hard set mechanic … if you take a sharp turn and hit a bump at the same time the dune buggy can roll over"): the 2026-10-06 lateral-g capped lock (`SteerLockDegreesAtSpeed`, `SteerLateralLimitG` / `…TopSpeedG` / `…Curve`) decided the roll speed by script and is deleted — steering is now GTA's speed-limited lock done as a **slip limit**, not a lateral-g target: `ApplySteerSlipAssist` lets the front wheels turn at most `SteerSlipAssistFraction` (0.8) × `TyrePeakSlipDegrees` past the direction the front axle is travelling, so the fronts sit just under peak grip at any speed (full `MaxSteerDegrees` 26 below ~2–6 m/s and in reverse), ramped at `SteerSpeedDegreesPerSecond` (110). It never asks for more than peak grip, so whether the buggy rolls is still tyre grip vs. the tip point plus whatever the ground adds. Planar sim (no load transfer): W + A from rest climbs to the 54 m/s cap on a widening line with the tail ~6° out. The airborne angular-damping swap and self-right torque (`AirborneAngularDamping`, `AirborneUprightStrength`) are deleted too: in the air the chassis keeps the spin it earned, under the Rigidbody's own 0.3 damping. What decides a roll now is **tyre grip against the tip point**: static tip ≈ half-track ÷ mass-centre height ≈ 1.19 m ÷ 1.10 m ≈ 1.08 g (≈1.04 g with body roll). The prefab's `TyreGripCoefficient` is **0.9** (was 1.3, which rolled any flat full-lock turn above ~20 mph without the cap), so a flat hard turn slides or rides on three wheels; a bump, kerb or slope during the turn adds the roll that trips it. Raise μ toward ~1.05 to roll more easily, lower it to make it slidier and harder to trip. The bump stop (`SuspensionBumpStopMultiple` 3 × static wheel load) stays: it is a spring limit, not a roll rule, and stops a fully compressed landing launching the chassis. `vehicle_rolltest` holds its entry speed through the turn (wide open it reached 37 mph on a "25 mph" run). Earlier (2026-10-04, mass centre +2, μ 1.2, 32° linear): 30 / 45 mph slid flat, 60 rolled. **Collider**: the root `ModelCollider` uses `dune_buggy.vmdl`'s own physics hull — one convex `SingleHull` of the body mesh (cage, nose, rear, A-arms; friction 0.8) — so the hoop can't sink into the ground and the buggy can rest on its side or roof; the old 168×90×34 box only covered the floor pan. **W** drives up to `MaxSpeedMetersPerSecond` (54 ≈ 120 mph; doubled from 27 on 2026-10-04, Mark: too slow); **S** brakes while rolling forward and only reverses once stopped (`ReverseSpeedMetersPerSecond` 7); W does the mirror when rolling backwards (Mark: wheels never spin against momentum). No pedal / on the limiter → `RollingDragMetersPerSecond2`; tank empty → the engine is dead, W does nothing and the buggy coasts down at `OutOfFuelCoastDragMetersPerSecond2` (1.5, ~35 s from the cap; S still brakes) — Mark 2026-10-04. Steering lock is the slip-limited `ApplySteerSlipAssist` (see Rollover). Measured 2026-10-02: 0–60 in ~4 s, 60→0 in ~2.5 s, a left turn of 150° in 3 s with the chassis level. Pedals also wake a sleeping body.
- **Who simulates**: the owner. Host-owned at spawn; `HostTryEnter` for seat 0 calls `Network.AssignOwnership(driver)` so the driver's WASD drives with no round trip; `HostExit` / the 2 Hz occupancy sweep drops ownership back to the host. `[Sync] SyncedSpeedMetersPerSecond` feeds the HUD everywhere.
- **Seats** (`Vehicle.Seats.cs`, `VehicleSeat` children, `SeatOccupants` NetList): first in = driver (seat 0), later = passengers. The pawn half is `PlayerMovement.Vehicle.cs` (`[Sync(FromHost)] SeatedVehicleId / SeatedSeatIndex`, `HostSetSeated`, owner applies via `[Rpc.Owner]`): the owner parents its root under the seat object, sets `Rigidbody.MotionEnabled=false`, **disables the controller's `ColliderObject`** (the stock controller re-enables the collider components every frame — a seated pawn whose capsule stays on pins the chassis dead; found 2026-10-02), turns `UseInputControls` / `UseAnimatorControls` off, and re-pins to the seat each frame. **The owner RPC outruns the synced id** (the RPC is sent at once, `[Sync]` values with the next network tick): a remote owner applies the RPC, records `_seatAppliedVehicleId` and holds `_seatAwaitingSync` until `SeatedVehicleId` agrees; only a synced change with no RPC behind it (vehicle wrecked, or the enter RPC could not find the vehicle yet) makes `TickSeatedPin` follow the sync. Before 2026-11-18 the pin read the not-yet-arrived id as "host cleared my seat" and threw a client straight back out the frame after it sat down, then left it in the sit pose outside the buggy (Rumple, listen-server playtest; the host never saw it because its own sync is immediate). Exit re-parents to the scene at the host-computed spot: **seat side first, the other side if that spot is under the ground or inside something, else the roof** (Mark 2026-10-06 — he kept falling through the earth when the side ray started inside a hill and found nothing). `Vehicle.ComputeExitPosition`: a side spot counts only when the ray from the hull centre to it at mid-hull height is clear, a floor lies under it within 3 m of the hull bottom and is not a steep bank, and two sphere overlaps (legs, chest) are free; the roof is never under the ground because the vehicle stands there. `vehicle_info` prints `lastExit`. No jump / roll / grapple / augment / harvest while seated; the **driver** also loses Attack1 / Attack2 (`PreInputSeated`, `PlayerCombat` owner gate, `CombatAuthority` reject, bow host gate) — passengers keep weapons. Death ejects (`Vehicle.HostEjectIfSeated` from `PlayerVitals`).
- **Pose** (`PlayerAnimation.Vehicle.cs`): the citizen animgraph's chair sit (`CitizenAnimationHelper.Sitting = Chair`) with this component feeding the graph what the controller's animator pass normally does (grounded, zero velocity, `WithLook` from the eye angles with body weight 0), ticked from OnUpdate + OnPreRender. Works only because `PlayerMovement.Vehicle` turns `UseAnimatorControls` off while seated — with the pass on, the controller rewrote the graph every frame and the sit never showed (2026-10-02). The root and the Body child are both pinned to the seat (`LocalRotation = Identity`, re-asserted each frame — the controller yaws the Body child, not the root, so without this the torso kept the entry look direction, Mark 2026-10-04); torso faces forward, head and eyes follow the camera. `vehicle_look <pitch> <yaw>` points the eyes from the console.
- **Flip** (`Vehicle.Flip.cs`, Mark 2026-10-04; physics shove 2026-10-06): `[Sync] IsFlipped` = fewer than two wheels down with the chassis tipped past ~45° (a level buggy in the air is not flipped). Then the prompt reads "Flip Dune Buggy" instead of Drive / Ride (boarding is refused) and E sends `RpcHostFlipVehicle` → host checks reach, state and a 1 s cooldown once → `[Rpc.Owner]` on the body's owner starts a **shove**: for `FlipPushSeconds` (0.3) the simulating machine applies `FlipPushWeightMultiple` (0.6) × weight with `Rigidbody.ApplyForceAt` at the hull point a ray from the pusher meets (walked down from the bounds top to chest height — the bounds top is a wheel tip, not the cage), pointed away from the pusher with `FlipPushLiftFraction` (0.5) of it upward. Nothing writes velocities (the old hop + spin was "not physics based at all"): rock back, slide or go over falls out of the lever, mass centre and ground, so the side you push from decides the roll — push from the roof side and it lands on its wheels, from the wheel side and it goes onto its roof. Values are authored on the prefab (a code default alone does nothing — the compiled prefab keeps its own copy). `vehicle_flip [roof|wheels|front|back]` presses it from the console, first standing the pawn off that face; `vehicle_tip [side|roof]` lays a buggy over to test on.
- **Interaction** (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Vehicle.cs`): look at the hull → "Drive Dune Buggy" / "Ride in Dune Buggy" / "… is full"; E boards (owner trace → `RpcHostEnterVehicle`, host re-checks `IsWithinUseReach`). While seated E exits and every other Tick* is skipped; `ToolBuildHammer` stands down entirely while seated (its hardcoded E snap cycle would fire on the exit press). An F-to-board / strafe / Q-E-rotate scheme was tried and reverted the same day (Mark 2026-10-08: "restore the control scheme"). The storage box child is tagged `vehiclestorage`: E on it opens the chest instead (`TryTraceOpenableContainer` refuses a vehicle container unless the hit is the box). A trace against the chassis rigidbody reports the **root** object even when the ray lands on the storage child's collider (compound body), so `Vehicle.IsStorageHit(hit, hitPosition)` decides by the hit point against the box collider's bounds (`IsInsideStorageBox`), not by the hit object; `vehicle_info` prints that trace.
- **Storage + fuel** (`Vehicle.Fuel.cs`, `ContainerInventory` restricted slot): the prefab's `ContainerInventory` ("Buggy Storage", 9 slots, 3 columns) has `RestrictedSlotIndex 8` = `crafted_fuel` only (`SlotAccepts` / `DragAccepts` / `AbsorbRespectingRestriction` guard every place, swap, drop, half, absorb, quick-move and `HostDepositStack`; the UI paints the slot amber with `RestrictedSlotLabel`). That slot is the tank: host mirrors its count into `[Sync] FuelUnits`; the simulating machine accumulates throttle time and burns one unit per `SecondsPerFuelUnit` (60) through `HostTryRemoveFromSlot` (clients via `RpcHostBurnFuel`, driver-only). The fuel is the existing **Fuel** item (`crafted_fuel`, 1 crude oil, stack 10) — a separate free Gasoline item was dropped 2026-10-04 because Mark reached for Fuel.
- **Health** (`Vehicle.Health.cs`): the prefab's `DamageReceiver` routes to `Vehicle.HostApplyDamage` (new branch in `DamageReceiver.TakeDamage`; `CombatAuthority` alive / dedup know vehicles). `MaxHealth` 100 = ten light sword hits. At zero: everyone out, every storage stack (fuel too) becomes a world drop around the wreck, object destroyed.
- **HUD**: `Code/UI/VehicleHud.cs` (mph, fuel units / NO FUEL, controls hint) on the passive HUD tick; prompt text goes through `FocusedVehicle` in `PlayerScreenHud`.
- **Model** (`Blender/scripts/create_dune_buggy.py` → `Assets/models/vehicles/`): `dune_buggy.vmdl` (tube cage, seats, striped hood plate, coil-overs, canister; origin = chassis centre; its `PhysicsShapeList` single hull is the chassis collider — the script's `BODY_PHYSICS` writes it) and `dune_buggy_wheel.vmdl` (knobby tyre + spoked rim, axle = local Y, origin = hub) on one 64 px palette atlas (`buggy_atlas.png/.vmat`, pixel_lit, 8 px cells; big cells planar-mapped for the plate stripes and rim spokes). The prefab's `WheelFL/FR/RL/RR` children carry the wheel model; `Vehicle.UpdateWheelVisuals` places them at the mounts minus spring travel, yaws the fronts by steer and pitches all four by travel / circumference, so any wheel mesh with its axle on Y spins correctly. The script models forward = Blender +X and bakes a −90° yaw into the mesh at export (`EXPORT_YAW`) because Blender +X lands on the engine's +Y — without it the hood faced the buggy's left and the chest looked bolted to its side. The chest is the prefab's `Storage` child, a 0.5 × 0.8 × 0.45 m box hung on the rear plate at x −1.6 m. The whole model is scaled 1.4x at export (`SCALE`) so the 1.8 m citizen sits inside the cage; wheel base 3.36 m, track 2.38 m, wheel radius 0.49 m, spring 0.62 m on the prefab match it. Seat anchors sit at (11, ±22, -2) u: the citizen sit pose puts the pelvis ~0.45 m above the pawn root, which lands it on the 0.28 m cushion. Rebuild: `blender -b --python Blender/scripts/create_dune_buggy.py`, then recompile the two vmdls.
- **Dev console** (`Code/Vehicles/VehicleDevCommands.cs`, host only): `vehicle_spawn [id] [metersAhead]`, `vehicle_enter`, `vehicle_exit`, `vehicle_fuel [n]`, `vehicle_stash <id> [n]`, `vehicle_storage`, `vehicle_drive <throttle> <steer> [s]`, `vehicle_test` (timed script: throttle / brake / reverse / turn / fuel with 0.5 s samples; teleports to x=600 first because the test plane ends near x=15000), `vehicle_damage [n]`, `vehicle_info`, `vehicle_flip [roof|wheels|front|back]`, `vehicle_tip [side|roof]`, `vehicle_rolltest [mph] [turnSeconds] [steer]` (`VehicleDevCommands.RollTest.cs`: up to speed, held steer, hands off; logs lowest up.z, times over, whether it ended flipped). **Scripted tests and `vehicle_drive` need no driver** (2026-10-06): `FindTestVehicle` takes the vehicle the local pawn drives, else the nearest one, and `TryGetSimulatedInput( vehicle, … )` feeds only that vehicle on its simulating machine — so tuning runs on an empty buggy while Mark keeps his pawn. `vehicle_demo` (`VehicleDevCommands.Demo.cs`): three dev-box ramps on the +x run, a chase `CameraComponent` named VehicleDemoCamera (id logged — pass it to the editor's `camera_screenshot`), scripted ramps → left sweep → right sweep → brake → reverse; the scratchpad client that captures it at ~27 fps talks to the editor MCP endpoint over HTTP directly.

#### Hover platform (Mark 2026-10-08: "a flat floating platform that players can build on")

- **Same `Vehicle` component, `DriveModel = Hover`** (`Code/Vehicles/Vehicle.Hover.cs`) on `Assets/prefabs/build/vehicle_hover_platform.prefab` (`vehicle_hover_platform` in `build_pieces.json`, 6 × 4 × 1.5 m ghost in `BuildModuleDimensions.SizesMeters`). Seats, fuel slot, health, prompts, HUD, exit, dev commands and the deck rules all run through the existing paths; `Vehicle.Physics.cs` branches to `SimulateHover` before any wheel code, so a hover vehicle has no wheels, no steer lock and is never `IsFlipped`.
- **Deck = 3 × 2 build tiles** (6 × 4 m, 0.4 m slab, origin at the slab centre, deck top at z +8 u). "3x2" was read as build modules so floor pieces tile it — the slab size is one number in `Blender/scripts/create_hover_platform.py` (`DECK_L` / `DECK_W`) plus the `SizesMeters` row if that reading is wrong. Four lift pods underneath, hazard stripes along the long edges, a pallet-truck style **steering console at the front centre** (pedestal + tilted screen + handlebar) driven **standing** from behind it, and a small **cell bay** at the front-right corner (the `vehiclestorage` child: E on it opens the one-slot "Power Cells" container; E anywhere else on the hull boards).
- **Hover physics**: four lift pads (`HoverPadLengthMeters` × `HoverPadWidthMeters`) each trace straight down (world down) up to max height + 10 m; the **highest** ground under the hull is the reference so the deck clears a slope flat instead of leaning into it; each pad carries its share of the weight outright plus a soft spring + damper (`HoverLiftFrequencyHz` 1, ratio 1, limit 2.5 × pad weight) toward `HoverTargetHeightMeters` + `HoverHullBottomMeters` (0.55, origin → pod underside) above it (without the weight term a bare spring sagged g / ω² = 0.5 m, so the deck sat at 0.5 m at the 1 m minimum — measured 2026-10-08). A pad with no ground in reach carries its weight share with vertical damping (floats on over a chasm). `HoverLevelStrength` / `HoverLevelDamping` torque the deck flat (yaw free). Gravity stays on; nothing writes velocities.
- **Ice drive** (controls Mark 2026-10-08: "E to enter, A and D are for steering, W and S for forward / backward … if A and D are pressed while W or S are not, the craft should spin on a dime"): W / S thrust along the deck's planar forward with the buggy's brake-before-reverse contract (`MaxSpeedMetersPerSecond` **17.9 = 40 mph**, accel 4, brake 5, rolling drag 0.8); A / D are a yaw torque — with W or S held toward `HoverYawRateDegreesPerSecond` (40), with neither held toward `HoverSpinRateDegreesPerSecond` (60) so it spins on the spot — both with `HoverYawResponse` 2.5. Sideways speed only bleeds at `HoverSideGripPerSecond` (0.7 /s) so the velocity lags the heading and it drifts through turns. W / S (and Space / Ctrl) burn cells; turning alone is free. HUD mph is the planar speed (a sideways slide still reads as speed).
- **Height**: the driver's **jump** action (Space) raises and **sneak** action (Ctrl) lowers `HoverTargetHeightMeters` at `HoverClimbRateMetersPerSecond` (3) between `HoverMinHeightMeters` 1 and `HoverMaxHeightMeters` 30; `PlayerMovement.PreInputSeated` leaves those two actions live for a hover driver (`Vehicle.UsesVerticalInput`) and clears them for everyone else seated; the vehicle reads them off the driver's `JumpInputAction` / `SneakInputAction`. Spawns at the minimum (`HostSettleOnGround` uses hull bottom + min height for hover).
- **Power cells**: the fuel system unchanged with `FuelResourceId = crafted_power_cell`, `SecondsPerFuelUnit` **120**, `FuelLabel` "Cells" (HUD reads "Cells 3" / "NO CELLS"). A cell burns while W / S or Space / Ctrl is held; idle hovering is free. **No power**: no thrust, no climb, and the target sinks to the minimum height at `HoverUnpoweredSinkRateMetersPerSecond` (1) — the deck never drops out of the sky onto what is built on it. Item + recipe in `crafting_recipes.json` (2 lithium + 1 copper ingot + 1 plastics → 1, stack 10, icon `ui/items/crafted_power_cell.png`).
- **Standing driver**: `VehicleSeat.Standing` on the console seat (`Seat_Driver` at (76, 0, 8) u: feet on the deck 1.9 m back from the nose, facing forward). `PlayerAnimation.Vehicle` feeds the graph the idle stance (`Sitting = None`) instead of the chair, pinned to the seat like the sit. Exit (`Vehicle.TryFindStandingExit`) steps left / right / behind the console **onto the deck** when a floor lies under the spot and the standing room is clear, else the old side / roof logic.
- **Building on the deck** (`Vehicle.BuildableDeck`): the hammer already treats the hull as a surface. On the host, `BuildAuthority.HostPlacePiece` runs `Vehicle.FindBuildHostUnder` once per placement (short rays down from the piece's lowest corners, then a box overlap for side-snapped pieces) and **parents the piece under the platform before `Configure`**, so: `BuildPieceCollider` leaves its collider non-static (it joins the hull's compound body and moves with it — a static box would stay nailed to the world), `BuildStructuralIntegrity.IsGrounded` treats a deck-mounted piece as grounded (a dynamic hull is never a world anchor), nav bake / rebake skip it (`IsVehicleHierarchy`), and the snap system sees it through the live piece transforms as usual. `Vehicle.FindOnHierarchy` **stops at a `BuildPiece`**, so a wall or chest on the deck is its own thing for prompts, damage, containers and focus (`TryFindFocusedVehicle`, `PlayerInventoryInteraction.Container`), while `Vehicle.FindDeckOnHierarchy` / `IsDeckMounted` walk through pieces to the deck. Deck pieces die with the platform (`GameObject.Destroy` takes the children; no refund). Not yet verified in the editor: networked children spawned under a client-owned hull, the controller riding a moving deck, and demolish / repair prompts on deck pieces.
- **Grapple rides moving bodies** (`Code/Player/PlayerMovement.GrappleRide.cs`, Mark 2026-10-08: the anchor stayed floating in the air when the platform moved): a plain surface hook (not a player, not a tow log) that lands on a collider whose first ancestor with an enabled, simulated `Rigidbody` exists — the platform, the buggy, a piece on the deck, a loose prop — stores that body as `[Sync(FromHost)] GrappleAttachRideId` with the hit in its local space (`GrappleAttachLocalOffset`, shared with player hooks). Decided once on the host at attach (one `FindInPhysics` overlap of ±0.1 m at the validated point). `ResolveGrappleAttachWorldPoint` — which every rope, swing, ledge and debug reader already used — recomputes it from the body each frame on every machine, so the rope follows and drags / carries the holder. Host drops the rope if the body is destroyed or ends up past 1.25 × max range. Static world hooks are unchanged; ride hooks still count as surface swings (not `IsMovingTargetAttach`).
- **Vehicle tow** (same file, Grapple Vehicle Tow group; Mark 2026-11-19: "if i drive rumple turns into a kite, and if i whip to the side hes like stuck, it should have that boat tube effect where he goes whipping to the side we just turned away from"): a ride hook no longer uses the static-anchor constraint (position snap onto the rope sphere + outward speed stripped in world space, anchor velocity never read). `ApplyGrappleRopeConstraint` hands ride hooks to `ApplyGrappleRideRopeConstraint`, which solves in the **anchor's frame**: `SampleRideAnchorVelocity` reads the velocity at the hook point (`Rigidbody.GetVelocityAtPoint` when this machine simulates the body, else the attach point differenced step to step and smoothed over 0.1 s), and a taut rope only removes the speed at which pawn and hook point *separate* — so the pawn inherits the body's speed along the rope and keeps its sideways momentum (the whip). Over-length comes back as inward speed (**Rope Stiffness (per second)** 12), never a position write; the one snap left is the safety net past 25 % + 2 m of stretch. `ApplyGrappleOverLengthCatchup` skips ride hooks. **Tow slide** (`TickGrappleTowSlide`, `IsGrappleTowSliding`): while a taut rope drags the pawn and the anchor is faster than **Slide Above Speed (meters/s)** 5, the pawn lets go of the ground (`PreventGrounding` each step) — feet on the ground brake to a stop every step, which was the "stuck" — so the controller treats it as a swing (walk muted, no brake, WASD leans against the rope through the normal pump) and it slides with **Slide Drag (per second)** 0.6; the slide ends when the pawn itself is under 60 % of that speed. No airborne-stamina drain while sliding; wall pin (`_grappleBlockedByWall`) and the wall-hit velocity kill are off while the anchor moves faster than 1 m/s (`IsGrappleRideAnchorMoving`). A parked body swings exactly like a fixed anchor. `grapple_test` drives a tagged rigidbody straight, round a 10 m circle and to a stop with a pawn hooked 6 m behind (tow speed, no kite, rope length, outside the circle, faster than the body, stands again). Not yet verified with a client-owned pawn behind a host-simulated vehicle (the finite-difference path).
- **Map pin**: `Vehicle.MapPinIcon` ("platform" → `MapPinCatalog.PlatformPinTexturePath` = `Assets/ui/map/pin_platform.png`; any catalog id also works) makes `TerrainWorldMapFace.UpdateVehicleMarkers` draw a pin-sized icon that follows the hull every map tick, labelled with `DisplayName` on the big map, for **every** player (no crew scoping — vehicles have no owner yet). It is not a saved pin: nothing to place, cross off or remove; the HUD minimap shares the face so it shows there too.
- **Dev**: `vehicle_spawn vehicle_hover_platform` drops one ahead of the pawn; `vehicle_hover [m]` sets the target height / prints `Vehicle.DebugHover()`; `vehicle_fuel` fills the cell slot (uses `FuelResourceId`). Model: `blender -b --python Blender/scripts/create_hover_platform.py` → `Assets/models/vehicles/hover_platform.fbx/.vmdl` (render mesh `hover_platform`, physics = a single hull of the `hover_platform_phys` slab + pods object in the same fbx) + `platform_atlas.png/.vmat` (64 px palette atlas, pixel_lit). Upgrades Mark has named for later: bigger deck, faster, in-vehicle storage — the deck size, speed numbers and the container are all prefab / script values already.
#### Pod scooter (Mark 2026-11-19: "a kinda small moped scooter inside the pod all folded up that the player can pick up and deploy whenever they want")

- **Same `Vehicle` component, `DriveModel = Bike`** (`Code/Vehicles/Vehicle.Bike.cs`) on `Assets/prefabs/build/vehicle_pod_scooter.prefab` (`vehicle_pod_scooter` in `build_pieces.json`, `"hidden": true` - never a hammer piece; the `SizesMeters` row only serves `vehicle_spawn`). Two wheels on the centre line (`WheelCount` 2: wheel 0 front, 1 rear; `WheelMountLocal`, the spring share and the tyre loop in `Vehicle.Physics.cs` all go through it), one seat, **60 mph** (`MaxSpeedMetersPerSecond` 26.8), 60 hp, mass 140.
- **Round wheels** (Mark: "the wheels need to be like actually perfect"): a bike wheel is a **sphere of the wheel radius swept down the spring travel** (`TraceBikeWheel`), not a ray from the hub - the tyre's leading edge meets a rock or a kerb first and rolls up it instead of the hub ray stepping onto it. The sweep starts 0.75 x radius above the mount so a bottomed-out wheel is still a clean hit; a sweep that still starts inside the ground while the bike is more upright than not (up.z > 0.5) counts as a grounded, fully compressed wheel - dropping it switched the balance off mid-corner and the scooter fell over on rough terrain. Radius 0.21 m, spring 0.16 m, 2.6 Hz, damping 0.8, bump stop 4 x.
- **Balance** (`SimulateBikeBalance`): with a wheel on the ground a roll torque (PD, `BikeBalanceFrequencyHz` 2.5, ratio 1, capped at 40 rad/s^2) holds the bike at its lean angle - `atan(speed x yaw rate / g)` capped at `BikeMaxLeanDegrees` 32, followed at `BikeLeanResponse` 6 /s; upright = world up as seen across the bike, so it stays vertical on a side slope. The tyres' **side force acts level with the mass centre** (`BikeSideForcePoint`), so cornering never levers it over; drive / brake still push at the contact (squat and dive are real). Pitch and yaw are free physics.
- **Air control** (`SimulateBikeAirControl`, Mark: "if the player is mid air and they hit W it should tip it forward, same with ASD ... very slow tip"): both wheels off the ground, W / S pitch the nose down / up and A / D roll left / right as a torque toward `AirPitchRateDegreesPerSecond` (90) / `AirRollRateDegreesPerSecond` (60) with `AirControlResponse` 2 /s (half a second to get most of the way; the first tuning, 60 at 1.2 /s, moved the nose 4 deg in a 0.66 s jump - gravity here is 850 u/s^2 = 21 m/s^2, so air time is short). No key = no torque: the bike keeps whatever spin it has. Nothing rights it in the air; land on a wheel and the balance takes over, land on its side and it stays down.
- **Fallen bike**: `IsFlipped` for a bike = no wheel down and tipped past ~45 deg. The prompt reads "Stand Pod Scooter up" and E runs the balance torque for `BikeRightingSeconds` (1.5) with no wheel down (`StartBikeRighting`, through the same `HostTryFlip` -> `RpcOwnerApplyFlip` path as the buggy's shove).
- **Fuel**: one-slot `ContainerInventory` ("Fiber Hopper", `RestrictedSlotIndex 0` = `resource_plantFiber`), no storage grid. `SecondsPerFuelUnit` **12** = 5 plant fiber per minute of throttle; `FuelLabel` "Fiber". The `Hopper` child (the reactor box under the saddle, tag `vehiclestorage`) is the E-to-open part; E anywhere else rides.
- **Fold / unfold** (`Code/Vehicles/Vehicle.Stow.cs`, owner side `PlayerInventoryInteraction.VehicleStow.cs`): `Vehicle.StowItemId` (`vehicle_pod_scooter_folded`) makes a vehicle foldable. **R** (`VehicleStowAction` = Reload) on an empty one -> `RpcHostStowVehicle` -> `HostTryStow`: reach check, the item goes into the bag (refused when it does not fit), the hopper's fiber follows (ground if the bag is full), the vehicle is destroyed. The prompt reads "Drive Pod Scooter  (R: fold up)". **Attack1** with the item on the active hotbar slot -> owner aims a floor spot 1.8 m ahead (`TryFindDeployFloor`: walkable floor + standing room) -> `HostTryDeployFromItem` re-checks reach and floor once, spawns the piece's prefab like the hammer drops a vehicle, then spends the item. The item row's `deployVehicle` (`ResourceDefinitionData.DeployVehicle`) names the build piece. Hit points do not travel with the item (it unfolds whole); a wrecked scooter is gone like any vehicle.
- **In the pod** (`Code/Props/StasisPod.Scooter.cs`): every pod's `Scooter` child (folded model + box collider, docked under the seat) shows until `[Sync] ScooterTaken`. E on it once the pod has landed (`FocusedPodScooter` in `PlayerInventoryInteraction`, shares `FocusedVehicleChanged` and the vehicle prompt row in `PlayerScreenHud`) -> `HostTryTakeScooter`: item into the bag, or a world drop at the pawn if it is full. Not saved: a pod that is re-created has its scooter again.
- **Visuals**: prefab children `WheelF` / `WheelR` (spin, ride the springs), `Steer` (handlebar + column + fork tubes: yaws with the steering), `ForkLower` (sliders + mudguard: rides the front spring and yaws), `SwingArm` (pitches so its tail follows the rear hub) - `UpdateBikeVisuals`. Rider = the chair sit on `Seat_Driver`, feet on the deck.
- **Model** (`Blender/scripts/create_pod_scooter.py` -> `Assets/models/vehicles/pod_scooter/`): `pod_scooter` (frame, deck, saddle, fibre reactor, rack; `pod_scooter_phys` single hull), `_wheel`, `_steer`, `_fork`, `_swingarm`, `_folded` (both wheels on one axle, deck on end, handlebar across the top: the pod dock model and the item's world drop via `item_world_models.json`) on one 256 px atlas (`pod_scooter_atlas`, pixel_lit, 32 px cells). The script also **writes the prefab** (every tuning number is in `write_prefab`) and the 64 px item icon. Wheel base 1.10 m, saddle 0.72 m off the ground.
- **Dev**: `vehicle_spawn vehicle_pod_scooter`, `give vehicle_pod_scooter_folded`, `vehicle_deploy [itemId] [m]`, `vehicle_stow`, `pod_scooter [take|restock]`; `vehicle_drive <throttle> <steer> [s]` also feeds the air control while the wheels are off the ground; `vehicle_info` prints the lean / righting readout (`DebugBike`); `scooter_demo [rampDegrees]` (`VehicleDevCommands.ScooterDemo.cs`) lays six 12 cm kerbs and a kicker ramp ahead of the nearest scooter, drives it flat out, holds S in the air, sweeps and brakes, with a chase camera (`ScooterDemoCamera`, id logged) and a logged air time / tip / lowest up.z.

### Beds + base raids

- **Bed** (`bed` in `build_pieces.json`, `Assets/prefabs/build/bed.prefab`, 2 × 1 × 0.5 m build-kit meters, wood material so 30 HP like a wall, 10 wood): `BuildBed` on the prefab. Look at it and the prompt says whose it is ("Sleep in Your Bed" / "<name>'s Bed" / "Claim Bed and Sleep"); **E claims** an unclaimed bed, or one whose owner has left, **and climbs in**; E on your own bed climbs in; another player's bed does nothing (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Bed.cs` → `RpcHostUseBed`, host re-checks reach → `BuildBed.HostUse`). One claimed bed per player — a new claim releases the old. Owner is the connection-keyed `TimeTrialSession.ResolvePlayerKey` + name, `[Sync( FromHost )]`; a reconnect is a new player (no persistence yet). `PlayerVitals.TryResolveSpawnTransform` → `BuildBed.GetRespawnTransform` respawns **standing beside** the claimed bed while it stands (`TryFindStandSpot`: each long side, the foot, the head — clear line from the bed, walkable floor, standing room), on the mattress when no side has room, else the scene `SpawnPoint`.
- **Sleeping** (Mark 2026-11-19; `Code/Player/PlayerMovement.Bed.cs`, same shape as the vehicle seat): host `HostEnterBed` / `HostLeaveBed` write `[Sync( FromHost )] BedLockId` and RPC the owner, who turns its body keyframed, switches the controller's input + animator pass off and rests its root on the mattress every frame (`TickBedPin`). Not parented under the bed (a torn-down bed must not take the pawn with it — `BuildBed.OnDisabled` → `HostEjectFromBed`), colliders stay on. `PlayerAnimation.Bed.cs` lays the **Body child** on its back, head on the pillow, on every machine (`BuildBed.GetLieBodyTransform`; the lie frame is read once from the root box collider + the `Pillow` child); the root, eye angles and camera are untouched (`OwnsBodyFacing` is false in bed). **Getting up**: Use, Jump or a move key pressed after lying down (`PreInputBed` → `RpcHostLeaveBed`), a hit (`PlayerVitals.ApplyDamageAfterArmor`), death / dev teleport, the bed breaking, or 06:00 — the host puts the pawn on the stand spot. No move / jump / swing / block / world interaction from a bed. **Sleep**: a pawn that has lain `BuildBed.SleepDelaySeconds` (5 s) is asleep (`HostBedSleepReady`); `EnvironmentDayNightCycle.TickSleepAuthority` (host, 4×/s behind a static in-bed list, every frame while fast) starts the fast night only when **every** pawn in the scene is asleep (`PlayerMovement.HostEveryoneAsleep`) and stops it the moment one is not — one sleeper alone changes nothing. While it runs every pawn has `[Sync] BedSleeping`: the owner's screen fades to black over `BuildBed.SleepFadeSeconds` (3 s; `BedBlackout01`, drawn by the pod blackout panel in `PlayerScreenHud`) and the clock advances `SleepTimeScale` (10×) — **the clock only**, not the game's time scale — never past 06:00. The cycle is still per-peer: the host RPCs each owner (`RpcOwnerBedSleepClock`), a client runs its own clock fast, holds just short of 06:00 if it gets there first and takes the host's clock when the host stops (`ClientSetSleepFastForward`). The sleep code asks `IsSleepAuthority` (`!Networking.IsActive || Networking.IsHost`), not the cycle's older `IsWorldAuthority()` — that one reads the scene object's network state and is true on a client too. At 06:00 the host wakes everyone, `DayNumber` rolls as at any dawn and `slept_in_bed` fires for each sleeper. No fast night while the cycle is paused, day-locked or absent from the scene. Dev: `bed_use` (local pawn uses the nearest bed / gets up, client too), `bed_info`. **Enemy hit zone** (Mark, 2026-09-22 — the model is too small for a scav swing to connect): enemies treat the bed as a 2 m build-kit cube (`BuildBed.EnemyHitZoneMeters`, standing on the bed's floor). `EntityBrain.StrikeDistance` uses it for reach and stand picking, and `LandMissedBedSwing` (end of each attack cycle, host) applies the swing's damage to the bed when the sweep missed the model but the raider was in reach of the cube (no double damage when the sweep did hit). Model and collider unchanged.
- **Raids are data** (`Assets/data/raids.json`, `BaseRaidCatalog` / `BaseRaidData`): totals, initial count, wave size + interval, `baseRadiusMeters`, spawn ring distances, aggro / drop-aggro ranges, weighted enemy mix. World meters (40 u/m), converted once in `BaseRaidSession`.
- **`BaseRaidSession`** (one per scene, the `BaseRaid` object in testscene1 — the whole system lives on it): `L` (`StartBaseRaid`) starts the raid on the presser's claimed bed, else the nearest bed; `L` again calls it off. Every standing bed within `baseRadiusMeters` of that bed is a target. Host spawns `initialEnemies` at once and `waveSize` every `waveIntervalSeconds` until `totalEnemies` on random standable ground `spawnMin..MaxDistanceMeters` out, via `EntityEnemySetup.Configure` + `EntityBrain.JoinRaid`.
