Checks every 0.5 s: every bed gone → `BedsDestroyed`; every raider dead → `Defeated`. **Survivors wander** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — not a run-off): on `BedsDestroyed` / `Cancelled` each live raider gets `EntityBrain.EndRaid` → home = where it stands, normal Wander (ordinary perception again); the host despawns a straggler once it has been out ≥30 s and no live player can see it (within 100 m, inside a 60° eye-aim cone from `PlayerController.EyeAngles`, clear line — the animals' stare test), or at 120 s regardless. The short-lived `Leaving` run-off state was removed. **Stragglers** (`_isRaidStraggler`, Mark: they walked off "together in a pack"): a chased player more than 25 m away (or gone) → `WanderFromHere` (home = here, Wander); breach's "no route, nothing to breach → return home" wanders from here instead; wander legs pick any direction (not ±70° of facing). Raiders enter `Raiding` at `JoinRaid` even while still waiting for nav (they walk on foot until the agent is placed). **Nav coverage**: hand-built scenes only mesh a ±150 m bubble around the first pawn (origin in testscene1), so a raid calls `BuildNavMeshSync.EnsureNavCoversArea( center, spawnMax + 20 m )` at start — grows the nav bounds and, because the tile grid is laid out from the XY bounds (strip-only generation left that side with tiles that never joined the mesh — partial paths at ~900 u, "half of them stuck, all from one side"), runs the load-time **full regenerate from physics** (~0.5 s for the bubble; no-op in streamed worlds); nav first, then raiders (Mark): the first wave waits (0.5 s checks) while the mesh is still generating, so nobody spawns onto tiles being built. Growing the bounds + `Generate()` alone left the new area empty — raiders from that side never got onto nav. A bed outside the bubble (x 8073) left half the raiders unable to path. **Blocked approach**: piece planning normally starts inside 18 m of the bed; a raider whose route to the bed is partial and that has not closed 1 m in 3 s plans anyway, and the piece on the line to the bed counts even outside the base radius (an outer wall / fence 20 m out left raiders parked at the end of their partial path). It logs `[Raid] … blocked Nu from the bed — in the way: <piece | no build piece>`. Waves (Mark): `initialEnemies` 5 arrive together, then `waveSize` 1 every `waveIntervalSeconds` 3 s — the rest trickle in. `[Sync]` center / radius / remaining + a broadcast `BaseRaidEvent` drive `BaseRaidHud` (banner — "Your base is being raided!" to owners of raided beds — and a status line) and the red ring on the ground (DebugOverlay, placeholder) and minimap (`TerrainWorldMapFace`; without a TerrainWorld the face is a camera-centered local radar so the ring still shows).
- **Raider AI** (`EnemyAiState.Raiding`, `EntityBrain.Raid.cs`): runs at the nearest target bed on plain nav; inside 18 m of it, plans (staggered, ≥1.5 s) what to hit — the bed, else the piece on the line to the bed, else the base piece nearest the bed — through the breach helpers (`BreachClaims` 3-per-piece, `TryFindBreachStand`, swing reach); no slot free → loiters 2–5 m around the bed. Piece down → holds for the nav rebake, re-plans. Only a player within `aggroRangeMeters` (3 m, Mark 2026-09-22 — they preferred the player too much at 12) **for 2 s** → normal Chasing / Attacking / Breaching; hits from further out are ignored; back to Raiding once that player has been more than `dropAggroRangeMeters` (3 m) away **for 2 s** (dwell both ways — a player on the line flipped raiders and their paths several times a second). **Smoothness rules** (Mark: seamless, no nav cost): plans are the only costly step — at most 2 raid plans per frame scene-wide, ≤3 stand candidates each, idle re-plan every 3–5 s; a raider whose agent is still walking a path keeps walking it through a structure rebake instead of switching to on-foot stepping (the snap back was the jerking); speed is set once per move, never toggled per frame. **Rebake only for the wall being worked on** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — "minimise the amount of rebakes"): `BuildNavMeshSync.OnBuildPieceBoundsChanged` rebakes at once only when the removed piece is within 160 u of a piece some entity has claimed (`BreachClaims.AnyClaimNear` — the wall it is hitting or the one next to it); every other removal is queued as a dirty area (overlapping ones merged) and rebaked one area per tick ≥8 s after the first, only while no structural bake is pending and nothing is generating then one every 3 s; never flushed in a burst. Placements are unchanged. **Raid end** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — the bed falling was the last hitch): a raider mid-way through a wall finishes it (`_raidFinishingPiece` keeps `IsRaider` true for that piece only, no player aggro), then wanders; everyone else idles 0.3–2.5 s (random) before the first wander leg so twenty wander goals are not picked in one frame. **Body follows the agent** (`EntityLocomotion.FollowAgent`, Mark 2026-09-23 — "definitely related to the walls and the renav", every hop 10–16 u on the frame a tile came back, every scav at once): the engine's `NavMeshAgent.UpdatePosition` is never left on. Locomotion turns any `UpdatePosition = true` (spawn placement, bake-complete, landing, manual-chase handoff, animals) into an attach (`AgentDrivesBody`) and each fixed update walks the body toward `Agent.AgentPosition` (XY) at intended speed + 120 u/s — a re-seat after a rebuild is a 2–3 frame slide, never a snap — and only while the agent is walking (`Agent.IsNavigating`): an idle agent (standing / mid-swing) is re-seated 12–18 u by every tile rebuild and following it slid every standing scav on each rebake (Mark's `[Twitch] STALL … locked=True` log); `RunPathCheckTo` seats an idle agent at the body before `MoveTo`. Follow / coast run in `OnUpdate` (render rate), not the fixed tick, and `ClipMovementAgainstSolids` runs right after them there — the feet glue resets the clip origin each frame, so a render-frame step clipped only in the next fixed tick was invisible and scavs walked through walls. While coasting on a stale mesh the brain does not path; once the mesh is back it paths again (`IsCoastingOnStaleNav`) and the coast ends when the agent walks. `DetachAgent()` for manual stepping / benching (`EntityBrain.TickManualStepToward`, `AnimalBrain` bench). The brain's `IsNavAgentReady` reads `IsAgentDriving()` (flag or attach). There is no incremental nav update on this engine (generate / unload / request only). **Coast through rebakes** (`EntityLocomotion.TickRebakeCoast`, Mark 2026-09-23: "all of them teleport every second on the nose", only the scavs): every structure rebake unloads the tiles under the raiders; the engine agent on one loses its polygon, its wish goes to zero and the body freezes until the tile is back, then re-seats — every scav stalled in sync once a second. While `IsNavStale` a body with a recent wish is carried on its last velocity with `Agent.UpdatePosition` off (the clip still stops it at walls); when the mesh is back the agent is seated at the body and takes over once navigating again (cap 1.5 s carry, then hold). Brain manual stepping (`ManualStepToward` / `TickManualStepToward`) yields while `Locomotion.IsCoasting`. Standing / mid-swing bodies are detached too and hold (velocity zero) — the tile unload itself re-seated a standing agent and dragged the body ~12 u; they hand back the moment the mesh is live again. **Per-entity cost caps** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — "is there just too many of them?"): `EntityBrain.RunPathCheckTo` takes a scene-wide slot (`MaxPathQueriesPerFrame` 4; over budget → retry next frame, `LastNavBlockReason` "budget") and jitters every interval ×0.85–1.35 so lock-step timers from a same-frame spawn drift apart; `PathCrossesSolid` sweeps only the first 640 u of a route (`PathProbeMaxUnits`). `EntityCombat` telegraph / recovery are jittered ±20 % per cycle so swings desync; `EntityEnemySetup` sets `MeleeSweepSubstepLength` ≥ 40 u for entities (a third of the sweep traces per swing). `[Perf]` logs frames over 30 ms. **Entity debug is off by default** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — "the game's FPS is trash … disable the logging on all the entities"): the scav / boss prefabs no longer draw melee debug sample points (the blade trail `MeleeDebugDrawEnabled` stays on — Mark reads it as the sword swing), `ShowBlockFootprintVolume` — a Gizmo fill per entity per frame — and `EntityCombat.ShowTelegraphDebug`), and log no melee phases (`LogMeleeAttackPhaseDebug`, code default now false; the player prefab keeps its own true). `EntityPerceptionDebug.Enabled` defaults false. `[Breach]`, `[Raid]`, `[BuildPiece]` damage, `[Loco]` hop and `[Twitch]` lines are all behind `nav_trace 1` (`NavDebugCommands.TraceEnabled`); `BaseRaidSession.LogRaid` defaults false. **Rebake hitch** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — "the lag every second", log had zero root hops left): `BuildNavMeshSync.RebakeMode` is back to **Tiles** (synchronous GenerateTiles from physics, ~20 ms, only the tiles around the change — the engine's small update). Request mode (rebuild across frames) was tried but can build from the stale bake and left walls uncarved ("running against the wall"); with rebakes now rare the hitch is affordable. `EntityLocomotion.FollowAgent`: an agent more than 64 u ahead of a held body pulls back to the body and re-paths. `EntityBrain.OnNavBakeComplete` / `OnStructureBlockerChanged` no longer set an immediate path check for every entity — re-paths are staggered over 0.05–0.55 s (a breacher waiting on its own kill's rebake still rechecks at once via `OnBreachNavRebaked`). **No random re-seat** (Mark, 2026-09-23 — "still shaking", every logged hop `navigating=False`, many with no rebake nearby): `EntityNavMeshUtility.EnsureAgentOnNavMesh` moved the body to the best of 6 **random** points in a ±48 u box (`TryFindNavAtFeet` is not a closest-point query) — 15–35 u every time a path check re-seated an idle agent (`agentReplaced`), a landing, or a settle. Now (Mark: "picking a new spot on the navmesh within 1 m") the small-snap path **never moves the body**: nav anywhere near the feet → `SetAgentPosition( feet )` and the engine seats the agent on the nearest polygon; no nav → false (keep waiting). Only spawn placement (large `maxSnap`) still moves the body. **No snap on rebake** (Mark, 2026-09-22 — scavs "in a new spot every second"): `EntityBrain.OnNavBakeComplete` leaves an entity that is already walking on nav alone (it used to `EnsureAgentOnNavMesh` every entity on every rebake — up to 120 u — and a raid rebakes ~1/s as walls fall), and `TryResolveNavOrigin` only returns the projected start for the path query; it no longer moves the body (the old `ApplyNavXyKeepTerrainZ` glue, up to 96 u, is gone). An agent that truly lost its footing is re-placed lazily by `RunPathCheckTo` (`agentReplaced`). **Visual hop smoothing** (`EntityLocomotion.SmoothVisualHops`, Mark: still "SO choppy"): the engine agent itself re-clamps onto a rebuilt tile when a structure rebake unloads the tiles under it, and UpdatePosition carries the root with it. Any root hop longer than a frame of running (420 u/s · dt + 12 u) and ≤ 320 u keeps gameplay's new root but holds the `Body` model child where it was and eases it in over ~0.2 s — measured and applied in `OnPreRender`, after the agent has written the root (from `OnUpdate` the hop rendered for a frame, then the model snapped back: jump-back-jump). Confirmed source (Mark's 2026-09-23 log): every `[BuildNav] tile regenerate` is followed by 20–33 u hops of the raiders in those tiles, moving or standing; each hop logs `[Loco] … root hopped Nu …` (throttled, with agent / nav state) so the source can be removed. Host only. Raiders ignore noise while raiding and never retreat.

### Procedural box dungeon (dungeonBoxTest scene)

- **`BoxDungeonGenerator`** (`Code/Dungeon/`, on the `Dungeon` object in `Assets/scenes/dungeonBoxTest.scene`): the object's transform *is* the entrance — the entrance room is centred on it and the outside doorway faces its -X, with the two-sided `ENTRANCE` sign (`DungeonSignPanel`, WorldPanel overlay) over the door. Host sets `[Sync] ActiveSeed` (`Seed` property, 0 = random each start); every peer builds identical geometry locally from that seed (`DungeonLayout.Generate` is pure — no engine types), so nothing but the chests is networked. Chests (dead ends) go through `BuildAuthority.HostPlacePiece( "chest" )` on the host and are filled with `ContainerInventory.HostDepositStack` from a small loot table, so they open / network like any hammer-placed chest.
- **Layout** (`DungeonLayout`): a cell grid per floor (`Width × Depth`, pitch **Cell pitch (m)** 16 m). Rooms occupy **footprints** of 1×1, 2×1, 2×2, 3×2 or 3×3 cells (`CellsW × CellsH`, interior = base 9 / 12 m + one pitch per extra cell, so a 2×2 is 28 m across and a 3×3 is 44 m) and are `Rect` or `Round` (1×1 → 6 m radius, 2×2 → 14 m). **No small rooms** (Mark: 6 m pockets were useless) — the smallest is 9 m. A growing-tree walk from the entrance cell (west edge, middle row) steps out of a free perimeter *slot* (cell-side) of an open room; a step lays a straight **corridor run** of 1–**Longest corridor run** cells with **Corridor chance** (a `Corridor` cell is a 2.5 m square with no wall on its open sides; later steps out of any corridor cell give bends and T-junctions), then a room at the end — **Big room chance** tries a multi-cell footprint first and shrinks until one fits, **Round room chance** picks the shape. Every multi-cell room is a **hub**: it immediately grows 3–6 branches so it ends up with many doors (Mark: "6 doors off it"); otherwise room picking is weighted by perimeter, so big rooms branch more. `Winding bias` picks between continuing from the newest room (long paths) and a weighted random open room. Doors are per perimeter cell-side (`DungeonDoor`: side + cell), so a wide wall can hold several. Floors link only through **stair rooms**: after a floor is grown, the deepest eligible single-cell room (≤2 doors, ≥2 free cells beside it upstairs) plus random deeper-half picks become `StairUp` (forced 12 m rect, a door-free wall side becomes `StairSide`, preferring one whose upstairs neighbour is already blocked) and a `Landing` is created directly above (same cell, same side blocked for doors — a door there would open onto the stairwell hole); the landings seed the next floor's growth, so the whole dungeon stays one tree. After each floor, `PruneDeadEnds` removes corridor stubs (one door) and landings that never grew a door (their stair room reverts), then re-indexes. Dead ends (one door, not entrance / stairs / corridor): the deepest always gets a chest (`Treasure`), the rest by **Chest chance per dead end**. Pillars: big rects draw Grid / Colonnade / Square / Ring, 12 m rects Center / Square / Corners / Row / Ring, 9 m rects Center / Square, round rooms Center / Ring; stair rooms and landings only Center or none (the strip along the stair wall must stay clear); corridors and the entrance none.
- **Geometry** (`DungeonGeometry`, all sizes converted once from the component's meter properties): dev boxes (`models/dev/box.vmdl` + `materials/default.vmat` tint, 50 u cube scaled, optionally yawed) with static `BoxCollider`s, one `Floor_N` child per floor (`dungeon_floors <n|all>` hides floors above n locally — upper plates cover lower rooms, there is no roof otherwise). Colours cycle by **room depth** (`ColorDepth`: corridor cells inherit their parent's, so a long hallway stays one colour) through a 10-hue palette (walls ×0.7, pillars ×0.45, halls the average of both ends, treasure floors gold), so walking back cycles the palette backwards. Rect walls are 0.4 m outside the interior (N/S walls cover the corners, E/W stop short — no overlapping volumes), split around every doorway on that side (hallway-width, 2.5 m, lintel from **Doorway height** up; corridor cells get no lintel and only their N/S corner posts remain). Round rooms: the floor is axis-aligned strips inscribed in the outer wall circle (there is no dev cylinder), the wall a ring of ~1 m rotated segments skipping the doorway's angular span, with a chord lintel over each gap. Halls run from wall face to wall face between the two door cells (`FaceDistance`: rect = half extent + wall from the cell centre; round = the ring point at the doorway's outer edge, and hall walls bury their ends one wall thickness into the ring); **hall plates sit 1 u below room plates** so tops never z-fight. Stairs: `StepCount = FloorHeight / StepRise` (4 m / 0.4 m = 10 steps of 0.7 m run) as solid blocks hugging `StairSide` from one corner; the landing plate is the full plate minus that exact footprint (four strips), so the last tread meets the plate flush and there is head-room from step 5 up. Ground plane top sits at z = -2 so floor-0 plates (top at 0) never z-fight it.
- **Map reveal** (Mark, 2026-09-24 — the map page maps rooms as you go, one floor layer at a time, stairs clearly marked): `DungeonMapModel` (built with the geometry from the same layout + params, everything in world meters: room centre / half extents / round flag, per-door centre + orientation, hall interior rects, stair-run / stairwell footprints, the entrance doorway) and `DungeonExploration` (client-local, per session: visited room + hall indices, `CurrentFloor`, `Version`; reset to "entrance only" on every rebuild). The generator samples the local input-owned pawn (fly camera when there is none) every 0.25 s: floor = `floor((z + 0.35·FloorHeight) / FloorHeight)` so the layer flips a third of the way up the stairs; the cell under the viewer marks that room (corridor cells count); halls are marked when stood in or once both end rooms are known. `TerrainWorldMapFace` has a `_dungeonLayer` (z 0, under strokes) rebuilt only when the (map, version, floor) key or the zoom geometry changes: **only the current floor's discovered rooms and halls are drawn** — interior rect (round rooms `border-radius: 50%`) grown by the wall thickness with the wall as its border in the room's cycle colour (treasure gold, halls the blend), doorways as opaque floor-coloured tabs across the wall band (so exits show as gaps), stair runs / stairwell holes as white pads with a constant-size **UP** / **DOWN** badge, **IN** at the entrance doorway. The minimap shares the face, so it shows the same reveal. `MapMenuSection` adds one fixed line under Controls — "Dungeon: floor 2/3 · rooms 5/38" (`BoxDungeonGenerator.Active`, `RoomCount` / `VisitedRoomCount` exclude corridor cells). Nothing is persisted or networked: each player's map is their own, like pins.
- Console: `dungeon_regen [seed]` (host; remotes rebuild when the synced seed changes), `dungeon_floors <n|all>`, `dungeon_info` (per-floor rooms / stairs / chests / deepest depth). Player: `L` (`TerrainTestPlayerSpawn` on the same object) spawns the playable pawn at the fly camera, which starts just outside the entrance.

### Procedural cave (caveVertTest scene)

- **`CaveDungeonGenerator`** (`Code/Dungeon/`, on the `Cave` object in `Assets/scenes/caveVertTest.scene`): the object's transform is the rim of the first drop. Same networking contract as the box dungeon — host sets `[Sync] ActiveSeed` (`Seed`, 0 = random), every peer builds identical geometry locally from `CaveLayout.Generate` (pure, meters, no engine calls — it compiles and runs under plain .NET with a `Vector3` shim, which is how it is verified), and only the chests are networked (`BuildAuthority.HostPlacePiece( "chest" )` + `HostDepositStack` loot). `TerrainTestPlayerSpawn` sits on the same object; the fly cam starts on the platform, `L` spawns the pawn with the grapple.
- **Shape: one long descent, mainly one path, almost no flat ground** (Mark: no central tube everything shoots off from; "mainly just one path, not so many offshoots"; horizontal areas "very rare"). The **trunk** (`Passages[0]`, `CavePassage.Kind == Trunk`) is one swept tube of 45 m **drop** sub-legs that drift, walk their radius (pinches down to **Narrowest radius (m)** 3, caverns that swell to 22–36 m over four sub-legs, never faster than 0.4 m of radius per metre of height so the wall stays within hop reach) and now and then become a **chute** — a steep leaning run (20–36 m sideways per 45 m) that carries the shaft sideways and turns its heading; that is how the cave wanders without ever going flat. Flat floors exist only where they are needed: a 10–16 m **landing** under a **chimney** (climb 40–150 m, leaning 20–40 m away from the drop it follows) and a 10–14 m landing on its apex, a rare **tunnel** (4–10 % per drop by archetype, sometimes a cavern), the one **junction hall** (25–40 m, **Junction halls** 1) where branches fork, and the **chamber** (**Chamber radius (m)** 40) at **Net depth (m)** 3000. Every straight run is checked against the polyline so far (skipping only the run it continues, or the apex + chimney for the drop after a chimney, which also chutes away slim for three sub-legs) so the chain never crosses itself. Five archetypes (`Shaft`, `Stairs`, `Serpent`, `Chimneys`, `Caverns`; **Archetype** −1 = from seed) set drop lengths, drift, chute and pinch chances, bulges, climbs and turn angles. Over 40 seeds: trunk 3.2–4 km, route 4–7.8 km, ~3.5 chimneys and ~2 side rooms per cave, spread 180–740 m.
- **Sweep** (`CaveLayout.Trunk.cs`): straight legs with corners rounded to ≈ 1.4 × tube radius, rings every **Trunk ring spacing (m)** 4 m / **Passage ring spacing (m)** 2.5 m, **parallel-transported frames** (continuous, so rings never twist; anything that needs "up" or "sideways" in a ring asks `UpAngle` / `SideOf` / `AngleAtHeight`), a per-leg radius profile (`R0 → R1` with an `RPeak` bulge), and a flat **floor** in walk legs that also reaches 1.6 radii into the neighbouring drop / chimney, so every shaft bottom is a flat floor; walk legs inherit the previous floor (a hall's far end is lowered to where the next tube keeps the same floor). `TubePoint( passage, ring, φ )` is the one rock-surface function (radius + `TubeNoise` + floor clamp + pond bowls) that the mesh, the mouths and the ledges all read.
- **Mouths** (`CaveMouth`, always on the trunk): hole patches in the trunk's ring × segment grid. `MakeMouthVertical` cuts a drop wall around a world direction (the whole hole sits under the previous ledge; the floor is the bottom edge's highest vertex); `MakeMouthSide` cuts a landing / hall side wall from one segment under the floor up to two throats above it (through `AngleAtHeight`, so it works whatever the frame roll). Mouths never overlap each other.
- **Branches** (`CaveLayout.Passages.cs`), deliberately few: **Dead-end rooms off the drops** 2 hang off route hops (doorstep under the mouth, validated as a real hop: lower, within grapple reach); the junction hall gets **Decoy rooms per junction** 1–2 dead-end mouths on its side walls and, if **Loops** > 0, a loop that rejoins the trunk through an exit mouth on a later walk leg (rare in practice, since walk legs are short). A dead end is walk out 15–30 m → 35 % a chimney up 15–40 m / 20 % a drop 10–30 m / else straight → a room of radius 10–25 m and length 2.5–4 × radius, domed shut; chest at the far end, a light, and with **Pond chance in a big room** an oasis pond when the radius ≥ 12 m. `PassageClear` rejects a tube that breaks the platform or touches any other passage; `DebugLog` keeps rejection reasons.
- **Route** (`CaveRoutePoint` list, `CaveLayout.Route.cs`): rim → down each drop group (`DescendTube` on a `LegTube` over the group's near-vertical samples, starting at the wall nearest the previous route point — from a floor the route first walks to that edge) → landing / junction (branches built here) → up each chimney (`AscendTube`, grapple hops rise 5–10 m) → … → chamber. Steppers solve the tangential step against the real horizontal distance, promote jump hops to grapple hops when the wall flares or drifts, allow the tube's own **lean** between two heights as free sideways distance, swing across pinches only where the tube is vertical, and instead of endless retries go straight down the same wall once the start would exceed the grapple drop limit. Ledge kinds (`Slab`, `Jut` tilted and rolled, `Shelf`, `Spur`, `Boulder` rolled ±15°, `Doorstep`) by weight, protrusion ≤ 45 % of the local radius. Fillers (**Filler ledges** fraction) go on the drops. `RoutePathMeters` / `RouteClimbMeters` / `RouteDescentMeters` are measured after generation.
- **Chamber + oases**: the chamber is the final walk leg; its pond is a `CaveBowl` against one wall with the waterfall crack above it (`AngleAtHeight` on the wall, 16–24 m up), the chest on the bank. Every bowl (chamber, junction hall with **Pond chance in a junction hall**, big rooms) gets `BuildOasis`: the water rig (`water`-tagged trigger + `WaterVolume` + translucent body + `tempwater.vmat` surface), slate paths stepping into the water, boulders, moss, reeds, a teal light. The waterfall stays a placeholder until there is a particle / shader waterfall.
- **Geometry** (`CaveGeometry` + `.Dressing`): one runtime `ModelBuilder` model — every passage as rings of **Trunk segments around** 48 / **Passage segments around** 28 vertices from `TubePoint`, the trunk first with its mouth holes skipped, each branch's entry ring **zipped** onto its hole loop (both walked by polar angle in the mouth frame) and its last ring zipped onto the exit mouth or domed shut, the trunk's first ring fanned out to the square **entrance platform** and its last ring domed (the chamber's far wall); `AddCollisionMesh` + `AddTraceMesh` → `ModelRenderer` + static `ModelCollider`, tags `solid` / `dungeon`, **no `grapple` tag**. Ledges are dev boxes grown from `AnchorMeters` along `IntoVoid`, buried 3 m + noise into the rock, with `BoxCollider`s and the **`grapple` tag** — the only hookable surfaces. Debug tint: jump hops gold, grapple hops blue, climb hops green, fillers grey, doorsteps pale.
- **Verification**: the session scratchpad `Harness` (net7 console app compiling the four `CaveLayout*.cs` files against a `Vector3` shim) generates N seeds, checks every hop against the limits (lean-aware on the trunk), tube-vs-tube clearance (the trunk against itself too), ledges-in-mouths and frame orthogonality, and renders X-Z / Y-Z side views as PNGs at 1 px = 2 m. Rebuild it whenever the layout changes, and look at the renders — the numbers alone did not catch the "central tube" problem.
- Console: `cave_regen [seed]` (host), `cave_info` (trunk legs with their branches, passages, every route point with gap / dz / kind), `cave_tp <routeIndex|top|bottom>`, `cave_tp room <n>`, `cave_tp junction <n>` (local pawn).

### Animal AI (shared state machine)

- **`AnimalBrain`** (`Code/Entity/Animals/`) is the single state machine every animal species runs: `Idle / Wander / Graze / Alerted / Tracking / Attacking / Fleeing` inside one master `TickStateMachine()`. Species never get their own brain classes.
- Per-species tuning lives in **`Assets/data/animal_behaviors.json`** (`AnimalBehaviorCatalog`, meters/seconds, converted once at load). The `threatResponse` field picks the transition set: **flee** (prey: any sight/sound → run from last-sensed position, calm when far), **harass** (fox 1 bite / coyote 2 bites: track → lunge → bite → flee → resume tracking when the player backs off), **predator** (lynx/wolf/bear: track → attack until target dies; flee at `fleeHealthFraction`, turn and fight if pressed inside `reengageRangeMeters`; `0` = proximity never turns it). Two opt-in flags (badger, 2026-11-08): **`chargeOnAlert`** — when the alert pause ends it goes straight to Attacking, never Tracking; **`lastStand`** — a hit taken while running from low health turns it round for good (`AnimalBrain._lastStand`: the low-health flee gate is off for the rest of its life, it fights to the death).
- **Badger** (Mark 2026-11-08): `prefabs/entity/badger.prefab`, species `Badger`, model `models/entities/badger.vmdl` from `Blender/scripts/create_badger.py` (fox-sized: 1.15 m nose to tail, low and wide; the fur is geometry - a skirt of guard-hair tufts over the legs, ragged back, cheek ruffs - plus hair-stroke paint; the texture size is picked so the coat gets exactly 256 texels per metre, 512 px; clips idle / walk / run / graze / bite authored at the json 1.1 / 6.0 m/s). Sight 25 m in a 160° cone ahead, hearing 22 m, 0.5 s alert then it charges; flees at 25 % health with no proximity re-engage; hit again while fleeing → last stand. Clover Hills row 245 m spacing / weight 0.75 (fox 226 / 0.8). Loot 1-2 **Badger Claws** (`resource_badgerClaws`, model `Tools/item_models` `badger_claws`, icon `ui/items/resource_badgerClaws.png`).
- Threats are plain **GameObjects** (not player-typed) — other entities can scare animals via `AnimalBrain.NotifyThreat`. Hearing rides the existing `EntityNoiseBus`; sight is an interval FOV+LOS scan via `EntitySight`.
- Animal prefabs carry `EntityVitals` + `DamageReceiver` (player weapons already damage them), `NavMeshAgent`, `EntityLocomotion`, `AnimalBrain`. Spawn via `AnimalSetup.Configure( …, prefabPath )` (`AnimalSpawnButton` for tests, input action `SpawnAnimal`); the prefab path is what lets `EntityChunkMemory` clone the animal back.
- **Animals leave the world past 100 m (`AnimalBrain.Dormancy.cs`, 2026-11-04)** — the animal range pair (`EntityChunkMemory.AnimalDespawnRangeMeters` 100 / `AnimalReturnRangeMeters` 90, see *Entity AI* above), 2 Hz staggered. Calm (Idle / Wander / Graze, no threat), untamed, not trapped → `EntityChunkMemory.Remember( AnimalBrain )`: species, prefab, max + current health, position, yaw, home, calm state, population slot; restored through `AnimalSetup.Configure` + `SetHomePosition` + `ResumeRememberedState` + `RestoreHealth`, so a deer comes back as the same deer. Anything else sleeps (locomotion, nav agent, skinned bodies off — waking restores exactly what sleep switched off): fleeing / tracking / attacking / alerted / approaching a food offer, trapped, tamed. A sleeper that is not tamed or trapped drops its threat after `TrackGiveUpSeconds` (15 s) asleep and leaves the world calm, so nothing piles up out there. **Tamed animals never leave the world.** A bird flying away or a body in the air is never put to sleep. Population slots carry the species (`BiomePopulationSlot.Species` → `BiomePopulationRespawnQueue`): before that, a bird that flew away respawned through `EntityEnemySetup` ("no EntityBrain — this spawn is skipped") and stayed in the world as a husk with its locomotion running.

**Animation, deer, birds (2026-11-02, prompts 19-21).** `AnimalBrain.Animation.cs`: the host publishes `[Sync] AnimState` (the `AnimalAiState` id, or `AnimStateFlyAway` = 10) and `BiteSerial`; every peer drives the body's `SkinnedModelRenderer` by sequence name (`UseAnimGraph = false`): `idle` / `walk` / `run` / `sneak` / `graze` / `alert` (deer) / `bite` / `takeoff` / `fly`, with `PlaybackRate` = measured planar speed ÷ the json walk / run / sneak m/s the clips were authored at. Models: `models/entities/fox_v2.vmdl` (rigged, clips in `models/entities/animations/fox_v2@*.fbx`, 256 px red-fox texture from `Blender/scripts/create_fox_texture.py`, which re-runs `create_fox_anims.py`), `deer_v2.vmdl` (whitetail prefab; torso ~33 % wider from behind, Body child at scale 1 - the dev-box 1.6 / 0.5 / 1.1 placeholder scale drew it at half width - with a 43 × 23 × 21 u box collider), `bluejay.vmdl` (`prefabs/entity/bluejay.prefab`, species Jay) - all from `Blender/scripts/create_deer.py`, `create_fox_anims.py`, `create_bluejay.py`. Deer: `sightRangeMeters` 60 / `hearRangeMeters` 50 (far more skittish than the fox) and a panic zigzag - `fleeZigzagLegMeters` 5 / `fleeZigzagTurnDegrees` 45: every 5 m leg turns up to ±45° from the last heading, bent back to 50° off straight-away if it would swing past 70° (`TryPickFleePoint`). Jay: `threatResponse` `flyaway` - any stimulus → `BeginFlyAway` (nav agent and locomotion off, rigidbody motion off), the brain climbs and carries it away from the threat for `flyAwaySeconds` (7), then the bird is destroyed and its `BiomePopulationSlot` queues a respawn; it is killable only while perched. Undisturbed it leaves on its own after a random perch of `perchMinSeconds`..`perchMaxSeconds` (40-150 s, `TickPerchTimer`, random heading). The jay has only `sit` / `takeoff` / `fly`, so every ground state (and death) plays `sit` - an unknown sequence name leaves the bind pose up with the wings spread. The prefab draws it 2.6x (Body child scale, box collider and health-bar anchor scaled to match; Mark 2026-11-02: 1.3x, then "2x its size currently, still too small"). `biome_population.json` rows with `species` spawn through `AnimalSetup` (fox 226 m / deer 184 m / jay 110 m spacing in Clover Hills - fox and deer doubled 2026-11-02). **Loot** (Mark 2026-11-02): each species row's `loot` list (`resourceId`, `min`, `max`) drops as world pickups round the body when it dies (`AnimalBrain.HostDropLoot`, host, once): jay 1 feathers + 1-2 wings (`rawmeat_bird`), deer 1 hide + 2-3 whitetail meat, fox 2 fox pelts + 2 fox meat. Herd rows (`groupChance` / `groupMin` / `groupMax` / `groupRadiusMeters`, `BiomePopulationScatter.ScatterEntry`): deer cells hold 2-4 within 9 m three times in four (Mark: "75% chance for there to be 2 near each other"); member 0 keeps the single-spawn slot key, the others are `…:m<i>` slots of their own, so each sleeps, restores and respawns through the usual population slot.

### Farming (hoe → tilled soil → seeds → growth stages)

- **Dirt**: tilling needs a flat surface tagged `dirt` (`FarmingRules.DirtTag`, testscene1's ground `Plane`) **or the world's ground** (tag `terrain`: heightfield chunks and voxel chunks, `ToolPickaxe.IsGround`) since the hoe menu (2026-11-02). Anything else = not ground.
- **Hoe menu** (2026-11-02, Mark: "Hoes should open up a menu like the build menu and one of them is for creating grass for that biome, bare ground for that biome, tilled soil, or the grading tool which flattens the surface"): **Attack2** opens `HoeMenuHud` (`Code/UI/Build/HoeMenuHud.cs`, the build menu's overlay + tiles) with six `HoeMode`s - **Grass**, **Bare ground**, **Tilled soil**, **Grade**, **Raise ground**, **Cobblestone**; a click picks and closes, right-click / Escape / click beside closes; `hoe_mode <till|grass|bare|grade>` and `hoe_menu` do it from the console. Grass / Bare paint a `Paint Radius (m)` (1.5) circle of the voxel ground (`VoxelBrushMode.PaintGrass` / `PaintBare`, hold LMB to keep painting; **F** or the menu checkbox swaps it for a square patch of side 2 r snapped to the 0.5 m voxel grid; the paint is a per-column coverage the mesher blends, so circles read round); Grade is the voxel rake (`Grade Radius (m)` 2.5, LMB lays the planned plane, **F** swaps flat / slope, the ring colours from the digger). Tilled soil on the world ground is a third paint (dark wet soil, no clutter, seeds sow on it through `FarmingRules.IsTilledGroundAt`); Raise ground adds one `Raise Layer (m)` 0.25 of dirt inside the patch per press and costs 1 `resource_dirt` (the pickaxe chip yields 1) - free while `PlayerTerraform.FreeMaterials` (prefab flag, on) or the `allCrafting` hack is on, the same switch as free crafting; Cobblestone paves the patch with the rock tile for 1 `resource_stone` per press under the same free switch. Every stroke costs stamina (`PlayerVitals`: Pickaxe Swing 4, Hoe Paint 1, Grade 3, Raise 3) and the shove (F) now needs someone to punch ahead and never fires with the hoe out. All of them go `ToolHoe` -> `PlayerTerraform.OwnerRequestBrush` -> host reach + tool check -> `IVoxelBrushTarget.RequestBrush` -> one durability tick when the stroke landed. Detail in `Code/World/Voxel/docs/VOXEL.md` (*Tools*).
- **Hoe, Tilled soil mode**: equipment profile `tool_hoe` (`actions: ["Till"]`, `toolPrefab: prefabs/tools/hoe_tool.prefab`, `durabilityMax` 100) + a hand recipe. `ToolHoe` (bound by `PlayerEquipment.SpawnTool`, same as the hammer) shows a translucent one-tile square snapped to the **1 m** world grid (`FarmingRules.TileSizeMeters`, `TilledSoil.SnapToGrid`) — light brown when tillable, red when not (not dirt, too steep, already tilled). Attack1 → `PlayerFarming.OwnerRequestTill` → host `FarmingAuthority.TryTill` re-traces the ground, spawns `prefabs/farming/tilled_soil.prefab` (`TilledSoil` + static box collider + light-brown visual) and charges **one durability tick only when a tile actually appears**.
- **Seeds**: rows in `resources.json` with a `"seed": { plantName, matureSeconds, color, matureHeightMeters, stageModels[] }` block (`FarmPlantData`; `ResourceDefinitionCatalog.IsSeed / TryGetSeed`). Seeds are plain stackable resources, not equipment — "equipped" means selected on the hotbar. `PlayerFarming` (on `basicplayer.prefab`) then draws a translucent matured-plant ghost at the aim point: plant-coloured where a seed can go, red anywhere else; prompt reads **"Sow &lt;plant&gt;"** and **E or Attack1** sows exactly at the aim point. With seeds in the bag but none selected, aiming at tilled soil prompts "Equip seeds to sow".
- **Sow rule** (`FarmingRules.CanSowAt`, shared by ghost and host): the point must be inside a `TilledSoil` tile and ≥ **0.3 m** (`MinPlantSpacingMeters`) from every other `FarmPlant`. Host `FarmingAuthority.TrySow` re-checks reach + rule, consumes one seed (hotbar first, then bag) and spawns `prefabs/farming/farm_plant.prefab`.
- **Growth**: `FarmPlant` ticks on the host only; `[Sync] StageIndex` walks FreshlySown → Seedling → Juvenile → Matured at 1/3, 2/3, 3/3 of `matureSeconds` (all four starter plants: 10 s). Every peer rebuilds the visual from the synced stage (`FarmPlant.BuildStageVisual`) — tinted dev shapes until a row names `stageModels`.
- **Two plant kinds** (`seed.type`): **`single`** (radish, daisy) — one harvest gives `harvestResourceId` × `harvestAmountLow..High` (1) and removes the plant; left matured for `boltAfterSeconds` (30 s) it enters the fifth stage **Bolting** (tall straw-yellow placeholder), where harvesting gives `boltSeedAmount` (2) seeds instead of crop. **`bush`** (blueberry, raspberry) — harvest gives 1–3 fruit, the bush stays, growth winds back so it is matured again after `regrowSeconds` (10 s); bushes never bolt. Harvest is E on the plant (`PlayerFarming` prompt "Harvest Radish (1 Radish)" / "Collect Radish Seeds"); host `FarmingAuthority.TryHarvest` requires bag room for the worst-case yield first (same rule as hand harvest — never lose items).
- **Uproot**: hoe aimed at any plant → prompt "Uproot … (+1 seed)" on **LMB**; `FarmingAuthority.TryUproot` destroys the plant, returns `FarmingRules.UprootSeedAmount` (1) seed, one hoe durability tick. This is how a bush is "destroyed" for its seed.
- Crop items: `food_radish` (edible, blueberry-like stats placeholder), `resource_daisy` (plain resource) — icons `ui/items/food_radish.png` / `resource_daisy.png` still to be drawn.
- Hand harvest yields E while farming owns it (`PlayerFarming.OwnsUseKey`: valid sow ghost or ready plant under the aim), mirroring the hammer's `IsPreviewingPlacePiece` gate. HUD prompt priority: container / station / bench → trial → arena → campfire → **farming** (key cap from `PromptKey`) → harvest.
- Scene kits: `ChestKitFill` (replaced `StarterKitChestFill`) deposits an authored `Items` list into an empty chest once on the host. testscene1 has Starter Kit, Farming Kit (hoe + 4 seeds), Archery (bow + arrows), Fish, Meats.

### Quest tracking (per player, client-local, write-through)

- Definitions: `Assets/data/quests.json` → `QuestCatalog`. Each quest has `requires` (prerequisite quest ids; empty = available from the start) and `objectives` (`event` + optional `match` + `count`). File order is display order. Event ids live in `Code/Quests/QuestEventIds.cs`.
- Main quests form **one chain**: each entry `requires` the previous main entry, and a locked quest shows `??????????????` for its name with description/task hidden. New main quests go at the end of the file with `requires` = the previous main id. Repeat-kill tallies ("kill 5 fox") are **side quests** (`"side": true`, no `requires`): always active, full details visible, listed under a "Side Quests" header. Boss kills are main. `"disabled": true` parks a quest whose content does not exist yet (Awakening / stasis pod today): the catalog drops it and strips it from every `requires`, so the chain heals around it — re-enable by deleting the flag when Mark says so.
- State: `QuestTracker` (static, client-local) — Locked / Active / Completed + per-objective counts. Progress belongs to the **player** (Steam id), not the world, and is saved under `FileSystem.Data/2Tgames/players/<steamid>/quests.json` on **every** change (tiny file, no shutdown hook to trust). `quests_list`, `quests_reset`, `quests_report <event> [match] [amount]` console commands exist for testing.
- Bridge: `PlayerQuests` on `basicplayer.prefab`. Host-validated actions (craft, kill, build, grapple, augment commit) call `HostReport` on the acting pawn, which forwards to the owning client via `[Rpc.Owner]`. Owner-local actions (wingsuit, pickups via `PlayerInventory.ResourcePickedUp`, biome poll) call `OwnerReport`. Never track quests on the host for other players — the host only reports.
- Adding a new trigger = pick/declare an event id, call `HostReport`/`OwnerReport` at the one place the action is committed, reference it from JSON. Reserved-but-unemitted today: `pod_exited`, `map_marker_placed`, `slept_in_bed`.

### Map page (pins, pen strokes, pings — per player, client-local, write-through)

- **Layout** (`Code/UI/Menu/MapMenuSection.cs`): crew column | wide rectangular map window (takes all remaining width) | eye / + / − strip hanging off the map's top-right edge | 300 px tool column (Pins grid, Markup pen + eraser, Controls text; 20 px headers, 17 px body — Mark found 13 px unreadable). The map is a *window* into the biome texture, never stretched: `TerrainWorldMapFace` sizes its zoom stage as a square of the longer face edge × zoom in pixels, so a wide window crops the map. `TerrainMinimapZoom.Max` is 40 (≈100 m across). Nothing may sit over the map itself — controls live beside it.
- **Data** (`Code/Map/`): `LocalMapMarkup` (static, client-local) holds the pins + strokes for the current (player, world) and saves the whole file on every committed change under `FileSystem.Data/2Tgames/players/<steamid>/map/<WorldName>_<seed>.json` (`scene_<name>` in hand-built test scenes). Positions are **world meters from center**, so they survive map re-renders and work on the local radar. Pin icons are the fixed `MapPinCatalog` list (`Assets/ui/map/pin_*.png`, ids stored in JSON). `map_markup_clear` wipes the current file. Tool / selected icon are session state, the eye toggle is saved.
- **Rendering** (`Code/UI/TerrainWorldMapFace.cs`): a world-anchored layer inside the zoom stage — pins are px-sized panels at map UV (constant screen size at every zoom), strokes are rotated segment panels (thickness stored in meters at draw time, so the on-screen width follows the zoom), pings are pulsing rings. The layer rebuilds only when `LocalMapMarkup.Version`, the zoom stage size, or the eye toggle changes; in radar mode the whole layer slides with the camera. The HUD minimap shares the face and shows pins/strokes without name labels.
- **Input** (soft-cursor hit rects, see the crew panel rule): LMB double-click on the map places the selected pin and opens a `TextEntry` for its name (Enter / click elsewhere commits, Escape closes the entry — not the menu, via `PlayerGameMenuController.EscapeConsumer`; hotkeys stand down while `IsTextCaptureActive`). LMB on a pin toggles crossed-off, RMB removes it, MMB pings the spot to the crew (`PlayerCrew.OwnerSendMapPing` → `[Rpc.Broadcast]` filtered to crew mates through `MapPingFeed`), wheel over the map zooms (`TerrainMinimapZoom`), pen / eraser draw while **RMB** is held (`InventoryMenuInputOverlay.BindPageDrag`), and **LMB click-and-drag pans the view with every tool** (Mark: click-and-drag is always left mouse; `TerrainWorldMapFace.PanByScreenPixels`, a pan offset on top of the player-centred focus, clamped to the map and reset when the page closes). Placing a pin reports `QuestEventIds.MapMarkerPlaced` with the icon id as match.
- **Coop sharing** (`Code/Map/CrewMapShare.cs`, `Code/Crew/PlayerCrew.MapShare.cs`): the owner mirrors its "Show location to crew" toggle (saved in the map file, default on; radio with a red X through it when off) and its pins (`CrewMapShare.EncodePins`, `icon|name|x|y|c` lines) onto two `[Sync]` properties on `PlayerCrew` every 0.5 s when changed. Sharing is **crew-scoped**: the face draws a coloured dot per crew mate with `ShareLocation` on (position comes from the pawn transform), and under "Crew Pins" one radio per crew mate opts that player's pins onto your map (session choice, faded, labelled `name · owner`, never hit-testable). A ping (`MapPingFeed.Add`) also drops a `MapPingBillboard` sign ("Ping: <name>") on the ground at the spot — a local `WorldPanel` facing the camera, overlay-rendered, gone after `MapPingFeed.LifetimeSeconds` (10 s). Pings are session-only: timed on the wall clock (`MapPingFeed.Now`, not `Time.Now`, which restarts per play session) and pruned when the scene changes — never written to the map file.

### Stasis pod (the spawn pod, Mark 2026-11-08 / 2026-11-09)

- **Assets** from `Blender/scripts/create_stasis_pod.py` (run it, never hand-edit the outputs): `models/props/stasis_pod/` holds the pod (`stasis_pod.vmdl`: plinth, hollow C-shell, roof cap, quilted interior, dangling body tubes, side lamp panels, rear conduit; the concave `PhysicsMeshFile` carries the shell AND every interior solid - pads, headrest, seat, pedestal, armrests - so the pawn cannot push into any of it; the profile is sized so the pawn's 0.4 m capsule at the occupant anchor x +0.15 touches nothing), the door leaf (`stasis_pod_door.vmdl`, origin on the hinge line at the +Y jamb, window hole + a `shaders/glass.shader` pane), one lamp dome, one readout screen plate, one faceted puff blob, and the materials: brushed-steel shell tile (pixel_lit roughness 0.3), diamond-quilt pad tile, a trim atlas, glass, five lamp colours, screen on / off, puff white / dust. Blender metres, `import_scale 0.4`; modelled +X = the door = engine +X. **s&box binds an FBX material to the vmat named like the texture the FBX references** (not the remap), so the Blender lamp material carries the off png. Previews in `Blender/blenderprojects/stasis_pod_preview/`.
- **Prefab** `prefabs/props/stasis_pod.prefab` (written by the script so every child sits on its mesh socket): root `ModelRenderer` + static `ModelCollider` + `Survival.StasisPod`; children `Door` (renderer + keyframed collider at the hinge, with the `SerialPlate` TextRenderer above the window), `Occupant` (the anchor the riding pawn is parented to, x +0.15 m, on the floor), `Scooter` (the folded pod scooter docked under the seat perch between two clamp posts: `pod_scooter_folded.vmdl` yawed 90 deg + box collider, disabled once taken - see *Pod scooter* under Vehicles), `Lamp_L1..4` / `Lamp_R1..4`, `Screen_L` / `Screen_R`, `SerialPlateInside`, `InteriorLight` (cool point light, no shadows).
- **Arrival sequence** (`Code/Props/StasisPod.cs`): the host sets `Arrival`, `LaunchStart` / `LandPos` / `LandYaw` / `LaunchTime` (`[Sync]`) and every peer evaluates the same curve: a quadratic arc from 160 m up and 110 m inboard of the landing point (so the pod flies **outward and down** from the spawn centre), feet first with a slow roll, blending upright for the last 18 %; impact at `LaunchTime + FlightSeconds` (6.5 s) thumps 14 cm into the ground and back with a 12-puff dust ring, `explosion_small` + `heavy_metal_move1`; **Unlatch** at +1.3 s pops the door 4 deg (kachunk, `impact-melee-metal`); **Vent** +1.6..2.6 s blows white puffs out of the free edge and top seam (pssshhh - no hiss sound asset exists yet); **Opening** eases the leaf to 115 deg over 1.8 s; **Open** releases the occupant. Power-down (lamps, screens, interior light off) when the occupant is 2.2 m from the pod (then `PlayerQuests.HostReport( pod_exited )`), or 12 s after opening for an empty pod. A standing pod (`pod_spawn`) skips all of it; `pod_door` swings its door. Puffs are `StasisPodPuff` objects (local, `NotSaved`).
- **Occupant lock** (`Code/Player/PlayerMovement.StasisPod.cs`, same shape as the vehicle seat): host `HostSetPodLocked` / `HostReleasePod`, owner parents its root under the pod's `Occupant` anchor, keyframes the body, disables colliders + input; `PodLocked` gates locomotion, wingsuit, melee, bow and bola. **The owner rides in first person** (Mark 2026-11-10: "put us in first person mode so we can see out the pod but when it lands you can swap to third person"; this replaced the 2026-11-09 black-until-open view): entering the pod sets `PlayerController.ThirdPerson` false and the eye yaw to the anchor's landed facing, `TickPodLock` holds first person while locked (the view toggle works again once released), and `PlacePodCamera` (from `PostCameraSetup`) glues the camera to the anchor at eye height, turning with the tumbling pod, mouse look relative to it - landed, it equals the plain first-person view, so the release does not jump. The release puts the camera back to the mode the pawn had before the ride (third person on basicplayer; Mark 2026-11-11). The view starts black (`PodBlackout01`, a full-screen panel in `PlayerScreenHud`; a fresh pawn is dark for 2 s while the host's lock arrives) and fades in over 3 s once the pawn is in the pod. **Map discovery is locked** while riding (`MapDiscoveryLocked`): the minimap hides and the Map page will not open (`PlayerGameMenuController.IsPageLocked`).
- **Spawner** (`Code/Props/StasisPodSpawner.cs`, authored in terrainTest only - Mark 2026-11-09: not in testscene1): host scans pawns twice a second; each player's first pawn of the session (per connection; the un-networked L pawn counts once per session) gets a pod landing within **Landing radius (m)** 25 of the centre (`CentreOverride`, else the scene's `SpawnPoint`, else the origin; ground from `TerrainWorldManager.TrySampleGroundMeters` or a trace), at least **Min pod spacing (m)** 4 from other landings. `pod_arrivals 0` (`GameHacks.PodArrivals`) turns arrivals off; `pod_test [N] [here]` crash-lands N empty pods, launches staggered over 2.5 s.
- **Serials** (`Code/Props/StasisPodSerials.cs`): one counter per world in `WorldSaves/<world>/pod_serials.json` (`FileSystem.Data`, beside world.json; world name from the terrain manager, else the network session, else DefaultWorld), first pod **885922**, +1 per pod, host-taken and `[Sync]`ed; drawn as `POD 885922` on the door plate and inside above the door. The player's own serial is not yet recorded on the player (follow-up for "find pod #1").
- The `awakening` quest (`pod_exited`) stays `disabled` in quests.json until Mark re-enables it.

### Playtest logger (Mark 2026-11-09: "analysis from the gameplay, not statements")

`Code/Playtest/PlaytestLog.cs` — host-only behaviour telemetry for the friends playtests. `playtest_log 1` on the
host opens a new `FileSystem.Data/2Tgames/playtest/<host steam name>_<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>_v<build>.jsonl` (one JSON object per line, `t` = seconds
since start), `playtest_log 0` closes it, `playtest_mark <text>` drops a marker ("loop phase over", "toys unlocked").
`Tools/playtest_report.py <file|folder>` prints the tables: per-player timeline (first gain / craft / build / kill /
death, deaths + causes, damage by source), weapon vs entity (hits, heavy share, kills, median time-to-kill, hits per
kill, the killer's HP% / ST% at the kill, damage taken per kill), entities vs players, blocks, stamina (empties per
10 min, how long empty, what the player was doing - the `flags` field: grapple / wingsuit / sprint / air / block /
swing / heavy), health, grapple (attaches per 10 min, re-fires under 1 s = spam, swings >= 4 s = the intended long
swing, median / best speed, distance, rise, rope length, rejects), crafts / builds / pickups., sessions (join / leave, overtime past a
`playtest_mark "stop"` marker, leaves within 2 min of a death), movement (roam radius, path, distance to the nearest
player - the `vitals` sample carries `x` / `y` / `z` in meters), client performance (`client_perf`: every peer, the host's
screen included, reports average and 1 % low fps, menu seconds and resolution every 30 s through the static
`PlaytestLog.RpcClientPerf`; the host switches the client accumulators with `RpcSetClientReporting`, repeated every
30 s for late joiners; menu time = `InventoryScreenPointer.SoftCursorActive` or the pawn's game menu / build hammer /
hoe menu open).

Hooks - one static call each, all behind `PlaytestLog.Enabled`, on paths that already run once per real event:
`VitalsAuthority.TryApplyDeltas` (player damage with its source, stamina-empty edges, death), `EntityVitals.ApplyDamage`
(`hit` + `kill`, fights tracked per victim so TTK runs from the killer's first hit), `PlayerCombat.ServerApplyBlockOutcome`
(`block`), `PlayerMovement` `ServerTryAttach` / `ServerDetach` (`grapple_attach` / `grapple_detach` / `grapple_reject`),
`PlayerCrafting.HostTryCraft` (`craft`), `BuildAuthority.TryPlacePiece` (`build`), `PlayerInventory.HostTryAddResource`
(`gain`, `src` craft / pickup). The weapon is the attacker's `PlayerEquipment.NetworkedMainHandResourceId` ("fists"
when empty); the `kind` is the swing in flight (`melee` / `heavy` via `PlayerCombat.TryGetServerSwingInfo`, with the
attack `swing` L / R / Forward / Stab) unless the source wrapped its `TakeDamage` in `PlaytestLog.Kind(...)` -
`arrow` (ArrowProjectile), `punch` (shove), `burn`, `deadshot`, `laser`, `bite` (AnimalBrain). Per-frame work is only
the grapple travel accumulator over pawns whose rope is attached, plus a 2 s `vitals` sample per pawn and a 3 s flush
(`PlaytestLogSystem`, a GameObjectSystem - no scene or prefab component needed). Entity labels: animal species, else
`EntityVitals` display name + tier; players by connection display name (`player_join` carries the Steam id).

### The hub (Mark 2026-11-10: the game's grand exchange)

A community space reached through a purple portal: time trials, the coliseum, a bar that watches the fights and
takes bets, and a games wing. **For now it lives inside the host's own world**, 3 km above the map at hub-local origin
`(0, 0, 3000 m)`, so the world keeps running and guests can follow or stay; later the same prefab moves onto company
servers (only `HubStore` and the spawn path change). Every hub record is keyed by Steam ID.

- **Assets** from `Tools/hub/generate_hub.py` (run it, never hand-edit the outputs): `prefabs/hub/hub.prefab` (~960
  dev boxes + build-kit wall models, every one a static `BoxCollider`), the buildable `prefabs/build/hub_portal.prefab`
  (bbox-centred like every build piece), `materials/hub/*` and `models/hub/barrel/`. Authored in metres, converted once on
  write. Surfaces use `shaders/hub_surface.shader` (box UVs stretch with scale, so it projects the texture on the world
  plane each face looks along, point sampled up close and faded over one mip level to an anisotropic mip-mapped
  sample once a texel is smaller than a screen pixel — the far walls / roof / grazing floor were breaking the thin neon
  lines into moire — plus `TextureGlow` emission); `hub_glass.shader` is the alpha-blended
  window (the engine glass needs a framebuffer copy offscreen captures lack); `hub_screen.shader` shows the feed.
  Only the hall is black + neon (10 m tiles, no lamps — lamps washed the panels grey); the coliseum is pale stone / dark
  metal / warm light strips (futuristic Roman), the trial cube is plain grey grid (Mark: "doesn't really matter for now").
  Building floors sit 5 cm above the hall floor (`FLOOR_LIFT`; coplanar floors z-fought).
- **Layout** (Mark 2026-11-10, second pass: "MASSIVE wide open space but fully enclosed"; metres, fountain at the
  origin, +Y north; `HALL` / `COL_C` / `CUBE` in the generator): one enclosed 1000 x 1000 m tron hall
  (`x -620..380, y -420..580`, roof at 140 m) that every building stands inside, free to walk round. Arrivals land 10 m
  south of the fountain. Coliseum: 150 x 100 m sand floor centred 270 m north, 5 m podium, six tiers of stands and a
  28 m three-storey arcade facade on three sides, one fort per side at `x ±50` (6 x 6 m tower six kit walls = 12 m with
  eight switchback ramps inside, battlement walls with a 4.5 m walkway and back ramps), team spawns at `x ±65`, three
  barrel piles, six high floodlights. Lobby against its south side (120 x 20 m, 12 m tall): hall with the fish-tank window
  and the arena kiosk, bar wing west (16 x 9 m screen, bookie, benches, commend button), games wing east (stratagem
  cabinet). Trial cube 500 x 500 m (`x -600..-100, y -400..100`, 100 m walls, open top): start platform by the east wall,
  a straight line of grapple bars (every 3 m, 30 m up, on hangers from a top beam) ~470 m west to the end platform,
  rings 10-15 spread along it; the queue building is on the cube's east face, door toward the fountain.
- **Sun indoors**: the sun's shadow cascades do not take the 1000 m roof, so it lit a hard disc of floor round the
  camera. `HubWorld.OnPreRender` zeroes the sun / moon direct light and shadows for any frame whose camera is inside the
  hub (the day/night cycle sets them again every update; ambient fill stays). Nothing changes for players in the world.
- **Portals** (`Code/Hub/HubPortal.cs`): `ToHub` (the build piece `hub_portal`, free, furniture tab — spawns the hub on
  first use via `HubWorld.HostEnsureSpawned`, remembers a return 2 m in front of the portal), `ToWorld` (the fountain —
  back to that return; else bed / SpawnPoint; else the ground straight below the hub), `Teleport` (the trial cube's exit
  pads → the trial building). The local pawn checks the short static portal list 10x a second
  (`PlayerInventoryInteraction.Hub.cs`) and sends `RpcHostUsePortal`; the host re-checks the distance once.
  `HubWorld` (prefab root) also puts anyone more than 20 m under the hub floor (within 700 m of the fountain) back at it
  (1 Hz host check).
- **Kiosks** (`HubKiosk`, look + E, static list, no scene scans): `Bets` on the bookie (`HubBetBoard`), `Stratagem`
  on the cabinet (`StratagemMachine`) — both open `HubMenuPanel` — and `Commend` by the big screen (acts, no menu) (fixed 760 x 560 card, rebuilt only on data change).
  While it is open the hub menu owns E, the cursor and WASD (`UseInputControls` off) and the other interaction ticks
  stand down.
- **Arena betting window**: `ArenaSession.BettingWindowSeconds` (30 in the hub, 0 = old behaviour elsewhere) — a filled
  match goes to `ArenaPhase.Announced`, nobody moves until the window ends; a team emptied meanwhile voids it.
  `ArenaSession.HostMatchResolved` (winner, or -1 for void) is what `HubBetBoard` listens to: it records every fighter's
  fight / win in `HubStore` and pays the pool out pari-mutuel (winners split all stakes by stake; void or nobody on the
  winner = refund all). One side per bettor per match, fighters cannot bet on their own match. The board publishes
  `BoardBlob` (current match teams / pools / fighter records, queue, bets) once a second.
- **Stratagem Hero** (`StratagemGame`, local to the player; codes in `data/stratagems.json`, 12 approved by Mark
  2026-11-10, each acting out its name): rounds of 4 + round codes (max 12), clock 10 s refilled each round, +1 s per
  code (max 12), wrong arrow restarts the code; 5 pts per arrow, round bonus 50 + 25·round, time bonus 10 / s left,
  perfect +100. The host takes the final score through `RpcHostSubmitStratagem`, rejects impossible runs (> 2.5 codes / s
  or more than `MaxScoreFor(codes)`), keeps the best per Steam ID and publishes `ScoresBlob` — players present in the
  session only, rebuilt every 3 s. WASD and the arrow keys (`ArrowUp/Down/Left/Right` input actions) both work.
- **Big screen + cameraman** (`HubArenaScreen` on the bar, Mark 2026-11-10): one feed camera, a 480 px render target
  shown by `hub_screen.shader`, rendering only while the local view is within 60 m of the screen. The host cycles
  `AngleIndex` through eight fixed `Angles` (south high, north / west / east stands, a low corner, each fort, overhead)
  every 10 s. A player can volunteer at the arena kiosk ("Be the Cameraman", first come, one per fight —
  `ArenaSession.CameramanId`): when the fight starts they go in at `CameramanSpawn`, with `PlayerVitals.EventInvulnerable`,
  `PlayerCombat.EventNoCombat` (no melee, bow, bola or shove) and `PlayerAnimation.EventOutfit = "referee"` (white polo +
  `hub_referee_stripes` + black trackies, replacing the worn clothing), and every other shot is their own eye view
  (index -1). The `Commend` kiosk by the screen: any one press (Mark) locks the screen on the cameraman until the fight
  ends; its prompt follows the state. Fight over → cameraman restored to the lobby, flags and outfit off, role free.
- **Grapple loan**: the trial cube needs a grapple, so `TimeTrialSession.LendGrapple` (on in the hub) sets
  `PlayerMovement.EventGrappleLoan` for racers from countdown to reset; with an empty Grapple slot the pawn grapples with
  `LoanGrappleResourceId` (`basic_hook`). Nothing enters the inventory. The trial menu lists only races whose rings exist
  in the scene (`TimeTrialCheckpoint.HasRoute`); the hub race is `hub_grapple_bars` (orders 10-15).
- **Store** (`HubStore`, the company-database stand-in): `FileSystem.Data/2Tgames/hub/players/<steamid>.json` — tokens
  (100 free per UTC day on hub entry), arena fights / wins, stratagem best; cached, written through. Synced to the pawn
  as `PlayerInventoryInteraction.HubTokens` / `HubStratagemBest`.
- Console: `hub` (go there as if through a portal), `hub_tokens [n]`, `stratagem_reload`.

## Commandment #2 — End every assistant reply with “what you need to do”

When the assistant sends a **final** message in this repo, it must end with a short **“What’s needed from you”** section: concrete follow-ups (prefab/scene steps, testing to run, settings to verify, decisions only you can make). If there is genuinely nothing, say **Nothing required on your side for this change.**

Enforced for the assistant via **`CLAUDE.md`** (footer order: build label line, then this section).

## Commandment #3 — Deprecate cleanly

When you **replace or remove** behavior, **delete the old path in the same change**: no orphaned facades, dead toggles, or duplicate post-process hooks. Settings that no longer affect output come out of the editor; keys needed only for old JSON live as plain fields in `TerrainPreviewSettings.Legacy.cs` (no `[Property]`).

Enforced via **`CLAUDE.md`**. Subsystem docs (e.g. **`Code/World/TerrainPreview/docs/TERRAIN_PREVIEW.md`**) must match the code you ship.

## Commandment #4 — No Ensure* bandage components

When a prefab or scene object is missing what it needs, **fix the asset**. Do **not** add a runtime helper (`EnsureChopableTrees`, `AutoWire*`, folder bootstraps that `Components.Create` on children, etc.) to paper over incomplete setup.

- Prefer clarifying requirements and s&box prefab/instance behavior over guessing.
- Author `ChopableTree` + `DamageReceiver` (and similar) on the tree prefab or scene instance.
- Repair bad prefab instance patches (`RemovedObjects`) instead of compensating in code.

Enforced via **`CLAUDE.md`**.

## Commandment #5 — Meters are the design language; convert once, at the read site

**Designer-facing values are written in meters.** A property named `…Meters` holds exactly the number the user said: 100 m → **100**. Never pre-scale that number in the property, in JSON, or in a scene transform you type by hand.

**The world is not 1:1.** s&box inherits Source units, and this project has two conversion factors:

| Space | Factor | Converter |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Terrain, environment, sky, fly-cam, harvest, containers, grapple | **40 u/m** | `TerrainWorldUnits.MetersToEngine` |
| Pawn distances (shove dash, bow range, wingsuit) | **≈40 u/m** | `BodyHeight / 1.8` — Citizen `BodyHeight` 72 ≈ 1.8 m |
| Build pieces — snap math, piece colliders, camp layouts | **40 u/m** | `BuildModuleDimensions.UnitsPerMeter` (kit vmdls import at 0.4) |

Convert **once**, inside the system that owns the value, and never carry a converted number across a system boundary. A value that has already been converted (a mesh AABB, a `BuildPieceModelCache` extent) is in final world units — do not scale it again.

| User says | Property value | What the read site produces |
|-----------|----------------|------------------------------|
| 1500 m star shell | `ShellRadiusMeters` = **1500** | 60,000 units via `MetersToEngine` |
| 1 m shove dash | `ShoveDashMeters` = **1** | ≈40 units via `BodyHeight / 1.8` |
| 2 m wall module | `ModuleMeters` = **2** | 80 units via `BuildModuleDimensions.UnitsPerMeter` |

**Build kit scale (2026-11-08, Mark: "same scale as the Valheim ones", "3–5 times thicker"):** the kit's 50 u/m was retired. Every kit vmdl imports at 0.4, `BuildColliderSnap.PrefabColliderSize` is gone, and a 2 m wall is 80 units — just over the 72-unit pawn. Plates (`ThinMeters`) and beams (`BeamMeters`) are both **0.25 m** (were 0.06 / 0.2); the doorway is 1.9 m (a 1.8 m hole is exactly the pawn's hull); stairs climb in 4 × 0.25 m steps with 0.08 m open treads. Thick plates made the mid-plane snap bury a wall 0.125 m into its deck and hang a ceiling 0.125 m under its wall top, which cost the pawn his headroom in a one-storey room — so `BuildSnapPlacement.ApplyFloorFaceOffset` moves the placing piece away from the other piece by the floor's half thickness on every seam that has a floor on exactly one side (wall / post / flight stands ON the deck, deck / roof sits ON the wall top, build-downward mates move the other way). Floor-beside-floor and wall-on-wall are untouched. Storey pitch is therefore 2.25 m floor-top to floor-top; two stair pieces (2 m) reach the upper deck's underside and the last 0.25 m is a step. Camp layouts in `enemy_camps.json` are unchanged numbers in meters and simply shrank with the pieces. The crafting-station models (`Tools/item_models/itemgen/items_stations.py`, same day, other session) still import at 0.5 and need 0.4 to sit in their table-frame colliders.

**Kit look: slats and beams (2026-11-16, Mark: "wood slats and a horizontal beam that holds them together. Think valheim style. 128 texels per meter and low poly"):** `Blender/scripts/create_build_kit.py` builds every piece from boards — walls / gables / door: vertical slats (varied widths, ragged tops on full walls) on two chamfered cross beams on one face; door: posts + lintel round the exact 1.0 × 1.9 m opening; leaf: slats + battens + Z brace; floors: planks on joists; roof, hip and valley: **thatch** (Mark 2026-11-16: "will help them seem less like a wood box") — four stepped courses of 0.4 m straw bundles (ragged butts, each bundle top twisted a little) on two purlins at the quarter points, own `build_roof_thatch` material (256 px straw, straws UV'd down each face's slope); the hip / valley are the panel's own courses mitred at the fold (`roof_corner_parts`, a convex plane clip), with the slab **mid-plane** on the module cube corners exactly like the straight panel, so corners meet panels flush; the eave course runs `THATCH_EAVE_OVERHANG` (0.35 m) past the panel edge with its underside climbing to the butt, so a roof on walls overhangs them instead of stopping mid-wall (Mark 2026-11-16: corner pieces looked "sunk down" on the hut) and on stacked panels it rides inside the course below with no shared faces — the old folded shell had its top surface there and sat 0.18 m under its neighbours; stairs: open plank treads between stringers; winders: a quarter of a spiral — four two-board treads with bearers fanned round a newel in the inside corner, cut on a 2 m arc so both faces stay 2 m wide and the far corner is open (the old wedges ran into that corner as long spikes); every winder step is closed by a 4 cm riser board on its front radial line and a curved stringer climbs the outer arc under the tread ends (Mark 2026-11-16: no "modern open concept" stairs); **support beams sit on one lattice** (Mark 2026-11-16: "the support beams on the back of the pieces are all misaligned"): wall cross beams 0.5 m above the piece bottom and every metre after (2 m wall 0.5 / 1.5, 1 m wall 0.5, so stacked half walls, door panels and gables carry the same lines), floor joists 0.5 m in from the edge and every metre; beams / posts: chamfered timber. Hip / valley corners are still the folded shell, only textured. Two materials, `materials/building/build_wood_{plank,beam}` (pixel_lit, 256 px = 2 m = 128 texels / m, eight 0.25 m board bands; each board is UV'd along its grain onto a random band). `split_build_kit.py` writes the textures, one FBX per piece holding `<id>` and `<id>_lod1` (the old one-slab shape, LOD switch 400 = 100 m) and the vmdl (import 0.4, physics = PhysicsMeshFile of LOD0 only). Every mesh still fills its size-table box exactly (the script reports mismatches), so colliders and snaps are unchanged. Kit `fallbackColor`s are white — the tint multiplies the texture. **Stairs meet a deck at its walking face either way (same day, Mark: "nor could I add a stairs that snapped to the top of the floors 4 points, it only snapped under it"):** `ApplyFloorFaceOffset` decided above / below by comparing centres, and a flight leading up onto a deck has its centre half a storey under it, so its top tread was pushed down to the deck's underside. A flight is now always the upper side of a stairs ↔ floor seam: its foot stands on the deck, or its top tread lands flush with it.

**Blueprints stay ghosts until someone builds them (2026-11-09, Mark: "objects should stay how they appear before we build them"):** `BuildPiece.IsBlueprint` is `[Sync, Change]`; a placed blueprint keeps the hammer's blue ghost tint (`RestingColor` returns `BlueprintTint` while unbuilt, so support-hover and damage tints restore to it), and the change callback swaps every client's copy to the catalog colour the moment the host flips it. Finishing a blueprint is `BuildAuthority.HostFinishBlueprint` — the shared tail of the hammer's repair click (`TryRepairBuildPiece`, costs a durability tick) and the new E prompt (`TryBuildBlueprint`, no hammer needed, any player; credits quests / playtest like a direct build). The prompt lives in `PlayerInventoryInteraction.Blueprint.cs`: it traces the hammer's view ray (so a wall two storeys up is what you point at), shows "E — Build <piece>" when the piece is unbuilt and within **Build Reach (m)** = 6 m (twice the 3 m of the other E prompts) measured from the eye to the nearest point of the piece, so tall pieces can be finished from the ground; the host re-checks the same reach + 1 m slack. It ticks first and the door / campfire / trap / lever / bed prompts stand down for it (a blueprint door frame only offers Build). Verified in testscene1: prompt at 2 and 5 m, none at 6.5 m, upper wall of a two-high stack focused from 3 m back, host refuses at 12 m and builds at 3 m, built pieces get no prompt. **Walk-through and cost (same day, Mark: "players should be able to walk through and clip through blueprint pieces. Blueprint pieces are free, it's the E to build that checks inventory"):** an unbuilt piece carries the `blueprint` tag (`BuildPiece.BlueprintTag`, applied with the flag on host and clients, grapple tag only once built); `ProjectSettings/Collision.config` pairs `player`/`blueprint` and `enemy`/`blueprint` as Ignore, so pawns and scavs walk through while traces still hit it (hammer snaps and the E prompt keep working), and `EnsureBuildTraversalSettings` adds the tag to the nav mesh's ExcludedBodies with no rebake at blueprint placement (the bake comes when it is built). Placing a blueprint is free; `HostFinishBlueprint` charges the catalog `costs` through `PlayerInventory.HostTryConsumeResources` (all or nothing, bag + hotbar) unless `BuildSettings.FreeBuild`, and the prompt reads "Build Chest  (needs 8 Wood)" from `BuildAuthority.DescribeMissingMaterials` until the bag covers it. The wood kit pieces have empty `costs` in `build_pieces.json`, so today only furniture / stations charge anything. Verified: hull and rigidbody pass through a blueprint wall and stop at a built one; chest refused on an empty bag, built after 10 wood with 8 spent, blueprint placement left the count alone.

**Held corner follows the role, not the slot (2026-11-09, Mark: "hit Q until Snap bottom left (4/4), let it snap, then scroll — rotation is wrong"):** a seam's Q/E list is sorted flush-fit corner first (cycle 0, "Auto"), then the other corners in hold order, and the flush corner changes with yaw — so a bare slot index named a different corner after a half turn and the ghost jumped to pivot on the far end of the seam mid-scroll. `ToolBuildHammer` now captures the placing snap behind a Q/E choice (`_heldAnchorSnapIndex`, taken from the first snapped pick after the press or after selecting a piece; Auto stays -1) and `BuildSnapCandidateGrouper.TryPickCandidate` picks that role in whatever slot it sits, falling back to the index only when the group has no member on it; a Q/E press drops the remembered corner for that frame (otherwise it outranks the new index and the press does nothing — the 2026-11-09 regression Mark hit) and re-captures it from the new slot; the hammer then syncs its index to the slot actually shown, so the HUD may read "Auto 1/4" for the one yaw where the held corner is also the flush corner and go back to "4/4" after. Verified with a 45° sweep on wall / 1 m wall / floor / stairs held by every corner: zero anchor changes, constant pivot radius.

Enforced via **`CLAUDE.md`**.

## Sword animation assets (one-handed, right hand)

- **Model + graph:** the pawn body is `Assets/models/player/human_male_sword.vmdl` — a clone of the engine's `citizen_human_male.vmdl` (mesh, skeleton and clothing prefabs referenced from the mounted citizen addon) plus a `99_Sword` animation folder and its own graph `animgraphs/human_sword.vanmgrph`, the engine `human.vanmgrph` with the holdtypes → melee subgraph chain cloned. The melee pose node is a `CSelectorAnimNode` on int **`sword_pose`** (0 cocked L, 1 rest L, 2 rest R, 3 cocked R, 4 cocked overhead, 5 swap mid, 6 rest up), the attack node one on **`sword_attack`** (0 R, 1 L, 2 overhead). The guard is a bool selector on `b_sword_guard` over that pose selector; its "true" child is an int selector on **`sword_guard_dir`** (= the guard's `SwingDirs`: 0 `Sword_Block_L` blade standing up on the left, 1 `Sword_Block_R` on the right, 2 `Sword_Block_Up` level in front of the head) cross-fading in `GUARD_DIR_BLEND_SECONDS` **0.15** (linear; 0.25 until 2026-11-16, Mark: "Blocks still take super long to go left right or up") while the held guard moves (Mark 2026-11-14: "new animations for blocking left blocking right and kinda blend between them"; 2026-11-16: "the blocks still snap poses", then "make block and attack .25s" - the guard's cover still switches after `BlockSwitchSeconds` 0.15); `PlayerAnimation` sets it from `PlayerCombat.GetActiveBlockDirection()` (owner: live guard, remotes: the host's broadcast direction). The three guard clips sat on node ids 1860012300-302 until 2026-11-14 - the same ids as the stance selector and the guard-direction selector - so every guard showed one pose (Mark: "Block directions don't actually change the block animation"); they are on +400 now and `make_human_sword_assets.py` refuses to write a graph with duplicate node ids. anim_test's guard cases raise the guard with the owner-side `PlayerCombat.TestHoldGuard` (the host intake it used is ignored for the pawn a machine drives, so no guard ever rose) and check `each guard direction has its own pose` (sword hand left / right / up). `Tools/make_human_sword_assets.py` regenerates all four files — never hand-edit them; after regenerating, recompile and **restart play** (a graph does not hot-swap on a live pawn). **Crouch:** the Duck blend's crouch inputs go through a `bone_mask_node` (`CROUCH_MASK_ID`, weight list `Only_UpperBody`) — legs from the stock crouch, upper body from the standing sword chain — so every stance, swap and strike also plays crouched; without it the stock 2H crouch pose replaced the sword stances. The attack state machine's `OnAlmostFinished` exit blends in `ATTACK_EXIT_BLEND_SECONDS` (0.08 s; stock 0.3 s covered the whole strike and snapped the chest).
- **Idle carry (`PlayerAnimation.MeleeSwing.cs`):** one rest pose, **the weapon by the waist** (`Sword_Rest` = `REST_WAIST`: fist by the right hip, blade forward and a little down), whatever side the cursor aims (2026-11-17, Mark: "the players 'weapon equipped but idle' position has the weapon by their waist? When we change from left right overhead dont change the pose or position of the weapon, that only happens when we actually initiate an attack"; the shoulder stances `Sword_Rest_L / _R / _Up`, the stance swaps, `NetworkedSwordRestSide` / `RpcSubmitSwordRestSide` are deleted). A click plays the aimed side's wind-up clip, which now starts at the waist: `REST_WAIST` → `WIND_*` (0.30 s) → `COCKED_*` (0.60 s), its playhead the windup progress; a click out of a strike's follow-through or out of the guard starts the playhead on the wind frame (`WindUpStartOutOfStrike` 0.5 = `WIND_AT_S / WIND_END_S`), so a chain never dips back to the waist. Strikes settle to the waist. Under a strike the `sword_pose` selector keeps the strike's cocked pose until 70 % of the clip (`StrikeRestFromShare`): a guard raised out of the strike cross-fades from that hidden pose, and the waist dragged the hand through the blend (anim_test "block out of a strike" 24.7 m/s). `Sword_Swap_Mid` is left only for a **held heavy morphed across the face** (right / overhead ↔ left: up in front for `SwordSwapMidSeconds` 0.125 first, `MorphCockedSwing`); a straight right ↔ left cross-fade swept the blade through the head — never add one back. Scavs (`EntityCombat`) use the same `sword_pose` values (`PlayerAnimation.SwordPose`), and present their swings exactly like a pawn (2026-11-17, Mark: scavs "arent using the same animations ... I want them to have the exact same speeds as players"): the host syncs `EntityCombat.SwingWindupSeconds` with the cock, every peer draws `sword_wind` over it from the waist (from the wind frame out of a strike or the guard, `PlayerAnimation.WindFrameShare`), a new wind-up cuts a settling strike with `b_sword_interrupt`, and the strike clip runs `PlayerAnimation.StrikeClipLengthSeconds` (0.64 s; the scav used 0.40 s).
- **Look limits (`PlayerAnimation.LookAim.cs`):** the engine `PlayerController` writes the raw look direction into `aim_eyes` / `aim_head` / `aim_body`; with free-look orbit the body never turns, so looking behind wound the hips toward the camera and **flipped them across at 180°**. PlayerAnimation now owns the controller's animation pass — it turns `UseAnimatorControls` off once and calls `PlayerController.UpdateAnimation` itself (not while seated; the seat pose owns the graph then), then rewrites the three aim channels in the same frame with human ranges (chest 40°, head 85°, eyes 105°, pitch 25° / 60°), a soft knee near each limit, and a fade back to forward past `AimLookBehindFadeDegrees` (120°) so straight behind = forward and ±180 give the same pose. Overwriting after the controller without owning its pass did nothing — the controller ran later in the frame.
- **Clips:** `Assets/models/player/animations/human@sword_*.fbx`, authored by `Blender/scripts/create_sword_anims.py` on the engine's `citizen_human_male_REF.fbx` rig.
