Base stance from the retargeted right-hand item pose (transferred by armature matrices — the retargeted FBXs store their pose as the rest pose); **fingers from the punch pose** (85% curl) and `hold_R` moved into the fist tunnel (`make_fist`: `GRIP_ORIGIN` / `GRIP_AXIS` hand-local, blade = hold local +Z, edge = local +X toward the knuckles) — the item pose is an open carrying hand, which showed the sword floating beside an open palm. Poses are world-space ops (`twist`, `lean`, `rot`, `grip`, `left`); `grip` solves the arm from fist position + blade direction with the rig's grip angle inside forearm-twist / wrist / shoulder-roll budgets — the wrist is never posed free-hand. `hand_*_IK_target` bones follow the hands. Clips are **baked frame by frame** from per-bone slerps between key poses (strikes accelerate into contact, decelerate through the follow-through), so the exported motion is exactly the path the checker validated. **Left / right strikes follow the debug attack path** (Mark 2026-11-02: primarily left/right, the sword tip where the spheres are): `COCKED_R` / `COCKED_L` and every 60 fps frame of the 0.1 s active window (`PATH_R_00..06`, `PATH_L_00..06`) are not hand-placed but solved from `MeleeAttackPath`'s lateral formula, mirrored in the script's *debug attack path* block (`PATH_RANGE_U` 76 = reachLateralMeters 1.9, arc 150°, pivot height 54, tilt 25°, push 8 — change them with the JSON / prefab). Each frame puts the blade tip (`SWORD_TIP_M`, sword_reforged's 40 u from the butt on `hold_R`) on the line pivot → sphere: on the sphere where the arm can reach it inside `LIMITS` (with `PATH_MARGIN`), else as far out along that line as it can; the blade's turn off the line is searched, the torso twist / lean follow the arc (`path_body`; the left turn is capped at 20° for anim_test's 65° chest limit while looking aside). The clip starts when the host active window opens, so frame *n* = path progress *n*/6; then follow-through 0.16 s, settle 0.22 s, rest 0.30 s. `-- check` prints an `info path` line per frame (tip distance along the line, off-line cm, tip → sphere) and `-- path` also prints the lateral reach that would put the tip on every sphere. At reach 1.9 m the arm's natural reach (shoulder → tip ≈ 1.5 m) leaves the tip 0.47–0.79 m inside the spheres.
- **Anatomy checker + tuner (`-- check` / `-- tune`):** body proxies (head sphere, neck and torso capsules, pelvis sphere, blade segment guard → tip, arm segments) and limits (`LIMITS`: blade clear of head 4 cm / neck 3 cm / torso and pelvis 2 cm, forearm and free hand outside the body, elbow 35–178°, reach error, forearm twist 95°, wrist 50°, shoulder roll 95°). `-- check` scores every pose, every frame of every strike clip, every key-to-key segment and every graph blend the code can produce (`GRAPH_BLENDS`: rest ↔ swap mid, rest ↔ each cocked pose, cross-side presses, raised stance) and exits 1 on any failure. `-- tune [POSE…]` searches a small fist offset (≤ 10 cm) and blade tilt (≤ 20°) per pose that clears the limits with a safety margin in the pose AND along every transition touching it, staying close to the authored intent; results live in `Blender/scripts/sword_pose_tuning.json` (`SWORD_TUNE_ROUNDS` / `SWORD_TUNE_PASSES` env for longer runs). A pose the tuner pins at its bounds needs a design change, not more rounds.
- **In-engine test (`anim_test`, `Code/Player/Tests/AnimTest.cs`):** clones a `basicplayer` far above the scene onto its own floor box (without one the pawn free-fell ~25 s and its landing read as a pelvis snap in the raised look sweep; the suite now fails fast if the pawn is not grounded), equips the sword and drives the live path (controller animation pass, look limits, the idle carry, strikes) through 26 scenarios — the waist rest, aim changes that must leave the carry and the sword hand still (2026-11-17), a 360° look sweep, up/down while turned, every attack, heavy, aim-flip-then-instant-press, overhead after aiming left, a held wind-up morphed across the face, attacks while looking sideways, and crouched: aim changes keeping the carry, a look sweep, every attack, crouch/stand while aiming left (pelvis / head caps widen for 0.5 s after the crouch input flips: that travel is the engine's stock Duck spring, measured 7.9 m/s) — asserts the graph's `sword_pose` matches the aim, and measures the real skinned bones every frame with the same limits plus: sword fixed in the fist (≤ 1 cm / 2°), chest ≤ 65° and head ≤ 110° from the hips, no snaps (hand 22 m/s, pelvis 1.5 m/s / 400°/s, chest 1200°/s, head 4.5 m/s / 1200°/s — the 0.30 s strike is fast). `PlayerCombat` stays enabled on the clone with `InputSuppressedForTests` (a disabled combat component returns no stance, so swaps were silently untested); the clone is tagged `test_pawn` so `PlayerSpawnSeparation` does not cull it. Gate: `.claude/hooks/anim_test_gate.py` makes both checks owed whenever an animation file changes.
- **Strike length and cadence:** the strike clips are 0.30 s (`MeleeStrikeClipSeconds`, written in `basicplayer.prefab` — the network spawner clones the prefab, not the scene instance, so a scene override never reaches the pawn). Soft recovery = rest of the strike clip + the class `RecoveryMiss/HitSeconds`; 2026-11-01 the player classes' recoveries were halved — a light one-handed tap is ready again 0.57 s after the press (was 0.84 s), 0.34 s after the active window (was 0.74 s).
- **Held sword:** `MeleeOneHandedGrip` (PlayerAnimation, default on) is **rigid on `hold_R`** (`TickOneHandedSwordTransform`): blade along the bone's up axis, edge along `HoldEdgeLocal` (local +X), the handle point `MeleeDemoStickGripAlongLength` up from the butt at the bone. `holdtype_handedness` is RH. The UI preview (`AugmentPlayerPreviewPanel`) uses the same model.
- **Wingsuit glide (`PlayerAnimation.Wingsuit.cs`, Mark 2026-11-05: "laying flat with their arms out like they're a bird"):** while the synced `PlayerMovement.IsWingsuitDeployed` is true every peer plays the looping `Wingsuit_Glide` clip (`WINGSUIT_A` ↔ `WINGSUIT_B` in `create_sword_anims.py`: arms spread wide a little behind the body, chest arched, head tipped back, legs apart, feet pointed, a slow wing flex) as a `UseAnimGraph=false` sequence like the dodge roll (registered as a plain looping sequence via `SEQUENCE_CLIPS` in `make_human_sword_assets.py`, not in the graph), and the **body renderer** is pitched 90° about the hips (`WingsuitLayPivotMeters` 0.95) so the head points along the glide and the belly at the ground; the root, collider and camera are untouched (the movement owner already pitches / rolls the root along the glide). The hit-reaction flail stands the body back up. `wingsuit_test [meters]` lifts the local pawn and opens the suit (no gear or mid-air jump; the deploy waits 0.2 s because the controller keeps its old ground contact for a tick after the lift and the first glide tick would stow).
- **Filming (`Tools/sbox_film.py <shot.json>`):** one MCP connection to the open editor, C# setup steps, slowed `Scene.TimeScale`, camera screenshot bursts → `frame_NNN.png`, labelled `sheet.png`, `film.gif`. Steps: `stop` / `play` / `console` / `write` / `csharp` / `wait` / `film`. Judge animation from these, never from Blender stills alone.
